# OpenTAP Architecture Brainstorm 02 (unified scope): full record

Produced 2026-10-12 by a workflow of four specialist personas (round 1), cross-examination (round 2), independent fact-checks of the personas' load-bearing claims, revised positions (round 3), and three critics (completeness, ONTAP veteran, homelab operator). The lead architect's synthesis, trait contracts and Phase 1 roadmap are in 03-architecture-brainstorm-02.md. Headings inside each persona's text are demoted two levels.

## Round 1: brainstorms

### Opus-Fabrics

#### Executive take
I would build one `MirrorLink` trait with three honest capability bits (`reject_advisory`, `unknown_fate_bound`, `header_granularity`) and three thin implementations, TCP (pinned busy-poll thread), RDMA (sideway `BasicQueuePair`, legacy `ibv_post_send`, one code path for RoCEv2 and IB) and FC (the local qla2xxx initiator role writing O_DIRECT from the ring into the partner's LIO fileio LUN that is a tmpfs file the partner daemon mmaps), all landing in a single tmpfs-backed inbound ring per node. The FC leg keeps the same ack contract (Placed = SCSI GOOD = bytes copied into partner tmpfs pages); what differs is write granularity and how long an unknown-fate slot stays unknown, and both belong in `Caps`, not in a second trait. Baseline is Debian 13 trixie: I verified from Debian's own config that bookworm's 6.1 kernel never enabled CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK, which alone disqualifies plain bookworm for the D-Blade. The single biggest risk is Linux-to-Linux dual-mode N2N FC, which nobody has published; the mitigation is a week-1 spike with a used 8G switch as the fallback topology. The thing I most disagree with is making FC a first-class Phase 1 mirror tier: it is the slowest leg (50-100 us per put), the one where initiator-side error recovery can ISP-abort a port shared with client targets, and the one needing the most kernel plumbing; it should ship in Phase 2 behind RDMA and TCP. Second disagreement: the digest's shared 4 KiB descriptor blocks of 64 B headers assume byte-granular remote writes, which a SCSI LUN cannot do; descriptor blocks must become link-private and whole-block-rewritten.

#### Proposed libraries and subsystems
##### Debian packages (trixie version / bookworm version; all verified on packages.debian.org or sources.debian.org unless marked)
- `linux-image-amd64` 6.12.107-1 / 6.1.176-1; `bookworm-backports` 6.12.95-1~bpo12+1 (same 6.12 config as trixie, lags trixie point releases).
- `firmware-qlogic` 20250410-2 / 20230210-5, section non-free-firmware. Ships ql2100..ql2500_fw.bin only; ql2500_fw.bin is 8.08.207 (linux-firmware WHENCE). No ql2600/ql2700 blob, so 16G cards (ISP2031 QLE2672, ISP27xx QLE2692) run whatever is in flash; 8G ISP25xx loads the Debian blob first (qla_init.c "FW Load priority: 1) request-firmware 2) flash" for 24xx/25xx; reversed for 81xx/27xx).
- `targetcli-fb` 1:2.1.53 (both), `python3-rtslib-fb` 2.1.76-3 / 2.1.75-2.1. Debian's only patch moves saveconfig to `/etc/rtslib-fb-target/saveconfig.json`; the qla2xxx fabric is unpatched and exposed. Our daemon should drive configfs directly (`/sys/kernel/config/target/qla2xxx/<wwpn>/tpgt_1/...`) and use targetcli only for the spike.
- `rdma-core` 56.1-1 / 44.0-2 (libibverbs1, librdmacm1, ibverbs-utils, rdma-core providers incl. mlx4/mlx5/rxe), `infiniband-diags`, `opensm` 3.3.23 (both). `multipath-tools`, `ethtool`, `nvme-cli`, `sg3-utils`.
##### Kernel modules (trixie 6.12 config, verified =m unless noted)
- Target: `target_core_mod`, `target_core_file`, `target_core_user` (TCM_USER2), `tcm_qla2xxx`, `tcm_loop` (LOOPBACK_TARGET, lets the FC receive path be tested with zero FC hardware), `tcm_fc`, `iscsi_target_mod`, `ib_srpt`, `ib_isert`.
- nvmet: TARGET, FC, TCP, RDMA, FCLOOP, LOOP (FCLOOP/LOOP are *not set* in bookworm 6.1), TCP_TLS and AUTH (=y, trixie only).
- Block/FS: `ublk_drv` (trixie only; absent from the 6.1 config), `md_mod`/`raid456`/`md-cluster`, `dm-multipath`, `ksmbd` (SMB_SERVER), `nfsd` with V4/BLOCKLAYOUT/SCSILAYOUT (SCSILAYOUT trixie only), TMPFS=y, HUGETLBFS=y, TRANSPARENT_HUGEPAGE=y.
- RDMA: `ib_core`, `ib_uverbs`, `rdma_ucm`, `mlx4_core/mlx4_ib/mlx4_en` (still built in 6.12), `mlx5_core/mlx5_ib`, `ib_ipoib` (+CM), `rdma_rxe` (soft-RoCE for CI and VMs), `irdma`, `bnxt_re`, `qedr`.
- Watchdog: WATCHDOG=y, `softdog`, `iTCO_wdt`, `sp5100_tco`, `ipmi_watchdog`, `i6300esb` (VM witness). HZ_250, PREEMPT_VOLUNTARY, NO_HZ_FULL=y, CPU_ISOLATION=y. CONFIG_IO_URING is not listed in either config, i.e. Kconfig default y; Debian ships no `kernel.io_uring_disabled` sysctl that I know of (verify on a live box).
##### Rust crates (honest maturity)
- `sideway` 0.4.3 (2026-06, RDMA-Rust, on `rdma-mummy-sys` 0.2.4): the pick. Has `BasicQueuePair` (`ibv_create_qp` + `ibv_post_send`) and `ExtendedQueuePair` (`ibv_wr_*`), plus an `rdmacm` module. Young API, small team; wrap it behind our own `verbs-lite` facade.
- `ibverbs` 0.9.2 (jonhoo, 2025-03): mature but drives QPs exclusively through `ibv_create_qp_ex` + `ibv_wr_*`; rdma-core's mlx4 provider does not include `IBV_QP_INIT_ATTR_SEND_OPS_FLAGS` in `MLX4_CREATE_QP_SUP_COMP_MASK`, so it cannot create a QP on ConnectX-3. Disqualified while CX-3 is a target.
- `rdma-sys` 0.3.0 (datenlord, 2023-02): stale raw bindings; fallback only. `rdma-core` crate: nightly, experimental, dead.
- `io-uring` 0.7 (tokio-rs, low-level, maintained), `rustix` (sockets, mmap, mlock), `memmap2`, `crc32c` (SSE4.2), `rustls`+`tokio` for ControlLink, `openraft` 0.9.25 per Consensus.

#### Proposed architecture
##### Components
- **Local ring** (sender side): a hugetlbfs or tmpfs file, one per node, registered once with io_uring (`IORING_REGISTER_BUFFERS`) and once per RDMA device (`ibv_reg_mr`, 1 GiB chunks to stay under mlx4 MTT limits). The D-Blade's dirty index points into it; the CP DMAs from it to disk. It is the local NVLOG.
- **Inbound ring** (receiver side): a tmpfs file (`mount -t tmpfs -o huge=always,noswap` on 6.4+, mlock'd by a tiny ring-holder unit so it outlives daemon crashes). tmpfs, not hugetlbfs, because LIO fileio writes through `vfs_iter_write` and hugetlbfs has no `write_iter` (verify). The same file is the FC LUN backing store, the RDMA MR and the TCP `readv` target. Three writers, one address space, zero conflicts because slot ownership is sender-side.
- **Layout change to digest item 9**: 64 B headers stay, but descriptor blocks become *link-private* (each link owns whole 4 KiB blocks of 64 slots) and are always rewritten whole by the owning link. Reason: a SCSI LUN cannot write 64 B; a 4 KiB descriptor write from FC would clobber 63 neighbours written by the TCP leg. Payload stays 4 KiB-aligned so the CP never memcpys.
- **Put = two writes issued concurrently** (payload region, then descriptor block). Placed is reported only when both complete. The receiver and takeover replay accept a record only if header CRC *and* payload CRC validate, so a header that lands before its payload (FC commands complete out of order) is simply "not yet placed". This keeps FC at two fabric round trips per put instead of four.
- **ControlLink**: TCP + mTLS (rustls) on Ethernet only; carries 100 ms heartbeats, leases, epochs, Raft, and the receiver's BUSY/stop-sending advisory. FC cannot carry it (no IPFC in Linux), so Ethernet is always present and FC is never the sole link.
##### Trait sketch
```rust
pub type Slot = u32;                      // index into the partner's inbound ring
pub struct RingRef { pub desc_block: u32, pub payload_off: u64, pub len: u32 } // all inside the local ring
#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
pub struct Caps {
    pub max_record: u32,              // FC: <= 8 MiB (FD_MAX_BYTES); RDMA: MR bound; TCP: unbounded
    pub max_inflight: u32,            // unacked puts the link accepts
    pub header_granularity: u32,      // 64 (TCP/RDMA) or 4096 (FC): whole descriptor-block rewrite
    pub reject_advisory: bool,        // only TCP can NAK before placement; FC/RDMA use ControlLink
    pub unknown_fate_bound: Duration, // TCP/RDMA ~0 after close/QP->ERR; FC = R_A_TOV (10 s) unless ABTS confirmed
    pub placed_is_hw_ack: bool,       // FC (SCSI GOOD) and RDMA (RC ACK) need no peer thread
}
pub enum Ack { Placed, Rejected, Unknown }   // Unknown => slot quarantined until unknown_fate_bound
pub struct PutToken(pub u64);
pub enum LinkHealth { Up { rtt_ewma: Duration }, Degraded, Dead { since: Instant } }
pub trait MirrorLink: Send {
    fn caps(&self) -> Caps;
    /// Non-blocking. Err(Full) at max_inflight. Never retries, never reorders on our behalf.
    fn put(&mut self, slot: Slot, rec: RingRef) -> Result<PutToken, PutError>;
    fn heartbeat(&mut self, hb: &HeartbeatRec) -> Result<PutToken, PutError>;   // every 20 ms, dedicated slot
    /// Drain completions; order unspecified; each token resolves exactly once.
    fn poll(&mut self, out: &mut Vec<(PutToken, Ack)>) -> usize;
    fn health(&self) -> LinkHealth;
    /// After Ok, no late write from this link can land (socket closed / QP in ERR / LUN unmapped + ABTS or R_A_TOV).
    fn fence_and_close(&mut self) -> Result<(), LinkError>;
}
pub trait MirrorInbound {                 // receiver side, per node not per link
    fn mapping(&self) -> &RingMapping;    // the mmapped tmpfs file
    /// Index new CRC-valid records (FC/RDMA: passive scan; TCP: also drives readv + acks).
    fn scan(&mut self, cur: &mut ScanCursor, out: &mut Vec<RecordLoc>) -> usize;
    fn quiesce(&mut self) -> Result<(), LinkError>;
}
pub trait ControlLink: Send { fn send(&mut self, m: CtlMsg) -> Result<(), CtlError>; fn recv(&mut self, out: &mut Vec<CtlMsg>) -> usize; fn peer_alive_since(&self) -> Instant; }
```
Must never leak: WWPN/LUN/NQN, rkeys/lkeys/QPNs, fds, SCSI tags or FC exchange IDs, sense data, delivery order, MTU, the transport's own retry policy or timeouts. `Rejected` is the only receiver-originated signal and is advisory.
##### One trait for four fabrics?
Yes, for the put/ack half, because all three mechanisms reduce to "bytes landed in partner DRAM, confirmed by a hardware or peer ack". The honest differences are the three `Caps` fields above. The FC leg does *not* need a different ack contract: SCSI GOOD from fileio means the kernel memcpy into the tmpfs page finished, which is strictly stronger than TCP's peer ack. What FC cannot do is reject early or resolve unknown fate quickly, and that is capability data, not a new trait.
##### Copy counts (sender / receiver)
- **TCP**: 1 / 1 on 6.12. Sender `sendmsg` copies ring pages into skbs; `SEND_ZC` (6.0+) removes it but only pays above ~16 KiB (MSG_ZEROCOPY guidance ~10 KB), so use it per record size. Receiver `readv` into the ring is one kernel-to-user copy; io_uring multishot recv with provided buffer rings (5.19/6.0) removes syscalls, not the copy; `IORING_OP_RECV_ZC` is 6.15 and needs NIC header split, so not on trixie. Thread per link pinned to an isolated core, `SO_BUSY_POLL=50`, `SO_PREFER_BUSY_POLL`, `TCP_NODELAY`, `tcp_autocorking=0`, MTU 9000, `ethtool -C rx-usecs 0 adaptive-rx off`, IRQ affinity to the polling core. io_uring NAPI (6.9) is optional polish, not a dependency. 6.1 lacks NAPI registration, incremental buffer rings (6.12) and bundles (6.10); none are required.
- **RDMA**: 0 / 0. `rdma_cm` resolves via IPoIB address on IB or the NIC IP on RoCEv2; one RC QP per link; payload and descriptor block as two RDMA WRITE WRs, second one signaled; Placed = sender CQE (responder HCA ACK). No receiver CQE needed on the hot path; the heartbeat uses WRITE-with-immediate so a sleeping scanner wakes (one RQ WQE per 20 ms). IB back-to-back still needs opensm on a node (run on both, `priority` differing); SX6036 with MLNX-OS has an embedded SM, IS5022 does not. IB MTU 4096, FDR on CX-3 (PCIe 3 x8 caps ~6 GB/s), EDR on CX-4/5. RoCEv2 direct-attach needs no PFC/ECN; through a lossy switch RC retransmission survives but go-back-N on CX-3 collapses throughput, so direct-attach is the recommendation.
- **FC**: 0 / 1 (kernel). Sender: `IORING_OP_WRITE_FIXED` O_DIRECT to `/dev/sdX` at slot offsets; block layer pins ring pages, HBA DMAs from them. Receiver: HBA DMAs into target-core sgl pages, fileio `vfs_iter_write` copies once into tmpfs pages, daemon reads in place. TCMU is the same copy count plus a userspace handler on the data path, and rd_mcp pages cannot be mmapped, so fileio wins. LIO opens O_RDWR|O_DSYNC by default; `fd_buffered_io=1` (targetcli `write_back=true`) clears O_DSYNC and forces `emulate_write_cache=1`; FD_MAX_BYTES caps one command at 8 MiB; set `block_size=4096`. Both nodes load `qla2xxx qlini_mode=dual ql2xmqsupport=1`, export the mirror LUN to the partner WWPN via an ACL, and log into the partner's LUN as initiator on the same port. Dual-port cards: port 1 mirror, port 2 clients; sharing one port works but puts the mirror behind client BB-credit contention and inside the same error-handling domain.
##### Link health, fallback
Ring heartbeat 20 ms per link (link health only); link dead at 1 s triggers redelivery only. FC detection adds `port_state` and rport state polled every 20 ms from `/sys/class/fc_host` and `/sys/class/fc_remote_ports`, plus command timeouts: udev sets the mirror sd `timeout=2`, rport `fast_io_fail_tmo=1`, `dev_loss_tmo=5`, so a wedged partner fails puts in ~2 s instead of the 30 s defaults. On flip: outstanding tokens resolve Unknown, their descriptor blocks are quarantined for `unknown_fate_bound`, records are re-put on the secondary into fresh slots; identity is (aggregate, epoch, seq) so replay's maximal-contiguous-prefix dedupe yields no gaps or duplicates. Active/standby only, never striping; different aggregates may pin different primaries. Fail-back after 30 s of stable health and a probe put, manual for FC by default (dying SFPs flap). Mixed transports per aggregate are allowed because the record format is transport-blind.
##### Debian baseline
Trixie. bookworm+backports is "same kernel, older userspace" (rdma-core 44, nvme-cli 2.3, no tmpfs `noswap` is a kernel matter so fine) and costs one extra qualification lane plus a kernel that lags by weeks. Plain bookworm 6.1: no ublk, no nvmet FCLOOP/LOOP, no tmpfs noswap, no io_uring NAPI; unsupported.

#### Bottlenecks
##### Per-put latency to Placed and bandwidth (my estimates; round-1 TCP/RoCE/FC figures plus serialization arithmetic; measure in the spikes)
| Transport | 4 KiB put | 64 KiB put | Payload BW | Outstanding to saturate (64 KiB / 4 KiB) | Cores |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 8GFC QLE2562 (ISP2532) | 50-100 us (2 fabric RTT + 2 firmware traversals + fileio copy) | 130-180 us (+82 us serialization at 800 MB/s) | ~0.8 GB/s | 2-4 / 15, but ISP25xx IOPS ceiling (~100-150k mixed) caps 4 KiB puts at ~0.4-0.6 GB/s | sender: io_uring on D-Blade thread; receiver: ~1 core of kernel LIO work at line rate |
| 16GFC QLE2692 (ISP2271) | 40-80 us | 80-130 us (+41 us at 1.6 GB/s) | ~1.6 GB/s | 4-8 / 40 | same |
| 10GbE TCP busy-poll | 25-40 us | 80-100 us (+55 us) | ~1.15 GB/s | 1-2 / 12 (BDP 1.15 GB/s x 40 us = 46 KB) | 1 pinned core each side |
| 25GbE TCP | 20-30 us | 40-60 us | ~2.9 GB/s | 2 / 20; single stream is copy-bound near 3 GB/s/core, use 2 links | 2 cores each side |
| 40/100GbE TCP | 20-30 us | 35-50 us | 4.5 / 11 GB/s | needs 3-6 streams; receive memcpy 3-5 GB/s/core | 3-6 cores: use RDMA instead |
| 25GbE RoCEv2 CX-4 Lx | 5-8 us | ~30 us (+22 us) | ~2.9 GB/s | 1 / 6 (BDP 23 KB) | 0 dedicated |
| 40GbE RoCE / FDR IB CX-3 | 8-12 us | 20-25 us | 4.5 / ~6 GB/s | 2-3 / 10 | 0 dedicated |
| EDR IB / 100GbE RoCE CX-4/5 | 5-7 us | 12-15 us | ~11.5 GB/s | 2-4 / 20 | 0 dedicated |
Batching is structural: a link allocates slots contiguously, so N consecutive records are one SCSI WRITE, one sendmsg or one SGE list; the IOPS ceilings above are for unbatched 4 KiB puts.
##### Ring sizing
Capacity = ingest x (CP interval + max CP duration) + in-flight. At 10GbE line rate (1.15 GB/s) with a 10 s ceiling and 10 s CP the formula asks for 23 GB; instead cap by log-half-full: an 8 GiB ring fires a CP every ~3.5 s at full ingest and a 12-drive RAID6 drains 4 GiB in ~3 s (1.4 GB/s), so 8 GiB per direction (16 GiB per node, 128 MiB of descriptors) balances a 10GbE front end against one HDD aggregate. 25GbE ingest (2.9 GB/s) exceeds any HDD pool's drain, so the ring fills and the honest behaviour is back-pressure (BUSY), not a bigger ring. Quarantine adds only in-flight bytes (256 x 64 KiB = 16 MiB), but on FC those blocks stay unusable for up to 10 s.
##### Translation overheads on trixie
- LIO -> ublk -> D-Blade: ublk on 6.12 is copy-based (USER_COPY 6.4; zero-copy is 6.15), so every client write costs one extra userspace memcpy (~6 us per 64 KiB at ~10 GB/s single-core) plus two wakeups (~5-10 us). Acceptable for homelab; it is the floor until trixie-backports or Debian 14 brings 6.15+.
- FC receive: one kernel memcpy at 1.6 GB/s is ~15% of a core; the scanner that indexes the inbound ring is off the ack path for FC and RDMA, so it can sleep 1 ms and cost ~0.
- TCP at 100GbE is CPU-bound by receive copies; RDMA is the only honest 100G leg.
##### FC-specific limits
qla2xxx initiator error handling escalates abort -> LUN reset -> target reset -> ISP abort; an ISP abort resets the whole port for seconds, so a shared mirror/client port couples a wedged partner to client sessions. `ql2xexchoffld`/`ql2xiniexchg` (1024 each) bound concurrent exchanges per port. R_A_TOV (10 s) bounds unknown fate.

#### Creative solutions
- **Spill-on-silence for node-local disks.** When the partner goes silent >2 s and no shared shelf exists, the survivor sequentially appends the partner's inbound ring to a local spill file (one 8 GiB sequential write, ~5-10 s on HDD, <1 s on NVMe). The volatile ring stops being the only copy of the partner's last window during a multi-hour partner outage, at zero hot-path cost, and the spill replays when the partner returns. This turns "manual failover, RPO = last CP" into "manual failover, RPO = 0 for mirrored writes".
- **Receiver-passive FC and RDMA.** Because Placed comes from hardware (SCSI GOOD, RC ACK) and records self-validate, the receiving daemon is off the ack path entirely; the inbound ring keeps filling through a daemon restart (tmpfs file outlives the process). Only the TCP leg needs a live receiver thread.
- **Spike the FC receive path with no FC hardware.** `tcm_loop` + fileio(`write_back`) on tmpfs reproduces the exact kernel path (target-core sgl -> `vfs_iter_write` -> shmem pages -> mmap) on any machine or VM; the qla2xxx spike then only has to prove login and exchange behaviour.
- **rxe for CI.** `rdma_rxe` is built in both Debian kernels; the RDMA implementation runs unchanged in a VM, so fallback-flip and replay tests run in CI without ConnectX hardware.
- **Whole-block descriptor rewrites as the universal rule.** Making every leg rewrite link-private 4 KiB descriptor blocks (not just FC) removes a transport-specific branch and lets the scanner validate one block CRC per 64 slots.
- **Dedicated-port rule from dual-port cards.** QLE2562/2672/2692 are all dual-port: mirror on port 1, clients on port 2, so initiator-side EH on the mirror port can never ISP-abort client sessions. Same rule for NICs: the busy-polled mirror link gets its own port and core.
- **One probe LUN per FC link for fail-back.** A 4 KiB scratch slot written and read back proves the partner's LIO is live before puts flip back, without touching ControlLink.

#### Holes in the plan
- **Descriptor-block granularity (digest 9).** Dense 64 B headers in shared 4 KiB blocks cannot be written from a SCSI LUN without clobbering neighbours. Fix: link-private blocks, whole-block rewrites, per-block quarantine; `header_granularity` in Caps.
- **Debian 12 cannot run the D-Blade as designed.** Debian's 6.1 config has no `CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK` line at all (Kconfig default n), so there is no ublk on bookworm without backports. Also no nvmet FCLOOP/LOOP for test rigs, no tmpfs `noswap`. Decide trixie now; stop qualifying 6.1.
- **The chosen RDMA crate decides whether ConnectX-3 works.** jonhoo `ibverbs` uses only `ibv_create_qp_ex`+`ibv_wr_*`; mlx4's provider rejects `SEND_OPS_FLAGS`. Anyone who prototypes with that crate silently drops CX-3, which is the cheapest used-market RoCE/IB card. Use sideway `BasicQueuePair` or raw bindings.
- **16G FC firmware is whatever is in flash.** Debian ships no ql2600/ql2700 blob and the driver prefers flash for ISP27xx; target-mode fixes live in firmware too. Phase 1 must record `fw_version` from `/sys/class/fc_host` and set a minimum (verify which), and the docs must point at Marvell's flash tools.
- **Volatile DRAM + node-local disks = hours of single-copy exposure.** Takeover is impossible anyway (digest 10), but the surviving ring is now the only copy of the partner's last window until repair; without spill-on-silence a power blip during the outage loses acknowledged writes. The plan's "clients accept losing 10-15 s on simultaneous loss" does not cover this sequential double fault.
- **Flush semantics under GrantUnmirrored.** With the partner down, SYNC CACHE/FUA/NFS COMMIT acks mean "one DRAM copy". Add a per-aggregate policy (`allow | busy | sync-to-disk`); `sync-to-disk` acks flushes only after the record is on the local aggregate and is the honest default for boxes without a UPS. Protocols/Storage own the plumbing; the fabric layer just exposes `mirrored: bool` per ack.
- **Watchdog petting on control-plane liveness alone misses FC stalls.** A qla2xxx ISP abort or hung partner target stalls I/O 30-60 s while Raft stays healthy, so the lease-gated watchdog never fires and clients time out at 15-35 s. Pet on control liveness AND I/O progress (digest 11 lesson); the FC leg must report stalls to the pet loop. softdog itself: default `soft_margin` is 60 s and it needs a running kernel timer, so set `soft_margin=5 nowayout=1` and prefer iTCO/sp5100_tco where the board has one; it cannot fence a hard-locked kernel, so also set `hardlockup_panic=1 softlockup_panic=1 panic=5`.
- **Partition matrix (ring link vs ControlLink).** Ethernet down, FC up: A cannot renew its 3 s lease and fences at ~8 s; B's inbound ring is complete so B+witness take over with zero loss; the FC leg's value is exactly this case. Ethernet up, FC down: redelivery on TCP, nothing else. Both down at A only: A fences, B+witness take over. Both down at both (shared switch): nobody has quorum, both fence, no split brain but full outage; recommend the witness on a separate switch/port and ControlLink on two NICs. Asymmetric wedge (link up, partner LIO hung): detected only by the 2 s sd timeout; without the udev rule it is 30 s plus EH.
- **N2N dual-mode is unproven and its failure mode is subtle.** The driver chooses who PLOGIs by WWPN comparison (`fcport_is_bigger`); two Linux ports both in dual mode have exercised this path only in vendor labs. If the spike fails, a used Brocade 300 (8G) is the fallback; design the FC leg so fabric vs N2N is configuration only.
- **One N-Blade box, many pollers.** Busy-polled TCP mirror links cost a core per link per side; LIO, nvmet, nfsd, ksmbd and the S3 gateway all want cores. On a 4-core homelab CPU this is three products fighting; set an 8C/16T minimum and reserve 1-2 isolated cores (`isolcpus`/`nohz_full`) for the mirror path, or default to RDMA/FC where no core is burned.
- **Plex replication doubles interconnect load.** ONTAP-Select-style plexes send every CP segment to the partner on top of the NVLOG ring: 2x ingest on the link, so 10GbE caps ingest at ~0.6 GB/s. Needs a second link or FC LUN for the bulk stream; the ring must have priority.

#### Phase 1 recommendation
##### Build (trixie, in this order)
1. Ring format and `MirrorLink`/`MirrorInbound` crates with a loopback implementation; replay/dedupe property tests (reordering, duplicates, torn records, epoch flips).
2. TCP leg (pinned busy-poll thread, SEND_ZC above 16 KiB), then RDMA leg on `rdma_rxe` in CI and CX-3/CX-4 in the lab via sideway `BasicQueuePair`.
3. FC leg against `tcm_loop` first, then qla2xxx N2N, then a used 8G switch.
4. Multi-link fallback and fail-back, heartbeat table, udev/modprobe drop-ins shipped as package files.
##### Spikes that gate belief (pass criteria)
- **S1 N2N dual-mode**: two QLE2562s and two QLE2692s, both ports `qlini_mode=dual`, each sees the other's LUN; 100 cable pulls, re-login <5 s each, no oops; 4 KiB put p50 <100 us (8G) / <80 us (16G).
- **S2 fileio-on-tmpfs**: `tcm_loop` + fileio(write_back) on `huge=always,noswap` tmpfs; 1 M records; every SCSI GOOD is followed by a CRC-valid record in the mapping within 10 us; single LIO thread copies >=2 GB/s; scanner at 1 ms sleep <1% of a core.
- **S3 RDMA on CX-3 vs CX-4**: register an 8 GiB tmpfs THP file as MRs on mlx4 and mlx5; 4 KiB WRITE completion p50 <12 us (CX-3) / <8 us (CX-4); 64 KiB sustained >=4 GB/s (FDR) / >=2.8 GB/s (25G); QP->ERR flushes every outstanding WR within 10 ms.
- **S4 fallback flip under load**: FC primary at 80% of line rate, pull the cable; TCP carries new puts within 1 s link-dead + redelivery; replay after a forced takeover shows zero gaps and zero duplicates; fail-back after 30 s with no client-visible stall >2 s.
##### Phase 1 success criteria
- 4 KiB mirrored write acked to the client in <60 us on 25GbE RoCE, <120 us on 10GbE TCP, <250 us on 8GFC (end to end, including the ublk hop).
- Sustained ingest at 10GbE line rate into an 8 GiB ring with back-to-back CPs on a 12-drive RAID6 for 10 minutes without BUSY.
- Partner daemon kill and restart with no client I/O error on FC and RDMA legs (receiver-passive proven).
- Ethernet-down/FC-up partition: B serves within 10 s with zero lost acknowledged writes.

#### Open questions
- **Is FC a Phase 1 deliverable or a Phase 2 tier?** Unblocks whether S1 is on the critical path and whether the week-1 hardware order includes a used 8G switch.
- **Which used FC cards are the floor: 8G ISP25xx (Debian-shipped firmware) or 16G ISP27xx (flash firmware only)?** Decides the minimum-firmware policy and whether we document Marvell flash tooling.
- **Shared SAS shelf, plex replication, or manual failover for the reference homelab?** Decides whether spill-on-silence is a must-have and whether the interconnect needs a second bulk link.
- **Is ConnectX-3 a supported RDMA card?** It forces the legacy `ibv_post_send` path and MTT-size handling; dropping it simplifies the RDMA leg and reopens the jonhoo crate.
- **Minimum CPU per node (cores reserved for busy-polling)?** Decides whether TCP busy-poll is default or RDMA/FC-first.
- **Default policy for flush/FUA acks when unmirrored (`allow | busy | sync-to-disk`)?** Owner's durability stance; affects N-Blade advertising of WCE/VWC.
- **Will the witness live on a separate switch or port from the HA interconnect?** Decides the both-links-down behaviour (outage vs takeover).

#### Load-bearing claims
- [FABRICS-1] (high) Debian trixie's linux 6.12.107-1 debian/config/config sets CONFIG_TCM_QLA2XXX=m, CONFIG_TCM_USER2=m, CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK=m, CONFIG_NVME_TARGET_{FC,TCP,RDMA,FCLOOP,LOOP}=m, CONFIG_SMB_SERVER=m, CONFIG_NFSD_V4=y, CONFIG_MD_CLUSTER=m, CONFIG_MLX4_INFINIBAND=m, CONFIG_RDMA_RXE=m and CONFIG_SOFT_WATCHDOG=m.
  - why it matters: The entire in-box kernel-target and ublk strategy on trixie, plus tcm_loop/rxe-based spikes and CI.
- [FABRICS-2] (high) Debian bookworm's linux 6.1.176-1 config files contain no CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK line and leave CONFIG_NVME_TARGET_FCLOOP and CONFIG_NVME_TARGET_LOOP unset, so plain bookworm kernels have no ublk_drv.
  - why it matters: Disqualifies Debian 12 without backports as a baseline for the D-Blade.
- [FABRICS-3] (high) Debian's python3-rtslib-fb (2.1.75-2.1 bookworm, 2.1.76-3 trixie) carries a single patch that only changes the saveconfig path to /etc/rtslib-fb-target/saveconfig.json, so targetcli-fb 1:2.1.53 exposes the upstream qla2xxx fabric.
  - why it matters: FC target configuration via stock Debian tooling and the configfs paths our daemon will write.
- [FABRICS-4] (high) rdma-core's mlx4 provider omits IBV_QP_INIT_ATTR_SEND_OPS_FLAGS from MLX4_CREATE_QP_SUP_COMP_MASK and implements no ibv_wr_* functions, while the jonhoo ibverbs 0.9.2 crate uses only ibv_create_qp_ex with the ibv_wr_* API; sideway 0.4.3's BasicQueuePair uses ibv_create_qp and ibv_post_send.
  - why it matters: Crate selection; whether ConnectX-3 (mlx4) can be a supported RDMA mirror card.
- [FABRICS-5] (high) In Linux 6.12 target_core_file opens the backing file O_RDWR|O_CREAT|O_LARGEFILE|O_DSYNC, fd_buffered_io=1 clears O_DSYNC and forces emulate_write_cache=1, writes go through vfs_iter_write on a bvec iterator (one kernel copy), and FD_MAX_BYTES limits one command to 8 MiB; hugetlbfs provides no write_iter so it cannot back a fileio device.
  - why it matters: The zero-userspace-copy FC receive path and the choice of tmpfs for the inbound ring.
- [FABRICS-6] (high) Linux 6.12's qla2xxx loads ISP24xx/25xx firmware via request_firmware before flash and ISP81xx/27xx from flash before request_firmware; Debian firmware-qlogic 20250410-2 ships ql2100..ql2500_fw.bin only (ql2500_fw.bin = 8.08.207), so QLE2672/QLE2692 run flash-resident firmware.
  - why it matters: Minimum-firmware policy and whether target-mode fixes reach 16G used-market cards without Marvell tools.
- [FABRICS-7] (high) qla2xxx in 6.12 exposes the qlini_mode module parameter with values exclusive, disabled, dual and enabled (default enabled), and its target-mode PLOGI/PRLI handlers contain N2N_TOPO branches, but there is no public report of Linux-to-Linux dual-mode N2N between two qla2xxx ports.
  - why it matters: Whether the FC leg can run switchless and whether S1 is the Phase 1 critical path.
- [FABRICS-8] (medium) A 4 KiB mirror put reaches Placed in roughly 5-10 us on 25GbE RoCE (sender CQE after responder ACK), 25-40 us on busy-polled 10GbE TCP, and 50-100 us on 8GFC via LIO (two fabric round trips plus two firmware traversals plus the fileio copy), so FC is the isolation tier, not the latency tier.
  - why it matters: Transport ranking, Phase 1 ordering (RDMA and TCP before FC) and the latency success criteria.

### Opus-Storage

#### Executive take
I would build the D-Blade as a hugetlbfs-backed DRAM mirror ring that is the only log, a per-volume dirty index over it, and a never-overwrite segment store with a copy-on-write extent map committed by an A/B superblock stripe; one code path for HDD and flash, with a `MediaProfile` (segment size, stripe geometry, CP timer, discard policy, I/O method) as the only media-specific knob, and HA that is media-dependent: a used dual-EMM SAS shelf for spinning pools, plex-by-reference for node-local flash, manual failover for everything else. The single biggest risk is not the volatile cache the owner has priced in; it is where the ring lives: if it is anonymous memory inside `dbladed`, every daemon segfault becomes a takeover, and an unmirrored segfault becomes a data loss, so the ring must be a hugetlbfs file the daemon re-attaches to, with the index rebuildable from it in seconds. The thing I most disagree with is round 1's ruling that DRAM mode advertises write-cache-disabled: with a volatile cache that is a lie initiators act on (they stop issuing flushes and lose their own ordering barrier), so OpenTAP must advertise WCE=1/VWC and define FLUSH as "everything before me is Placed on both nodes", with flush-forces-a-CP reserved for unmirrored operation. Second disagreement: the brief treats node-local disks and takeover as compatible; they are not, and the numbers below say a $300-500 used shelf buys real takeover for HDD pools while flash gets plex-by-reference at 50% capacity. One verified fact reshapes the baseline: Debian 12's stock 6.1 kernel ships no `ublk_drv` at all, so the floor is trixie 6.12 (or bookworm-backports 6.12), and trixie-backports (6.16 to 7.2 today) is the opt-in tier for ublk zero-copy.

#### Proposed libraries and subsystems
##### Crates (my view of maturity)
- `io-uring` 0.7.x (tokio-rs): low-level, tracks kernel features, the right base for a thread-per-core reactor with `SINGLE_ISSUER` + `DEFER_TASKRUN`; do not put `tokio` on the data path (control plane only). `monoio`/`glommio` are usable but hide the registration and NAPI knobs we need.
- `libublk` 0.4.8 (ublk-org, Aug 2026, the ublk maintainer's own crate, ~480K downloads): the ublk server for `nbladed`; mature enough, API still moving (several yanked 0.4.x). Fallback: speak `/dev/ublk-control` directly over `io-uring`.
- `crc-fast` 1.10 (SIMD CRC32C/CRC64) or `crc32c`; `blake3` for superblock and map-root digests; `zerocopy` 0.8 for the `repr(C)` 64 B header, superblock and map pages (mature, Fuchsia-grade).
- `rustix` 1.x / `libc`: mmap of hugetlbfs (`MAP_HUGETLB|MAP_HUGE_2MB`), `mlock2`, `madvise`, `fallocate`, `BLKDISCARD`, `SG_IO` for persistent reservations; `memmap2` for convenience.
- `parking_lot`, `crossbeam` (epoch for the CP cut grace period, SPSC queues); `hdrhistogram`, `metrics`/`prometheus`; `proptest`, `loom`, `cargo-fuzz`, Miri on the arena and index crates.
- No KV-store crate for the block map (`sled`, `redb`, `fjall` bring their own logs); the CoW B+tree is ours. `roaring` only for the free-segment set if it ever leaves DRAM.
- Phase 3 option: Reed-Solomon GF(2^8) for user-space RAID6 syndrome repair (`reed-solomon-erasure` or a 300-line port of mdadm's `raid6check` math).

##### Kernel interfaces (Debian release that provides them)
- ublk (`CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK`): absent from Debian 12's 6.1.176 config and package contents; `ublk_drv.ko.xz` ships in trixie 6.12.94/107 and bookworm-backports 6.12.43 to 6.12.95 (verified). USER_COPY (6.4) is in 6.12; zero-copy and AUTO_BUF_REG need 6.15, i.e. trixie-backports (6.16 to 7.2.x available today).
- io_uring: registered buffers over huge pages coalesce to one bvec per 2 MiB page (since about 6.3, verify exact release); `IORING_REGISTER_CLONE_BUFFERS` since 6.12 (verified) so the ingest ring, CP ring and transport ring share one registration; NAPI busy poll 6.9; NVMe passthrough `IORING_OP_URING_CMD` 5.19 with IOPOLL from 6.1 (verify). `kernel.io_uring_disabled` upstream default 0; I believe Debian ships no override; verify.
- md raid456 (`=m` in both kernels): `stripe_cache_size` 32768 (RAM = 32768 x 4 KiB x nr_disks = 1.5 GiB for 12 drives), `group_thread_cnt`, `skip_copy`, write-intent bitmap, `raid456.devices_handle_discard_safely=Y` (DISCARD is disabled by default on RAID4/5/6; verified).
- hugetlbfs (`=y`): pinned, never swapped, registrable for io_uring and RDMA; no `write(2)` path (contents change only via mmap/truncate, verified), so a kernel target can only land data in a tmpfs ring, not a hugetlbfs one. tmpfs with swap off and `mlock` is the FC/iSCSI "memory window" ring.
- LIO (`TCM_IBLOCK/FILEIO/USER2=m`, `TCM_QLA2XXX=m` in both 6.1 and 6.12, verified): fileio on tmpfs for the FC ring LUN; `emulate_write_cache=1` for honest WCE. `SMB_SERVER=m`, `NFSD_V4=y`, `NVME_TARGET_{FC,TCP,RDMA}=m` in both; `NVME_TARGET_FCLOOP` unset in 6.1, `=m` in 6.12. `MD_CLUSTER=m` both (unused: not a fence).
- Test tooling in both kernels: `dm-log-writes`, `dm-flakey` (`=m`), the crash-consistency replay used by xfstests; `SOFT_WATCHDOG=m`, but prefer `iTCO_wdt`/`sp5100_tco`/`ipmi_watchdog` where present.
- `sg3_utils` (`sg_persist`, `sg_vpd` for DRAT/RZAT, `smartctl -l scterc,70,70`), `mdadm` 4.3/4.4 (trixie); `raid6check` exists in mdadm's source but I believe Debian's binary package does not install it (verify with `apt-file`).
##### Daemons
`dbladed` per aggregate (ring, index, CP, map, GC, cache), `nbladed` (ublk server, LIO/nvmet applier), `otap-ctl` (leases, fences, watchdog, Raft seeds), plus XFS-on-LUN mounts for NAS, exported by knfsd and ksmbd or Samba (Protocols' call).

#### Proposed architecture
##### 1. Write pipeline
- **Arena = outbound ring.** One hugetlbfs file per (aggregate, direction) under `/dev/hugepages/otap/`, 2 MiB pages (1 GiB if reserved at boot), `MAP_SHARED`, mlocked, registered once with io_uring and once as an RDMA MR, cloned into the other rings. Round-1 layout stands: dense 64 B headers in 4 KiB descriptor blocks plus a 4 KiB-aligned payload ring. The daemon never owns this memory: a `dbladed` crash leaves the ring intact, the restarted daemon re-attaches, rescans self-validating records (8 GiB in 1 to 2 s, CRC-bound) and resumes under ublk `USER_RECOVERY_REISSUE` with `req_id` dedupe. A daemon crash is a 2 s pause, never a takeover.
- **Ingest path.** ublk request → slot chosen by a per-queue bump allocator with lap counter → one kernel copy into the slot (USER_COPY `pread` on 6.12; on 6.15+ the request buffer is sent to the partner directly and copied once into the arena, the copy is needed anyway for the CP) → CRC32C per 4 KiB (0.2 us) → aggregate-wide `seq` → insert into the volume's `BTreeMap<u64, DirtyExtent>` with split/trim of overlaps → post to `MirrorRing`. Ack = local insert done AND partner Placed, overlapped (the put goes out before the insert). Superseded extents stay slot-resident until the CP covering their `seq` commits: slot free is a CP-commit event only, so local and partner slot lifetimes are identical by construction.
- **Read-your-writes.** `resolve(vol, lba, len)` yields pieces from the active index, the frozen (CP-in-flight) index, then the map: `Ring(slot, lap) | Disk(pba) | Zero`. Sub-4 KiB writes (512e LUNs are mandatory for ESXi, see claims) are patch records with a sector mask, merged at read and at CP time against the old block from cache or disk.
- **CP triggers.** Timer (HDD 10 s, flash 5 s, a ceiling), ring half full (the ring is double-buffered: one half drains while the other ingests), back-to-back (previous CP done and the active half already over 25%), takeover, shutdown, unmirrored flush, snapshot. Under load the effective interval is `min(timer, ring/2 ÷ ingest)`: on a 64 GB box with 8 GiB rings at 1 GB/s that is a CP every 4 s.
- **Throttle.** ZFS-shaped: zero delay below 60% of the active half, `delay = k · occ/(1−occ)` above, hard wait at 100%, `k` recomputed from the drain rate of the last three CPs; never fail a write (ublk cannot return BUSY to LIO; an error becomes a CHECK CONDITION), single delay capped at 2 s to stay under initiator command timeouts.
- **The cut is a consistent global cut.** One `seq` per aggregate; the CP flips an epoch and waits a grace period until no ingest thread is still inside an insert of the old epoch (microseconds, no I/O pause), then `cut = all records with seq ≤ S`. A guest can only make W2 depend on W1 by waiting for W1's ack, and W1 is inserted (seq assigned) before it is acked, so `seq(W1) < seq(W2)` for every dependency across every LUN and volume of the aggregate; a seq-prefix is therefore exactly an image a power cut could have produced, which is what guest journals are designed for. Unacked writes inside the prefix are fine (a power cut lands those too). N-Blade obligations: complete to the kernel only after the D-Blade ack; never re-submit in a way that lets a later-issued dependent write reach the D-Blade before an earlier acked one (ublk REISSUE only reorders unacked requests, which carry no dependency; duplicates dedupe by `req_id`).
- **Flush/FUA.** Advertise WCE=1 (LIO `emulate_write_cache=1`) and NVMe VWC. FLUSH completes when every record with `seq ≤ flush.seq` is Placed on both nodes, which acks already guarantee, so a flush costs one in-flight-put wait, nothing more; FUA = ordinary write. Under `GrantUnmirrored` a cluster knob `unmirrored_flush = placed | cp` lets the operator make FLUSH force and await a CP (0.5 to 5 s) while the partner is down. WCE=0 is never advertised.

##### 2. Media-adaptive allocator
- **HDD (shared shelf, 12 x 12 to 16 TB NL-SAS).** md RAID6 10+2, chunk 256 KiB, stripe 2.5 MiB data, segment = 32 stripes = 80 MiB (whole-segment GC read 0.4 s at 200 MB/s; 100 TB = 1.3 M segments = 40 MiB of segment table), bitmap chunk 128 MiB ≥ segment, `stripe_cache_size` 32768, `group_thread_cnt` 4, `skip_copy` 1, SCT ERC 7 s, SCSI timeout 10 s. The CP writes data segments with O_DIRECT `writev` straight from ring slots (1 to 2 MiB per call; md merges to full stripes, zero member reads), then map and segment-table pages, one `fsync` (flush fan-out), then the superblock as a FUA full stripe (A/B alternating). Drain 1.2 to 1.8 GB/s pre-GC, 0.7 to 1.0 GB/s at 80% full; 10GbE ingest (1.1 GB/s) is the cap anyway; 1.5 to 2.4 K random IOPS from spindles, so the read cache is mandatory.
- **Flash (node-local 4 to 8 NVMe).** md RAID5 (RAID6 from 8 drives), chunk 64 KiB, segment 8 MiB, CP timer 5 s, two open segments (ingest and GC-cold) plus a metadata stream. Log-structuring stays: never-overwrite is what closes md's write hole and makes the CP a pure commit; it is also what SSDs want (F2FS, ZNS). Costs: GC WA 1.2 to 1.6 at 70 to 80% full on top of SSD WA; endurance budget 3.84 TB x 1 DWPD = 44 MB/s continuous versus a homelab average of 5 to 20 MB/s. Discard: `raid456.devices_handle_discard_safely=Y` and DRAT/RZAT verified at pool creation, whole-segment discards on free, guest UNMAP becomes map holes. CPU: ingest about 6 to 8 us per 4 KiB write (ublk CQE 2, copy 0.3, CRC 0.2, index 0.5, mirror post and ack 2 to 3, completion 1) so 1 M IOPS is about 7 cores on the owner plus 2 to 3 on the partner; the CP costs about 1.5 us per record; 25GbE caps at about 700 K records/s. Single NVMe without md uses io_uring NVMe passthrough with IOPOLL; RAID1/10 passes discards through and needs no parity.
- **Hybrid.** HDD pool plus a node-local NVMe read cache (1 to 4 TB) plus, in the shelf, 2 x SAS SSD RAID1 as a metadata class (map pages, segment summaries, superblocks). Cache keyed by physical address with 32 KiB lines (16 B per line: 1 TB = 0.5 GiB of index), demand-filled on random read misses under 128 KiB, always holding map pages and summaries, CP write-through only for blocks under 32 KiB and only inside a token bucket of 1 DWPD of the cache device (1 TB = 12 MB/s average). Tiering hot extents to flash: no; a cache gives most of the benefit with none of the map and GC complexity. Cold tiering to S3 is Phase 4.

##### 3. Map and features
Per-volume CoW B+tree, 16 KiB nodes, ~16 B per extent `{lba_delta, len, pba(seg:28|off:20), birth_cp}`: worst case (4 KiB random) 0.4% of volume size, typical 0.05%; 100 TB fully fragmented = 400 GB, so the map lives on disk with a 4 to 16 GiB DRAM page cache; each CP writes changed leaves and their paths (2 to 5% of CP bytes), no delta log, boot = read the root. Snapshots pin a volume root at a cut (a `Snap` record); clones are writable child roots; frees go to per-snapshot deadlists (ZFS semantics) and decrement per-segment `live_bytes`. GC selects victims by cost-benefit (free x age), reads whole segments, writes survivors to the cold stream. Reserves: warn 80%, GC urgent 90%, write-stop 95%; usable shown as 0.9 x physical. Scrub reads live segments at 10% of drain and verifies per-4 KiB CRC32C from segment summaries; a mismatch retries, heals from a plex if one exists, else returns a medium error and flags the extent; lost writes are caught because the summary carries `(vol, lba, seq)` to compare with the map's `birth`. Phase 3: user-space P/Q reconstruction from md members (`raid6check`-style), rewriting through `/dev/mdX`.

##### 4. Crash consistency
(a) Clean: final CP, clean flag in the superblock, no scan. (b) Single-node crash: partner holds the inbound ring and standby index; fence (lease expiry plus PR preempt-abort on every shelf drive, 0.2 s), `mdadm --assemble` 1 to 3 s with a background bitmap resync, read superblocks A/B, take the highest `(epoch, cp_seq)` whose root pages verify, residual scan of placed-but-unindexed records (bounded by 20 ms x ingest = 20 MB), admit I/O, then an immediate CP to close the dual-fault window; records per CP capped at ring/2 ÷ 4 KiB = 1 M at 8 GiB, standby index ≤ 64 MiB. (c) Dual power loss: boot to the last committed CP; an "opened" stamp written at mount means the alert fires even if no CP ran since boot: `ROLLBACK{agg, cp_seq, cp_wallclock, window ≤ interval, est_bytes = bytes_since_prev_cp}` to syslog CRIT, cluster DB (needs operator acknowledgement) and webhook, plus a power-on UNIT ATTENTION on every LUN so initiators re-probe. Clients see a crash-consistent image at most 15 s old; snapshots and clones are untouched.
**Memory per box.** 64 GB: 2 x 8 GiB rings (own outbound, partner inbound), indexes 0.3 GiB, map cache 6 GiB, segment tables 0.1 GiB, cache index 0.5 GiB, md stripe cache 1.5 GiB per array, 30+ GB left for OS, XFS page cache and headroom; 128 GB: 2 x 16 GiB rings, 16 GiB map cache; 256 GB: rings stay at 16 GiB (more buys nothing at ≤ 2 GB/s and 5 s CPs), the rest to map cache and page cache. Footprint fixed at start, swap off, `oom_score_adj=-1000`.

##### 5. Node-local disks versus takeover
- **HDD pools: used dual-EMM shelf (recommended).** MD1200/MD1220 (6G) ≈ $150-300, MD1400 (12G) ≈ $400-800, 2 x LSI 9300-8e ≈ $100, cables ≈ $40, 12 TB NL-SAS ≈ $90-120 (SATA price parity on the used market; prices are my belief, verify). Host A on EMM0, host B on EMM1, one path per drive per host, no multipath; 100% capacity and ingest; takeover 6 to 8 s. The single midplane is the one shared fault (data survives, drives move to any SAS host). md is assembled by the lease holder only (`AUTO -all`), PR on the members is the second fence.
- **Flash pools: plex-by-reference (recommended).** Identical md geometry on both nodes; the partner already holds every CP payload in its inbound ring, so the owner ships only a `CpManifest` (slot→PBA placements, new map pages, freed segments, ~5% of CP bytes) and the partner writes the same segments to its own array; GC runs by manifest too. Link traffic stays at 1x ingest; each array absorbs two aggregates' CPs (1.5 to 2 GB/s per aggregate on a 4-NVMe RAID5, above a 25GbE link); usable 50%; takeover 5 to 6 s with no assembly; rejoin resync is a segment diff by `cp_seq`.
- **Manual failover.** RPO = last CP, RTO = human; for single-box labs with a backup target.
Allocator differences: shelf = one allocator, partner loads superblock and segment table at takeover; plex = deterministic allocator on both nodes with the manifest as the proof; manual = none.

##### Sketches
```rust
pub struct MediaProfile {                      // the only media-specific knob set
    pub segment_bytes: u64,                    // HDD 80 MiB (32 full stripes), NVMe 8 MiB, RAID1/single 4 MiB
    pub stripe_data_bytes: u32,                // md chunk x data disks; 0 = no parity geometry
    pub cp_timer: Duration,                    // HDD 10 s, flash 5 s; ring/2 wins under load
    pub io: IoPath,                            // Md { fua_super } | NvmePassthru { iopoll } | Plain
    pub discard: Discard,                      // Never | WholeSegment { requires_rzat }
    pub gc_start: f32, pub gc_urgent: f32, pub write_stop: f32,   // 0.80, 0.90, 0.95
}
pub struct SlotHandle { pub slot: u32, pub lap: u32 }            // ABA-safe: ring[slot].lap must match
pub struct DirtyExtent { pub h: SlotHandle, pub len: u32, pub seq: u64, pub sector_mask: u8 }
pub struct VolumeIndex { pub by_lba: BTreeMap<u64, DirtyExtent>, pub bytes: u64 }   // split/trim on overlap
pub enum CpTrigger { Timer, RingHalfFull, BackToBack, Takeover, Shutdown, UnmirroredFlush, Snapshot(SnapId) }
pub struct Pba(u64);                                             // seg:28 | off:20 (4 KiB units) | spare:16
pub struct MapExtent { pub lba: u64, pub len: u32, pub pba: Pba, pub birth: CpSeq }   // ~16 B on disk
pub trait BlockMap {                           // per-volume CoW B+tree, 16 KiB nodes, one root per CP
    fn resolve(&self, vol: VolId, lba: u64, len: u32, out: &mut Vec<MapExtent>) -> Result<()>;
    fn apply(&mut self, vol: VolId, new: &[MapExtent], dead: &mut DeadList) -> Result<()>;
    fn checkpoint(&mut self, w: &mut SegmentWriter) -> Result<MapRoot>;   // writes dirty pages
    fn pin(&mut self, vol: VolId, at: CpSeq) -> SnapId;                   // snapshot = pinned root
}
pub trait ReadCache {                          // keyed by PHYSICAL address: the log never overwrites
    fn get(&self, pba: Pba, len: u32, dst: &mut [u8]) -> bool;
    fn fill(&self, pba: Pba, src: &[u8], class: CacheClass, filled_at: CpSeq);
    fn drop_segment(&self, seg: SegId, lap: u32);
}
pub trait Backstore {                          // md array | single NVMe | (Phase 3) native LS-RAID
    fn profile(&self) -> MediaProfile;
    fn write_segment(&mut self, seg: SegId, iov: &[IoSlice<'_>], tag: u64) -> Result<(), Busy>; // full stripes
    fn read(&mut self, pba: Pba, len: u32, dst: &mut [u8], tag: u64) -> Result<(), Busy>;
    fn commit(&mut self, sb: &Superblock, slot: SbSlot, tag: u64) -> Result<(), Busy>;   // flush, FUA stripe
    fn discard(&mut self, seg: SegId) -> Result<(), Busy>;
    fn poll(&mut self, out: &mut Vec<Done>) -> usize;
}
pub struct CpManifest { pub agg: AggId, pub cp: CpSeq, pub cut_seq: u64,                 // plex-by-reference
    pub placements: Vec<(SlotHandle, Pba)>, pub meta_pages: Vec<(Pba, Box<[u8; 16384]>)>, pub frees: Vec<SegId> }
```

#### Bottlenecks
##### Link versus media
10GbE carries 1.1 GB/s, below an HDD drain of 1.2 to 1.8 GB/s, so a spinning pool on 10GbE is link-bound and the CP never falls behind; on 25GbE (2.9 GB/s) the same pool is drain-bound and the throttle holds ingest at the GC-adjusted 0.7 to 1.0 GB/s. Four NVMe absorb 4 to 8 GB/s, so flash is link-bound until 100GbE RoCE (11 GB/s), and CPU-bound before that at small blocks: 25GbE = 700 K 4 KiB records/s, which at 6 to 8 us each is 5 to 6 cores of ingest plus 1 core of CP plus 2 to 3 on the partner.

##### DRAM, not disks, sets the CP interval
Ring capacity = ingest x (interval + max CP duration) + quarantine would need 1 GB/s x (10 + 7) s = 17 GB per direction, 34 GB per node, on a 64 GB box. The rule therefore inverts: ring = DRAM budget (8 GiB per direction at 64 GB), interval = ring/2 ÷ ingest = 4 s at 1 GB/s, CP duration 2.7 s at 1.5 GB/s drain, back-to-back the steady state. The "10 to 15 s window" is a ceiling at low load, not a design point. A hung disk (30 s SCSI timeout plus retries) turns a 3 s CP into a 60 s one, the ring fills, the throttle goes to a hard wait: degraded service, not corruption, but the SCT ERC and timeout settings are therefore mandatory, not tuning.

##### The map
Worst case 16 B per 4 KiB = 0.4%: 100 TB fully fragmented = 400 GB on disk; an 8 GiB cache covers 2%. On a spinning pool every uncached map page costs a 10 ms seek before the data seek, so a 4 KiB random read over a fragmented aggregate is 20 ms, not 10 ms: the flash metadata class is mandatory for HDD pools above about 20 TB; realistic workloads (256 KiB to 1 MiB extents from VMFS and NTFS) cut the map 16 to 64x.

##### Translation hops
Through LIO iSCSI → iblock → ublk → `dbladed`: client RTT 50 us + LIO 20 to 40 us CPU + ublk wakeup 5 to 20 us (2 to 5 busy-polled) + mirror 20 to 35 us (TCP) or 5 to 10 us (RoCE) + index and completion 5 to 10 us ≈ 150 to 250 us QD1 on 10GbE; the ublk hop is under 10% of it. NFS → XFS → ublk → D-Blade: async writes are page-cache absorbed; a stable write or COMMIT is an XFS log write with FUA that becomes a Placed-gated flush (+50 us); the cost is memory (XFS page cache competing with rings; cgroup it) and takeover (XFS log replay plus the NFSv4 grace period, Protocols' problem to shrink).

##### GC and the cliff
WA = 1/(1−u) for victim utilisation u: uniform random overwrite at 80% full gives victims near u = 0.75, WA 4, and HDD sustained ingest collapses to about 300 MB/s; skewed workloads give u ≈ 0.3, WA 1.4. Hence the 80/90/95 reserves and hot/cold separation; a log-structured store has no fix for a full pool under uniform overwrite, only a floor.

##### Partial-block writes
Each sub-4 KiB write needs the old block at CP time: a cache hit is free, a spinning miss is a 10 ms seek, so 1,000 unaligned writes per CP cost 10 s. Mitigation: 512e with physical exponent 4 KiB so guests align, a counter and alarm per volume, and patch targets pinned into the read cache at ingest.

##### md itself
The stripe cache costs 1.5 GiB per 12-drive array at 32768; md raid5 writes on NVMe reach, I believe, 3 to 5 GB/s per array with group threads before the stripe-cache memcpy saturates (verify in Phase 1); reads bypass the cache when chunk-aligned. Discards on RAID5/6 are off by default and only safe with RZAT drives.

#### Creative solutions
- **The ring is a file, not daemon memory.** hugetlbfs-backed rings survive `dbladed` crashes; restart = re-attach, rescan, resume under ublk REISSUE. Removes the entire class "software bug equals failover equals (if unmirrored) data loss".
- **Plex-by-reference.** The partner's inbound ring already contains every CP payload, so plex replication ships a manifest (placements, map pages, frees) instead of segments: link traffic 1x, not 2x; the partner's allocator executes the same CP program; GC likewise. Turns the ONTAP Select model from "halved link and halved disks" into "halved disks only".
- **Read cache keyed by physical address.** The log never overwrites, so a PBA is a stable identity; persistence across reboot and power loss is free, invalidation is the segment lap counter plus "drop entries filled after the committed `cp_seq`" at boot. No LBA-keyed invalidation logic at all.
- **The FC mirror ring as a LUN.** Export the partner's tmpfs ring file through in-box LIO fileio over `tcm_qla2xxx`; the sender's qla2xxx initiator writes record R at LBA = slot address, the SCSI completion is the Placed ack, the receiving daemon polls self-validating headers. Zero user-space copies on receive, no dual-mode port needed with a dual-port QLE2562 (port 0 initiator, port 1 target), N2N direct-connect. This is the only shape under which "a kernel target writing into a tmpfs file" is true: the file must be the ring, not a client LUN image.
- **Honest WCE plus a degraded-mode knob.** WCE=1, FLUSH = Placed-on-both barrier (free), `unmirrored_flush=cp` when the partner is away. Guests keep their ordering semantics and the admin keeps the rollback alert.
- **Epoch-flip cut.** Aggregate-wide seq plus a grace period gives a consistent cross-LUN cut with no I/O pause and no per-volume barrier.
- **Takeover-triggered CP.** Admit I/O, then flush immediately: the dual-fault exposure shrinks from a CP interval to a CP duration.
- **Native log-structured RAID later.** Full-stripe-only writes make parity trivial (no RMW, no write hole), so a Phase 3 user-space RAID-Z-style layer replaces md on flash, owns parity for CRC-guided repair, and enables NVMe passthrough with IOPOLL end to end; md stays for Phase 1 and 2 because rebuild and degraded reads are the unglamorous 80%.
- **ZNS and FDP readiness.** Segment = zone maps one to one onto ZNS SSDs; FDP placement hints (io_uring write hints, 6.16+) separate the ingest and GC-cold streams on conventional SSDs. Both are `MediaProfile` variants, not redesigns.

#### Holes in the plan
- **Debian 12's 6.1 kernel has no ublk.** `CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK` is absent from Debian's 6.1.176 config and no `ublk_drv.ko` exists in bookworm's packages; trixie 6.12 and bookworm-backports 6.12 ship it. "Debian 12+" must mean trixie (or bookworm with the backports kernel). Beyond ublk, 6.1 also lacks ublk USER_COPY (6.4, one extra copy per write), io_uring NAPI busy poll (6.9) and CLONE_BUFFERS (6.12); zero-copy ublk needs 6.15 = trixie-backports.
- **"Mirror ring is the only log" plus anonymous DRAM means every daemon crash is a takeover.** Fix: hugetlbfs files, re-attach on restart, index rebuilt from the ring. Without this the volatile model quietly multiplies the loss probability by the daemon's crash rate.
- **Advertising WCE=0 (round-1 ruling for DRAM mode) is wrong under the revised brief.** Initiators that are told there is no write cache stop sending SYNCHRONIZE CACHE, so after a rollback their journals have no barrier semantics to lean on. Advertise WCE=1; FLUSH is free anyway.
- **Flush-forces-a-CP as a general policy would destroy batching** (databases fsync at kHz); it is only acceptable as the unmirrored-mode knob.
- **The CP interval claim.** On 64 GB homelab boxes the ring, not the timer, sets the interval (4 s at 1 GB/s). State the DRAM floor (32 GB absolute, 64 GB recommended) and the sizing rule in the product docs.
- **The tmpfs zero-copy receive claim in digest item 9 is half true.** LIO fileio writes at the LUN's LBA offset, so the daemon learns nothing about which blocks changed unless the file is the ring itself (FC ring LUN). For client LUNs the honest paths are ublk USER_COPY (one kernel copy into the slot) and, on 6.15+, zero-copy send from the request buffer plus one copy into the arena.
- **Takeover with a dead partner and no witness** leaves the survivor unable to get `GrantUnmirrored`; after Stalled 2 s it returns BUSY and the homelab loses service because one VM is down. Put the witness off both nodes and let `ControlLink` fall back to the mirror link's IP (IPoIB, RoCE NIC) so a control-path cut does not look like a partner death.
- **Dual-fault after takeover.** The survivor holds the dead node's last window only in RAM until its first CP; mandate the takeover-triggered CP.
- **Stale I/O from a stalled owner on a shared shelf.** A node stalled past its lease can have HBA-queued CP writes land after the survivor allocated the same free segments. Lease plus watchdog does not cover HBA queues; SCSI-3 PR preempt-abort on every member drive at takeover does (EMMs in unified mode pass PR to the drives; verify on MD1200 with SAS drives). softdog adds nothing here and misses hard lockups; prefer `iTCO_wdt`/`sp5100_tco`/`ipmi_watchdog`, keep softdog as the last fallback with `nowayout=1`.
- **Partition combinations (storage view).** Mirror down, control up: Stalled, `GrantUnmirrored`, partner ring marked stale, resync on return as a ring replay or a full CP plus ring restart if the outage exceeded ring retention. Control down, mirror up: ring heartbeats must never count as liveness; the owner's lease lapses and it reboots although healthy (availability loss, no corruption). Both down: survivor plus witness take over; the loser fences itself. FC ring plus Ethernet control behaves identically; only the Placed latency differs.
- **One `MirrorTransport` trait is honest only as put-at-slot plus Placed.** RDMA and FC receivers cannot reject (the write lands regardless), TCP receivers must run a daemon to produce Placed, and Placed latency spans 5 to 150 us. The trait spans all four if `Caps` carries those three facts and the ring is receiver-optional, which is the round-1 shape; a stream or RPC-style trait would not.
- **md RAID5/6 discard default.** Guest UNMAP on all-flash md pools is silently dropped unless `devices_handle_discard_safely=Y`; the installer must set it and refuse non-RZAT drives.
- **md's periodic `checkarray` will report mismatches** in torn stripes of never-committed segments after a crash until the bitmap resync completes; document it and run our scrub instead.
- **Unified N-Blade on one homelab box.** Block, file and object each bring memory (XFS page cache, Samba, an S3 gateway) that competes with 16 to 32 GiB of rings; without per-service cgroups the first OOM takes `dbladed`. XFS-on-LUN is the right Phase 1/2 file layer (one allocator, LUN-level snapshots mounted with `-o nouuid`); a native inode layer is a multi-year product and should stay off the roadmap until block is shipped.
- **SMR drives.** "Media agnostic" must exclude DM-SMR (undetectable from the block layer; model lists); HM-SMR fits the log design later but md does not support zoned members.
- **Consumer SSDs without PLP** can lose their own volatile cache on power loss; our flush-then-FUA superblock is correct, but some drives corrupt FTL state at power loss; document "enterprise PLP recommended, consumer at your own risk".

#### Phase 1 recommendation
##### Build (Debian trixie 6.12, two nodes, in-process load generator first)
1. `otap-record` and `otap-ring`: the frozen record, hugetlbfs ring files, re-attach and rescan, slot laps, `req_id` dedupe.
2. `otap-index` and the cut: per-volume `BTreeMap`, read-your-writes, sector masks, aggregate seq, epoch-flip cut, ZFS-shaped throttle.
3. `otap-cp` on real media: a 12-drive md RAID6 (HDD) and a 4-drive md RAID5 (NVMe) behind one `Backstore` trait and two `MediaProfile`s; full-stripe `writev` from ring slots, one flush, FUA superblock stripe A/B, a flat per-CP map image (the CoW tree is Phase 2) so recovery is real in Phase 1.
4. TCP mirror plus standby index on the partner (Fabrics owns the transport; I own what the receiver indexes), honest WCE=1 semantics through LIO iSCSI over ublk, rollback detection and alerting.
5. The checker: client ack history versus recovered image, run under `dm-log-writes` replay points, QEMU `kill -9` power cuts, 50 real PDU pulls, `dbladed` `kill -9` under load.

##### Success criteria
- Recovery: after 1,000 simulated and 50 real dual power cuts, the recovered aggregate equals a seq-prefix of acked writes, that prefix is at least the last committed CP, zero acked writes newer than the cut survive, and the ROLLBACK alert fires with the right `cp_seq` and window in 100% of trials; XFS over the LUN mounts clean at every `dm-log-writes` cut point.
- Daemon crash is not failover: 50 `dbladed` kills under 1 GB/s load re-attach and resume within 2 s with zero lost acks and zero duplicate applies through ublk REISSUE.
- HDD drain measured, replacing every estimate: ≥ 1.2 GB/s pre-GC on 12 x 16 TB CMR with zero member reads during a CP; ≥ 0.8 GB/s with the pool aged to 80% full under random overwrite.
- 64 GB box, 8 GiB rings: 1.0 GB/s 4 KiB random ingest sustained 10 minutes with CPs every ≤ 4 s, throttle delays never above 2 s, zero initiator errors on ESXi 8, Windows Server 2022 and Linux at 2x drain offered load.
- Flash: ≥ 2 GB/s and ≥ 300 K 4 KiB mirrored write IOPS within 6 cores on 4 NVMe md RAID5; discard verified to reach the drives.
- Latency (Fabrics' numbers, measured through my path): D-Blade ack p50 ≤ 60 us, p99 ≤ 200 us on 10GbE TCP; client 4 KiB QD1 through LIO and ublk p50 ≤ 200 us.
- Memory: daemon RSS fixed at start and unchanged after 24 h; boot-to-serving ≤ 10 s for a 50 TB aggregate.
- Decision inputs delivered: the shelf spike (MD1200 class, two hosts, PR preempt timing, assemble time) and the plex-by-reference manifest size measured at 1 GB/s.

#### Open questions
- **Media-dependent HA accepted?** Shelf for HDD, plex-by-reference for flash, manual for the rest. Unblocks the Phase 2 allocator (deterministic replicated CP or not) and the lab purchase.
- **DRAM floor.** Is 32 GB per node (4 GiB rings, CP every 2 s at 1 GB/s) a supported configuration or is 64 GB the minimum? Unblocks the sizing rule and the throttle defaults.
- **Unmirrored policy default.** `placed` (lose a window on a survivor crash while the partner is down) or `cp` (fsync costs a CP while degraded)? Unblocks the flush semantics document.
- **Flash metadata class on HDD pools.** May a pool depend on a 2-SSD RAID1 for its map (loss = pool loss), or must every pool be single-tier redundant? Unblocks the hybrid design and the shelf drive mix.
- **Kernel posture.** Trixie 6.12 stock only, or is trixie-backports (6.16+, ublk zero-copy) allowed in the product? Unblocks the ingest path choice and the CPU budget.
- **Block size exposure.** 512e only (ESXi compatibility) or also 4Kn LUNs for Linux and NVMe-oF hosts? Unblocks the patch-record design priority.
- **Capacity honesty.** Is "usable = 0.9 x physical (0.45 x with plex)" acceptable marketing for a homelab product? Unblocks the reserve constants.
- **Consumer NVMe without PLP.** Supported with a warning, or refused at pool creation? Unblocks the installer probes.

#### Load-bearing claims
- [STORAGE-1] (high) Debian 12 bookworm's stock kernel (linux 6.1.176-1) has no CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK entry in its config and ships no ublk_drv.ko in any linux-image package, while Debian trixie (6.12.94 and 6.12.107) and bookworm-backports (6.12.43 to 6.12.95) ship ublk_drv.ko.xz.
  - why it matters: The whole N-to-D path (ublk under LIO/nvmet) and the Debian baseline recommendation (trixie or bookworm-backports, not stock bookworm) depend on it.
- [STORAGE-2] (high) Debian trixie's 6.12.107 kernel config sets CONFIG_TCM_QLA2XXX=m, CONFIG_TCM_USER2=m, CONFIG_SMB_SERVER=m, CONFIG_NVME_TARGET_FC/TCP/RDMA/FCLOOP=m, CONFIG_MD_CLUSTER=m, CONFIG_DM_LOG_WRITES=m, CONFIG_HUGETLBFS=y and CONFIG_SOFT_WATCHDOG=m, and bookworm's 6.1.176 config matches except that NVME_TARGET_FCLOOP is not set.
  - why it matters: The FC ring-as-LUN idea, the hugetlbfs ring, the dm-log-writes crash harness and the in-box target story all assume these modules exist on Debian without rebuilding the kernel.
- [STORAGE-3] (high) md raid456 refuses DISCARD on RAID4/5/6 arrays unless the module parameter raid456.devices_handle_discard_safely=Y is set, which is only safe when every member reliably returns zeroes after discard.
  - why it matters: The all-flash recommendation (md RAID5 with guest UNMAP passthrough and whole-segment discards) requires the installer to set this parameter and verify RZAT/DRAT; otherwise UNAP is silently dropped and the SSDs fill.
- [STORAGE-4] (high) hugetlbfs implements no write(2) path (its contents change only through mmap, truncate and hole punch), so a kernel target such as LIO fileio can land data only in a tmpfs-backed ring, not a hugetlbfs-backed one, while both can be mlocked and registered with io_uring and RDMA.
  - why it matters: Decides that the daemon's own ring is hugetlbfs and the FC/iSCSI ring LUN is tmpfs with swap off; if hugetlbfs accepted writes one file type would serve both.
- [STORAGE-5] (medium) io_uring coalesces registered buffers backed by huge pages into one bvec per huge page (since roughly 6.3) and IORING_REGISTER_CLONE_BUFFERS (6.12) lets several rings share one buffer registration.
  - why it matters: The thread-per-core design registers a 16 GiB arena once and clones it into the ingest, CP and transport rings; without coalescing and cloning, registration cost and per-ring duplication would force a staging-copy design.
- [STORAGE-6] (high) ESXi releases before 9.0 do not natively support 4Kn storage devices, so OpenTAP block LUNs for ESXi must be 512e (512-byte logical, 4 KiB physical) and sub-4 KiB writes must be merged by the D-Blade.
  - why it matters: Justifies the patch-record (sector-mask) path in the index and the CP-time old-block read, plus the 'unaligned write' counter and read-cache pinning.
- [STORAGE-7] (medium) A 12-drive md RAID6 of modern 16 to 24 TB CMR drives absorbs 1.2 to 2 GB/s of full-stripe log writes before garbage collection and 0.7 to 1.0 GB/s at 80% full, while a 7.2K drive delivers 80 random IOPS at QD1 and 150 to 210 at QD32.
  - why it matters: Sets the CP duration, the ring/2 interval arithmetic, the claim that 10GbE pools are link-bound and 25GbE pools drain-bound, and the mandatory read cache.
- [STORAGE-8] (high) libublk 0.4.8 (ublk-org, published 2026-08-29) and crc-fast 1.10 (SIMD CRC32C/CRC64) are current, actively maintained crates on crates.io.
  - why it matters: The nbladed ublk server and the per-4 KiB CRC in the ingest path are built on them; if either were abandoned, the ublk control protocol and CRC kernels would have to be written in-house.

### Opus-Protocols

#### Executive take
Build the N-Blade as a control-plane program, not a data path. On Debian trixie (6.12) the block front end is in-kernel LIO (iSCSI, tcm_qla2xxx) and nvmet (TCP, RDMA) over one ublk device per LUN, with the ublk queue handlers living inside the D-Blade process, so the "unified internal RPC" is a trait call with zero hops and one copy in Phase 1; file and object are XFS-on-LUN inside a per-SVM network+UTS namespace served by knfsd, Samba 4.22 and versitygw, so one primitive (move the namespace with its home aggregate) is the whole NAS failover story. The single biggest risk is file-protocol takeover semantics: NFSv4 grace and Samba's durable-handle rules turn a 10 s block takeover into a 20-90 s NAS stall, and no Rust fixes that; Phase 1 must measure and shorten it (lease 10 s, grace 20 s, nfsdcld on the SVM root volume, fsid pinning). What I disagree with most: "Debian 12+" is not viable as written, because bookworm's stock 6.1 kernel config does not build ublk at all (bookworm-backports 6.12 or trixie is the floor), and the brief's native WAFL-like inode layer plus a per-I/O RPC layer are ONTAP-shaped costs (10+ engineer-years, an extra hop and copy) that buy a homelab nothing XFS plus block snapshots cannot give in Phases 1-2. Second disagreement: "durable handles plus kernel oplocks and kernel share modes for cross-protocol correctness" cannot coexist on one Samba share; shares must be declared SMB-only or multiprotocol, which is ONTAP's security-style compromise and should be designed as such from day one.

#### Proposed libraries and subsystems
##### Kernel (trixie linux 6.12.107-1 config verified; bookworm-backports 6.12.95-1~bpo12+1 carries the same config)
- **LIO**: `CONFIG_TARGET_CORE/ISCSI_TARGET/TCM_QLA2XXX/TCM_FC/LOOPBACK_TARGET=m`. Mature, configfs-driven; qla2xxx needs `qlini_mode=dual` only if a port must also initiate (FC mirror). Also `=m` in bookworm 6.1.
- **nvmet**: `NVME_TARGET=m` with `TCP/RDMA/FC/FCLOOP/LOOP=m`, `NVME_TARGET_AUTH=y` (DH-HMAC-CHAP), `NVME_TARGET_TCP_TLS=y` (needs tlshd). Bookworm 6.1: TCP/RDMA/FC only, no AUTH, no LOOP/FCLOOP (no local test loop). Reservations arrive in 6.13 (opt-in `resv_enable`, not persistent), so trixie has none.
- **ublk**: `CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK=m` in trixie and bpo; absent from every bookworm 6.1 fragment. 6.12 has USER_RECOVERY, USER_RECOVERY_REISSUE (6.1) and USER_COPY (6.4); it lacks USER_RECOVERY_FAIL_IO (6.13), zero-copy buffer registration (6.15), AUTO_BUF_REG and PER_IO_DAEMON (6.16).
- **TCM_USER2=m**: fallback only; no initiator identity, one ring per device.
- **knfsd**: `NFSD=m, NFSD_V4=y, BLOCKLAYOUT/SCSILAYOUT=y`; per-network-namespace instances; early grace lift exists since 2019 but a 2026 patch series says duplicate reclaim entries defeat it (grace runs the full timer). Verify on 6.12.
- **ksmbd**: `SMB_SERVER=m`; stable since 6.6, durable handles since 6.9; Phase 2 experiment only (no vfs_fruit, no shadow_copy2 equivalent; Apple extensions unverified).
- **Watchdogs**: `SOFT_WATCHDOG=m`, `IPMI_WATCHDOG=m`, iTCO on Intel boards. `MD_CLUSTER=m` present, unused.

##### Userspace (trixie versions)
- **Samba 2:4.22.11** (smbd, winbindd; vfs_acl_xattr, streams_xattr, fruit, shadow_copy2): mature. Verified man-page rules: `smb2 leases` needs `kernel oplocks = no`; `durable handles` needs `kernel oplocks = no`, `kernel share modes = no`, `posix locking = no`; `kernel share modes` needs a filesystem VFS module XFS does not have.
- **nfs-utils 1:2.8.3**: `rpc.nfsd --scope`, `nfsdcld -p pipefsdir -s storagedir`, rpc.mountd, rpc.gssd. Bookworm's 2.6.2 has no `--scope`.
- **targetcli-fb 2.1.53 / python3-rtslib-fb 2.1.76**: only a saveconfig-path patch, so the qla2xxx fabric is exposed. Debugging and `saveconfig` diffs only; the runtime applier writes configfs directly.
- **versitygw v1.8.0** (Go, Apache-2.0, Sep 2026, monthly releases): POSIX backend with object lock (needs its object-lock mode option, verify flag name), versioning (`--versioning-dir`), multipart, bucket policies, IAM (dir, LDAP, Vault, FreeIPA, S3, standalone), TLS, events; no lifecycle, no object ACLs, SigV4 only. Not packaged; ship the static binary.
- **ktls-utils 1.0.0** (tlshd): NVMe/TCP TLS, Phase 2. **nfs-ganesha 6.5**: only relevant to a native-FS FSAL path; not recommended. nvme-cli, multipath-tools (shipped multipath.conf), open-iscsi (test initiator).

##### Rust crates
- **libublk 0.4.8** (ublk-org, the ublk kernel maintainer; Aug 2026; async/await, `io_threads_per_queue` needs 6.16; 0.4.7 was yanked, so expect API churn): the ublk front door.
- **io-uring 0.7**: stable. **tokio** (control), **zbus** (systemd D-Bus for per-SVM units), **rtnetlink** (LIFs, netns), **nix/rustix** (unshare, setns, mount), **inotify** (APTPL file), **rustls/tokio-rustls + axum** (mTLS management API), **crc32c**, `nft -f` via process (nftables-rs is immature).
- **s3s 0.17.0** (Sep 2026, active, 1.2M downloads): Phase 3 option for a native gateway; s3s-fs is a demo, not a backend.
- Not used: nfsserve (NFSv3 only), fuser (FUSE cannot grant F_SETLEASE leases, so no delegations or kernel oplocks), any Rust iSCSI or NVMe-oF target (none is production-grade).

#### Proposed architecture
##### Components per node
- **nbladed** (control plane, no I/O): reconciles Raft-derived desired state into kernel and daemon state. Modules: `blade_lio`, `blade_nvmet`, `svm` (netns + utsns, LIFs, mounts), `blade_nfs`, `blade_smb`, `blade_s3`, `identity` (AD join artifacts), `state_export` (APTPL, nfsdcld), `firewall`. Children run as systemd units in `opentap-svm-<name>.slice` with `NetworkNamespacePath=/run/netns/<svm>`, driven over D-Bus; we do not write a supervisor.
- **dbladed** (data plane): the engine plus the ublk front door: one `UblkDevice` per LUN (6.12: one queue thread per device, depth 128, max I/O 256 KiB), one io_uring per queue thread, USER_COPY `pwrite/pread` on `/dev/ublkcN` so the kernel copies request data straight into a ring slot.
- **Kernel**: LIO iblock over `/dev/ublkbN`; nvmet bdev namespace over the same; knfsd; XFS.
- **Per-SVM children**: rpc.nfsd, rpc.mountd, nfsdcld (and rpc.gssd when AD-joined); smbd + winbindd with `-s /vol/<svm>/.root/samba/smb.conf`; versitygw.

##### Data flow
Block: initiator -> LIO/nvmet -> bio -> ublk -> dbladed queue thread (busy-poll 50 us, then wait) -> `BlockService::submit` -> ring put + mirror -> ack -> ublk commit -> target completion. Reads: ring/index hit (copy) or SSD cache/HDD via registered buffers, then `pwrite` to `/dev/ublkcN`. File: client -> knfsd/smbd -> VFS -> XFS -> bio -> same path. Object: versitygw -> syscalls -> XFS -> same path. Every protocol ends in one block abstraction; the only custom data-path code is the ublk front door and the engine.

##### One device from two nodes
- **SCSI**: `wwn/vpd_unit_serial` = volume UUID (LIO derives NAA-6 from it); fixed vendor/product/revision; same IQN on both nodes, TPGT 1 on A and 2 on B; one ALUA `tg_pt_gp` per *aggregate* (group id = aggregate id) created on both nodes with only local ports linked; RTPI set explicitly (A: 1-99, B: 101-199; attribute since the 6.4 cycle); `emulate_write_cache=1` (volatile cache is the truth), `emulate_tpu/tpws/3pc/caw=1`, `alua_support=1`, `pgr_support=1`; CHAP in TPG `auth`. States per ownership: owner Active/Optimized, partner Standby, Transitioning during takeover.
- **NVMe**: identical NQN, `attr_serial`, `attr_model`; namespace UUID/NGUID = volume UUID; disjoint `cntlid_min/max` (A 1-32767, B 32768-65519); ANA group id = aggregate id, every port publishes both groups and sets its own states; `allowed_hosts` and `dhchap_key` from Raft secrets.
- **NAS**: SVM name = uts nodename = NFS server scope = NetBIOS/AD computer name; `fsid` = hash(volume UUID); the SVM root volume holds nfsdcld's db, Samba's private/state/lock/cache dirs, the keytab, TLS certs and versitygw's IAM dir, so the survivor restarts the same server identity.

##### Memory structures (per LUN, 6.12)
ublk queue depth 128 with no per-tag buffers (USER_COPY); per-queue SQ/CQ of 256; `pending: [Option<InFlight>; 128]` by tag {slot, req_id, op, started_ns}; the `CompletionGate` shared with the engine. NAS page cache stays the kernel's, capped by `vm.dirty_bytes`.

##### Trait sketch
```rust
// dbladed: the "unified internal RPC" is a trait call; a network impl can come later.
pub struct IoCtx { pub lun: LunId, pub aggregate: AggregateId, pub owner_epoch: u64, pub req_id: u64 }
pub enum BlockOp<'a> {
    Write { lba: u64, slot: RingSlot, len: u32, fua: bool }, // payload already in the ring (ublk USER_COPY)
    Read { lba: u64, buf: &'a mut [u8] },
    Flush,                       // completes when every earlier write of this LUN is mirror-acked
    Discard { lba: u64, len: u32 },
    WriteZeroes { lba: u64, len: u32 },
}
pub enum BlockErr { StaleEpoch, NotOwner, Fenced, Busy /* Stalled > 2 s */, Media }
pub trait BlockService: Send + Sync {
    fn reserve_slot(&self, lun: LunId, len: u32) -> Result<RingSlot, BlockErr>;
    fn submit(&self, ctx: IoCtx, op: BlockOp<'_>) -> impl Future<Output = Result<(), BlockErr>> + Send;
    fn gate(&self) -> &CompletionGate;   // checked on every ublk completion; no syscalls
}
pub struct CompletionGate { pub lease_deadline_ns: AtomicU64, pub owner_epoch: AtomicU64 }

// nbladed: all of this is derived from Raft state and reconciled idempotently.
pub struct Svm { pub id: SvmId, pub name: String, /* uts nodename = NFS scope = NetBIOS name */
    pub home: AggregateId, pub root_vol: VolId, pub lifs: Vec<Lif>,
    pub nfs: Option<NfsCfg>, pub smb: Option<SmbCfg>, pub s3: Option<S3Cfg> }
pub enum AccessState { ActiveOptimized, Transitioning, Standby, Unavailable }
pub trait ProtocolBlade {
    fn kind(&self) -> BladeKind;                       // Iscsi, FcScsi, NvmeTcp, NvmeRdma, Nfs, Smb, S3
    fn apply(&mut self, desired: &BladeSpec) -> Result<(), ApplyErr>;   // configfs / files / systemd
    fn set_access(&mut self, agg: AggregateId, st: AccessState) -> Result<(), ApplyErr>; // ALUA/ANA or LIF up/down
    fn fence(&mut self) -> Result<(), ApplyErr>;       // fail closed in < 50 ms; never waits for I/O
    fn exported_state(&self) -> Vec<StateBlob>;        // APTPL PR metadata, nfsdcld db, Samba secrets.tdb
}
```

##### Takeover sequence on the survivor
Raft commits ownership(agg, epoch+1) -> engine replays the residual ring and ADD_DEVs the LUNs -> ALUA/ANA groups for that aggregate flip Standby -> Transitioning -> Active/Optimized (block done, target 10 s) -> mount XFS volumes (log replay), create netns+utsns, raise LIFs with gratuitous ARP, start nfsd (`--scope <svm> -L 10 -G 20`), mountd, nfsdcld, smbd/winbindd, versitygw (+3-6 s).

##### Stateless, per protocol (what survives only as Raft config, volume contents, or client recovery)
- iSCSI/FC: I_T nexus and CHAP sessions re-login; PRs survive via APTPL shipping; ALUA state is Raft-derived.
- NVMe: controllers are per node; hosts reconnect; no reservations on 6.12.
- NFSv4: client ids, opens, locks, delegations recovered by the client in grace from the nfsdcld db on the SVM root.
- SMB: sessions and handles are lost; machine account, idmap (deterministic `idmap_rid`) and share config live on the SVM root.
- S3: no session state; multipart parts and lock/version metadata are files and xattrs on the volume.

##### Security posture
Mutual CHAP per TPG; DH-HMAC-CHAP on nvmet (6.12), TLS PSK later; NFS `sec=sys` with export ACLs, `krb5p` when the SVM is AD-joined (`nfs/<svm>` SPN in the SVM keytab); SMB `server min protocol = SMB3_00`, `smb encrypt = desired`, signing on; S3 TLS only, SigV4; management API (axum, rustls, mTLS) bound to the management VLAN; ControlLink mTLS; per-netns nftables default-deny with a per-LIF allowlist.

#### Bottlenecks
##### 4 KiB write latency budgets (10GbE, busy-polled TCP mirror 25-35 us; estimates to be measured)
| Path | Without mirror | With mirror |
|---|---|---|
| iSCSI (LIO, immediate data): wire+LIO 50-70, ublk hop 5-15 polled / 20-40 sleeping, copy 0.5, completion 15-25 | 70-100 us | 100-150 us |
| NVMe/TCP | 40-60 us | 80-120 us |
| FC 8G QLE2562 via tcm_qla2xxx (two fabric round trips + firmware) | 100-150 us | 140-200 us |
