| NFSv4.1 sync write (RPC 80-120 + XFS data and log = two serialized block writes of 10 + 30 each) | 200-300 us | 300-450 us |
| SMB3 sync write (+ smbd) | 250-350 us | 350-500 us |
| S3 small PUT (HTTP, TLS, open, fsync, rename) | 1-3 ms | 1-3 ms |
Async NFS and SMB writes batch to line rate; the mirror RTT is the floor for every sync path, so the mirror link, not the protocol stack, sets p50.

##### Copies and CPU at 10GbE line rate (1.1 GB/s of writes)
- iSCSI: skb -> sg pages (kernel), ublk USER_COPY -> ring slot, mirror TCP send (unless SEND_ZC from the pinned ring), CP flush by DMA from the pinned ring: 3 copies x 1.1 GB/s = 3.3 GB/s of memcpy at 7-9 GB/s per core = 0.4-0.5 core, plus hardware CRC32C of 1.1 GB/s = 0.1 core. iSCSI data digest on costs about one more core (software CRC in LIO); ship it off.
- FC: HBA DMAs into sg pages, so 2 copies.
- NFS: skb -> page cache, writeback bios -> ring, mirror: 3 copies, and knfsd needs 2-3 cores at line rate (experience; verify).
- SMB: 4 copies (socket -> smbd buffer -> page cache -> ring -> mirror); smbd is one process per client at 0.6-0.9 GB/s per core; multichannel helps a single client.
- S3: TLS + MD5/SHA256 at 1-2 GB/s per core + file write: about 2 cores per GB/s.
- 6.15/6.16 zero-copy only removes the read-miss copy (disk -> request pages) and lets the mirror send from kernel bvecs; the ring copy stays because the ring is the durability domain. Net saving about 0.15 core per GB/s: not worth waiting for.

##### CPU and memory on a homelab box with iSCSI, NFS, SMB and S3 live
Cores: mirror poll 1 (0.3 adaptive), ublk handlers 1-2, engine CP/allocator 1-2, LIO/nvmet softirq and kworkers 1, knfsd 1-2, smbd 0.5-1 per active client, versitygw 1-2 during backups, core 0 reserved for the OS: 8C/16T minimum, 12C comfortable. Memory: mirror ring 8 GB own + 8 GB partner inbound (CP forced at half-full, so CP <= 4 s at 1 GB/s; back-to-back CPs are steady state), dirty index 8 GB / 4 KiB x 96 B = 200 MB, read-cache index at 64 KiB extents for 1 TB = 0.5 GB, NAS page cache 8-16 GB, smbd 30 MB per connection, versitygw 0.3-1 GB: 64 GB floor, 128 GB comfortable.

##### Queue depth and absorption
16 LUNs x depth 128 = 2048 outstanding; filling 10GbE at 30 us RTT needs only 33 KB in flight, so latency, not bandwidth, drives the mirror design. A 12-drive RAID6 absorbs 1.4-2 GB/s before GC: one 10GbE client cannot overrun it, two 25GbE clients can, so the Stalled -> BUSY path is a real code path for NAS too (knfsd and smbd then stall threads, not clients, so thread counts must exceed outstanding ops).

##### Takeover windows the clients actually see
- iSCSI/FC: ALUA flip, 10 s p99 target; ESXi's 35 s only when the dying node goes silent, which the fence order (gate, Standby flip, ublk delete) avoids on a node that still runs.
- NVMe: ANA, immediate after reconnect.
- NFSv4.1: reconnect + EXCHANGE_ID + reclaim; default grace 90 s blocks new opens; set grace 20 s and lease 10 s; early lift may not fire on 6.12.
- SMB: sessions reset, handles lost; Windows and macOS re-establish sessions in 10-60 s, in-flight writes fail and Explorer or Finder prompts to retry; Time Machine restarts its sparsebundle check.
- S3: connections reset; Veeam retries; multipart parts on the volume survive.

##### The two-block-layer tax for file
XFS over ublk adds one queue hop (about 10 us) and gives the engine XFS's pattern (4 KiB metadata randomness, log appends). The log-structured engine absorbs the writes; the real cost is metadata read amplification on HDD, so XFS metadata must sit in the SSD read cache (1-3% of data). Online `-o discard` turns every freed extent into a trim RPC; use weekly fstrim.

#### Creative solutions
##### SVM = network namespace + UTS namespace
Running each SVM's nfsd, mountd, nfsdcld, smbd/winbindd and versitygw inside `unshare -n -u` with the SVM name as nodename makes knfsd's NFSv4 server scope and clientid major id the SVM name on whichever node hosts it, gives Samba a NetBIOS identity, isolates ports and firewall rules, and makes "LIF migration" one primitive for all three NAS protocols: move the namespace with its aggregate. No floating-IP scripts per protocol.

##### ublk front door inside dbladed, USER_COPY into the ring
A separate ublk-server process costs a shared-memory hop and a wakeup (3-10 us) and doubles REISSUE duplicates across two failure domains. In-process, the kernel's one unavoidable copy lands directly in the ring slot, which is where the payload must live anyway; the `BlockService` trait keeps the network implementation possible without touching the engine.

##### Previous Versions and .snapshot for free
fsfreeze (milliseconds) -> D-Blade snapshot -> unfreeze; mount snapshots `ro,nouuid,norecovery` under `.snapshot`; `vfs_shadow_copy2` exposes them as Windows Previous Versions. Single-file restore and file clones come from `cp --reflink` across the snapshot mount. This is most of what the native inode layer was supposed to buy.

##### ALUA/ANA group = aggregate, published by both nodes
Both nodes configure both groups; takeover flips two group states per aggregate instead of rewriting per-LUN config. The target configuration is byte-identical on both nodes and can be diffed from configfs dumps as a health check.

##### Fail-closed completion gate
A shared `lease_deadline_ns` + `owner_epoch` checked on every ublk completion is the safety signal; the ALUA flip is the courtesy signal. Fence order: gate closes -> ALUA Standby / ANA inaccessible (milliseconds; LIO then answers NOT READY instead of hanging) -> `DEL_DEV_ASYNC`. On 6.13+ `USER_RECOVERY_FAIL_IO` replaces the delete.

##### Ship SCSI-3 PRs across takeover
inotify on LIO's APTPL file (`$dbroot/pr/aptpl_<serial>`) -> Raft log entry -> survivor writes `res_aptpl_metadata` before going Active/Optimized. WSFC and Hyper-V keep their reservations through takeover, the one piece of SCSI state LIO can export. (Verify that re-applying through `res_aptpl_metadata` is the supported path.)

##### FC mirror receive as LIO fileio on tmpfs
The partner exposes the ring as a fileio LUN on tmpfs (`huge=always`, mlocked); SCSI status means "in partner DRAM", exactly the receiver-optional ring semantics. hugetlbfs cannot be written with write(2), so tmpfs it is.

##### Share classes instead of a new filesystem
Per share: `smb-only` (durable handles, SMB2 leases, `posix locking = no`) or `multiprotocol` (`posix locking = yes`, `kernel oplocks = yes`, no leases, no durable handles). Honest, documented, ONTAP-shaped.

##### systemd as the supervisor
Per-SVM slices with `CPUWeight`, `MemoryMax`, `CPUAffinity` (core 0 free) and `NetworkNamespacePath`; nbladed talks D-Bus via zbus. Budgeting protocols against each other becomes cgroup policy, not code.

#### Holes in the plan
##### 1. Debian 12's stock kernel cannot run the design
Bookworm's 6.1.176-1 config has no `CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK` in any fragment, no `NVME_TARGET_AUTH`, no `NVME_TARGET_LOOP/FCLOOP`, and its nfs-utils has no `--scope`. "Debian 12+" must mean "bookworm with linux-image-6.12 from bookworm-backports, or trixie". State it and test only 6.12.

##### 2. The per-I/O "unified RPC layer" is a tax with no Phase 1 payoff
In a 2-node pair every LUN moves with its aggregate, so no indirect N-to-D path exists to justify a hop. Keep the trait, drop the wire format until a third node exists.

##### 3. Durable handles and cross-protocol correctness are mutually exclusive in Samba
Verified in smb.conf(5): durable handles require `posix locking = no`, `kernel oplocks = no`, `kernel share modes = no`; SMB2 leases require `kernel oplocks = no`; kernel share modes need a VFS module XFS lacks (LOCK_MAND left the kernel), so SMB deny modes are never enforced against NFS. The brief's share spec is unimplementable as one share. Design the two share classes and say that ONTAP-style cross-protocol share modes are out of scope.

##### 4. NFSv4 grace can erase the 10-15 s takeover budget
Default grace is 90 s; the early-lift path has a 2026-reported bug on existing kernels. Mitigation: `-L 10 -G 20`, nfsdcld db on the SVM root volume so the survivor knows the client list, NFSv4-only exports by default (NFSv3 adds NLM/statd notify state). Measure on 6.12 before promising numbers.

##### 5. One MirrorTransport trait cannot hide SCSI error handling
FC as a mirror link lives under the SCSI midlayer: default command timeout 30 s, `dev_loss_tmo` 30 s, abort and reset escalation that stalls the HBA, and no way for the receiver to reject a write except by changing LUN state. The 1 s link-dead timer is reachable only with per-command timeouts around 500 ms (bsg/SG_IO) and `fast_io_fail_tmo=1`. The trait needs capability bits (`receiver_can_reject`, `one_sided`, `ack_latency_class`) and the CP sizing must follow the transport; it cannot be one set of numbers.

##### 6. Volatile DRAM changes FLUSH, and GrantUnmirrored quietly removes durability
The ublk device and LIO (`emulate_write_cache=1`), nvmet (VWC) must advertise a volatile cache so XFS and ESXi send FLUSH; FLUSH then means "all earlier writes mirror-acked", which is cheap. But under GrantUnmirrored the mirror is gone and DRAM has no battery: a single node's power loss loses up to a CP of acknowledged writes, which is a different promise from "simultaneous site loss". Add a degraded-durability policy: CP interval 1-2 s and FLUSH forces a CP (sync latency rises to milliseconds), or pause I/O for paranoid users; alarm loudly either way.

##### 7. A hung node that still answers NOP-Out is worse than a dead one
If the ublk server stalls while LIO keeps the iSCSI session alive, ESXi waits for command timeouts (30 s class), not the 10 s budget. Hence the fence order in creative solutions, and the kernel targets must be flipped to Standby by the same code path that closes the gate; on 6.12 the only hard stop is deleting the ublk device.

##### 8. Node-local disks make Standby paths a lie
With no shared shelf, a Standby port can never become Active; presenting it makes initiators wait on a path that will not come. In that mode present single-node targets and no ALUA partner, and call failover "manual" honestly. Phase 1 should be the shared SAS shelf (MD1200/MD1220 with dual EMMs, two 9300-8e), the only configuration where block takeover under 15 s is real; plex replication halves ingest and belongs in Phase 3.

##### 9. Thin LUNs under XFS turn aggregate-full into filesystem shutdown
XFS cannot see aggregate space; ENOSPC surfaces as write errors and an XFS shutdown. File volumes must be thick-reserved by default; thin only for block LUNs with alerts at 80 and 90 percent.

##### 10. VAAI and ODX expectations
LIO's XCOPY is LID1 only, 0xE4 target and 0x02 segment descriptors, both LUNs mapped to the same session on the same node; cross-aggregate clones return COPY_ABORTED and ESXi falls back to software copy. There is no ODX, so Windows server-side copies do not exist. ATS, UNMAP and WRITE SAME work. Document rather than promise.

##### 11. Windows needs iSCSI for a long time
Windows has no shipping NVMe-oF initiator; the Insider build's initiator has no multipathing. NVMe/TCP is for ESXi 8, Proxmox and Linux only; keep iSCSI first-class.

##### 12. Ethernet-only quorum strands FC clients
Both nodes losing Ethernet with FC alive fences both nodes although FC clients are fine. Either accept it, or carry ControlLink over a direct second NIC, which the consensus design must size.

##### 13. Busy-polling eats a homelab's cores
Two permanent poll threads cost 2 of 8 cores. Poll 50 us then sleep; accept the p99 jitter and document it.

#### Phase 1 recommendation
##### Platform
Trixie (6.12.107+) or bookworm with linux-image-6.12 from backports; no 6.1 support. Lab: two nodes with a shared SAS shelf, 10GbE for mirror and clients, one QLE2562 pair for FC-SCSI client testing, ESXi 8, Proxmox, Windows 11/Server, macOS and a Veeam server as clients.

##### Build (in order)
1. **ublk front door in dbladed** (libublk 0.4.8, USER_RECOVERY|REISSUE, USER_COPY into ring slots, the CompletionGate). Null engine first.
2. **blade_lio**: direct configfs applier for iSCSI and tcm_qla2xxx; ALUA groups per aggregate, explicit RTPI, identical identity on both nodes; APTPL shipping; fence order implemented.
3. **blade_nvmet**: NVMe/TCP with DH-HMAC-CHAP, ANA group per aggregate, disjoint cntlid ranges.
4. **SVM namespaces**: knfsd per SVM (`--scope`, nfsdcld on the SVM root), Samba per SVM with both share classes, `vfs_shadow_copy2` from D-Blade snapshots, deterministic `idmap_rid`.
5. **versitygw per SVM** with object lock and versioning; zero custom S3 code.
6. **Shipped client recipes**: multipath.conf, ESXi claim rules (ATS, NMP SATP_ALUA), both portals in discovery, Windows MPIO ALUA settings.

##### Measurable success criteria
- ublk null round trip p50 <= 8 us polled, <= 30 us sleeping; per-GB/s copy cost measured with perf per path.
- iSCSI 4 KiB QD1 write over 10GbE with mirror: p50 <= 150 us, p99 <= 400 us; sequential writes >= 1.0 GB/s using <= 2 cores for LIO + ublk + engine front end.
- NVMe/TCP 4 KiB QD1 write p50 <= 120 us.
- Block takeover: I/O resumes <= 10 s p99 on ESXi, Linux dm-multipath and Windows MPIO with zero guest I/O errors over 100 forced takeovers; ALUA flip <= 50 ms; PR registrations intact after takeover.
- A configfs dump of both nodes differs only in local port addresses.
- NFSv4.1: Linux and ESXi datastores resume <= 20 s with locks reclaimed and zero application errors; grace lift behaviour on 6.12 documented.
- SMB: sessions re-established <= 20 s on Windows and macOS; the documented in-flight-write failure on smb-only shares reproduced and described; Previous Versions visible.
- S3: Veeam immutable repository passes backup, restore and immutability-tamper tests; a takeover during a backup job completes without a failed job.
- CPU and memory budget table validated on an 8-core, 64 GB box with all four protocols loaded at once.

#### Open questions
- **Shared SAS shelf or node-local disks for the owner's lab?** Decides whether block ALUA takeover is real in Phase 1 or whether Phase 1 ships single-node targets with manual failover.
- **Is a Go child process (versitygw) acceptable inside the "unified daemon"?** Decides S3 build-versus-reuse: zero custom code now versus 1-1.5 engineer-years on s3s for a Veeam-grade native gateway in Phase 3.
- **Kernel floor: trixie 6.12 only, with trixie-backports (6.16+) for zero-copy and per-I/O daemons later?** Decides whether bookworm+backports stays in the support matrix at all.
- **Which shares need simultaneous NFS and SMB access?** If none, every share defaults to smb-only (durable handles, leases) and the multiprotocol class becomes opt-in.
- **Is AD assumed, or standalone workgroup with local users?** Decides winbind `rid` versus local tdb, and whether Kerberos NFS and per-SVM machine accounts are Phase 1.
- **Is FC wanted as a mirror transport in Phase 1, or only as a client fabric?** Decides whether the MirrorTransport trait must carry SCSI-timeout capability bits now.
- **Under GrantUnmirrored, keep serving with zero durability, force 1-2 s CPs, or pause?** Decides the FLUSH semantics the blades advertise in degraded mode.
- **Do NAS LIFs move only with their home aggregate, or also on link failure without a takeover?** Decides whether LACP per node is mandatory and whether a LIF can live on the non-owner node (which would reintroduce the network RPC).

#### Load-bearing claims
- [PROTOCOLS-1] (high) Debian trixie's linux 6.12.107-1 config sets CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK=m, CONFIG_TCM_QLA2XXX=m, CONFIG_TCM_USER2=m, CONFIG_NVME_TARGET_{TCP,RDMA,FC,FCLOOP,LOOP}=m, CONFIG_NVME_TARGET_AUTH=y, CONFIG_NVME_TARGET_TCP_TLS=y, CONFIG_SMB_SERVER=m, CONFIG_NFSD_V4=y and CONFIG_MD_CLUSTER=m.
  - why it matters: The entire block design (ublk front door, FC via tcm_qla2xxx, NVMe/TCP with DH-HMAC-CHAP) assumes these are in-box on trixie and bookworm-backports.
- [PROTOCOLS-2] (medium) Debian bookworm's linux 6.1.176-1 config contains no CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK entry in debian/config/config, kernelarch-x86/config or amd64/config, so the stock Debian 12 kernel ships no ublk driver, while bookworm-backports provides linux-image-amd64 6.12.95-1~bpo12+1.
  - why it matters: Sets the kernel floor at 6.12 and removes 'Debian 12 stock' from the support matrix.
- [PROTOCOLS-3] (high) Samba 4.22 grants SMB2 durable handles only when kernel oplocks, kernel share modes and posix locking are all off, negotiates SMB2 leases only with kernel oplocks off, and kernel share modes require a filesystem-specific VFS module that XFS does not provide.
  - why it matters: Forces the two-share-class design and makes ONTAP-style cross-protocol share modes out of scope.
- [PROTOCOLS-4] (high) LIO's EXTENDED COPY implements only LID1 with 0xE4 target and 0x02 segment descriptors between devices mapped to the same session on the same node, and has no ODX (POPULATE TOKEN / WRITE USING TOKEN) support.
  - why it matters: Defines what VAAI and Windows offload OpenTAP can promise; cross-aggregate clones fall back to host copies.
- [PROTOCOLS-5] (medium) ublk gained USER_RECOVERY and USER_RECOVERY_REISSUE in 6.1, USER_COPY in 6.4, USER_RECOVERY_FAIL_IO in 6.13, zero-copy buffer registration in 6.15 and AUTO_BUF_REG plus PER_IO_DAEMON in 6.16, so trixie's 6.12 has recovery and user copy but no fail-io flag and no zero copy.
  - why it matters: Determines the copy count on the Phase 1 write path and the fence mechanism (device delete rather than FAIL_IO) on 6.12.
- [PROTOCOLS-6] (medium) nfs-utils 2.8.3 in trixie provides rpc.nfsd --scope, knfsd captures the NFSv4 server scope per network namespace from the starting process's utsname nodename at startup, and nfsdcld accepts -p pipefsdir and -s storagedir, so one knfsd instance per SVM namespace with a migratable scope and client database is possible.
  - why it matters: The SVM = netns + utsns design and NFSv4 lock reclaim after takeover depend on it.
- [PROTOCOLS-7] (high) versitygw v1.8.0 (Go, Apache-2.0) implements S3 object lock, bucket versioning (--versioning-dir), multipart uploads, bucket policies and several IAM backends on its POSIX backend but not lifecycle or object ACLs, whereas Garage has no released versioning or object lock and MinIO's community repository is archived (2026-04-25) and source-only.
  - why it matters: Selects versitygw as the Phase 1 S3 blade for Veeam immutability and rules out Garage and MinIO.
- [PROTOCOLS-8] (medium) Windows Server 2025 GA and Windows 11 have no inbox NVMe-oF initiator; an in-box NVMe/TCP and NVMe/RDMA initiator exists only in Windows Server Insider preview builds and lacks multipathing.
  - why it matters: Keeps iSCSI mandatory and first-class for Windows clients, with NVMe/TCP limited to ESXi, Proxmox and Linux.

### Opus-Consensus

#### Executive take
Build a lease-centred control plane, not a fence-centred one: openraft 0.9.25 on Node A, Node B and a non-electable Witness (`enable_elect=false`, a verified 0.9 feature) holding a sub-megabyte state machine; leases granted by the Raft leader through `ensure_linearizable()` (one quorum heartbeat round, zero log writes, so a microSD Raspberry Pi witness survives); every I/O checked lock-free against a seqlock epoch page at submission and again at completion; takeover as a Raft-committed, idempotent step plan that drives block flips and NAS migrations from the same executor so the control plane cannot race itself. Watchdog, BMC, PDU and SCSI-3 PR are media fences and accelerators; the safety proof rests on the lease arithmetic alone, and that proof holds with softdog in plex mode because the media is private. The single biggest risk is the shared-shelf path: late DMA from a wedged owner (md stripe-cache drain 1-2 s, SCSI timeouts up to 30 s) can land on segments the survivor just allocated, and softdog cannot bound that; the shelf therefore requires drive-level PREEMPT-AND-ABORT on SAS drives or a confirmed power fence, or the storage layer adopts the two-arena allocation protocol below. For the homelab default I recommend plex-by-log-replay (both nodes apply the mirrored NVLOG to their own array, geometry-independent, 50% capacity) and the shelf as the SAS-pro tier. What I most disagree with in the revised plan: (1) "replay as the maximal contiguous prefix" silently drops acknowledged writes unless client ACKs are cumulative per aggregate; replay all CRC-valid records instead; (2) the 8 s watchdog bound is not on the takeover critical path if the data path checks leases at completion; the real floor is lease TTL plus the leader-change wait, about 4.3 s, and that is where the 10 s p99 comes from; (3) lease renewals must not be Raft log entries.

#### Proposed libraries and subsystems
##### Consensus and control plane
- **openraft 0.9.25** (stable; 0.10 alpha): verified features we depend on: `Config.enable_elect` / `enable_heartbeat` toggles (witness never campaigns but votes and counts for quorum), `Raft::ensure_linearizable()` (leadership confirmed by a heartbeat round, no append), `Raft::trigger().elect()` to pull leadership back to a data node, joint-consensus membership. Storage traits must be implemented (about two weeks): log on **redb 2.x** (pure Rust, stable) with `fdatasync` per append, snapshot every 1,000 entries. Fallback: tikv raft-rs 0.7 (lightly maintained).
- **tokio** for the control daemon; the Raft RPC on **ControlLink** = TCP + mTLS (rustls) on every Ethernet path, plus a **ring mailbox** implementation (control frames in reserved slots of any MirrorRing: FC, RoCE, IB) so A-B keep quorum when the LAN switch dies.
- **Epoch page**: a 4 KiB `MAP_SHARED` seqlock page in a hugetlbfs/tmpfs file; `std::sync::atomic` only, no crate.

##### Fencing (Debian packages and kernel interfaces)
- Linux watchdog API: `/dev/watchdog0`, `WDIOC_SETTIMEOUT=5`, `WDIOC_KEEPALIVE` every 500 ms, `wdctl` to read identity. Drivers in order of trust: `ipmi_watchdog` (iDRAC6+, Supermicro, iLO with IPMI enabled), `hpwdt` (iLO), `iTCO_wdt` (Intel PCH, present on most consumer boards but often disabled by the BIOS NO_REBOOT bit), `sp5100_tco` (AMD), then `softdog`. softdog (verified in 6.12 source): hrtimer on CLOCK_MONOTONIC, expiry calls `emergency_restart()` or `panic()` with `soft_panic=1`, `soft_margin` up to 65535 s. Pair it with `nmi_watchdog=1 hardlockup_panic=1 softlockup_panic=1 panic=5 panic_on_oops=1 reboot=efi` (test `reboot=` per board). I believe Debian's linux-image-amd64 builds all of these as modules; verify `CONFIG_SOFT_WATCHDOG`, `CONFIG_IPMI_WATCHDOG`, `CONFIG_ITCO_WDT`, `CONFIG_SP5100_TCO`, `CONFIG_HP_WATCHDOG`.
- **fence-agents** (Debian: 4.12.1 in bookworm as one package; 4.16.0 in trixie split into `fence-agents-ipmilan`, `fence-agents-redfish`, `fence-agents-apc-snmp`, `fence-agents-all`): exec them with the standard stdin argument protocol rather than writing BMC drivers; `ipmitool` and `freeipmi-tools` for diagnostics. Shelly (Gen2 `Switch.Set` RPC) and Tasmota (`cm?cmnd=Power%20Off`) are a 30-line `reqwest` fence each. Every power fence must *confirm* (Redfish `PowerState=Off`, PDU outlet state) before it counts.
- **SCSI-3 PR**: `PERSISTENT RESERVE OUT / PREEMPT AND ABORT` via `SG_IO` (nix ioctl) or `sg_persist` (sg3-utils); SAS/NL-SAS drives only, which is also the shelf's existing SATA exclusion.

##### Kernel targets and NAS pieces the control plane drives
- **LIO configfs**: `alua/<tpg>/alua_access_state` (0 AO, 2 Transitioning, 3 Standby, 14 Offline), `trans_delay_msecs`, `implicit_trans_secs`, `wwn/vpd_unit_serial` (same NAA on both nodes), `pr/res_aptpl_metadata` (writable; the store refuses while fabric exports exist, so PR must be loaded before the LUN is exported) and `force_pr_aptpl=1` (writes PR metadata on every change; explicitly intended for active/passive HA). Verified from the LIO patch history; re-check the attribute names on 6.12.
- **nvmet configfs**: `ana_grpid`, `ana_state` (optimized/inaccessible/change).
- **nfsd**: `rpc.nfsd --scope <svm>` (verified; same scope on both nodes so NFSv4.1 clients reclaim after migration), `/proc/fs/nfsd/nfsv4leasetime` and `nfsv4gracetime` (grace is forced ≥ lease), `v4_end_grace`, `nfsdcld` client tracking (its SQLite DB lives on the SVM root volume). nfsd and `rpc.mountd` are per network namespace; one netns per SVM.
- **Samba** 4.17 (bookworm) / 4.22 (trixie, verify minor) with `durable handles` (same-smbd reconnect only), `private dir` and `state directory` on the SVM root volume; **ksmbd** durable handles v1/v2 since Linux 6.9 (trixie yes, bookworm no); neither replicates open-handle state across nodes.
- **S3**: the `s3s` crate (Rust S3 protocol frontend, moderately mature, Apache-2.0) as the gateway shell; bucket, multipart-upload and ACL tables on the SVM volume so uploads resume on the partner. MinIO is in maintenance mode and licence-constrained; Garage brings its own replication.

##### Testing
TLA+ with Apalache (protocol core), **stateright 0.30** (Rust actor model checker with linearizability checking), **madsim** (deterministic tokio, used by RisingWave) or **turmoil 0.6** (tokio-rs network sim), **loom** for the seqlock page and ring index, a 500-line Rust port of Porcupine's WGL checker for canary-LUN histories, `tc netem` for witness RTT injection.

#### Proposed architecture
##### Processes
- `otap-ctl` per node, pinned to one dedicated core, `mlockall`, `SCHED_FIFO`: openraft voter, lease client, fence executor, takeover-plan executor, epoch-page writer, watchdog petter, heartbeat sender on every path. Budget: 1 core, 200 MB, 30 msg/s.
- `otap-d` (D-Blade) and the protocol blades only *read* the epoch page; they never speak Raft.
- Witness: `otap-ctl --witness` (voter, `enable_elect=false`, no data paths). 1 vCPU, 512 MB, 2 GB disk, fsync rate ≈ 0 in steady state (log appends only on ownership, membership, config and PR changes). Runs on a Proxmox VM on a third box, a Raspberry Pi 4/5 (microSD is fine at this fsync rate), or a cloud VM. RTT budget: election timeout 1 s ≥ 10× RTT, lease renewal (500 ms period) costs one RTT only when a data node is down, takeover commit costs two; **RTT ≤ 100 ms with p99 jitter ≤ 200 ms is acceptable**. The witness must have a path independent of the mirror cable; it need not be independent of the LAN switch (A+B keep quorum over the ring mailbox).

##### Replicated state (openraft state machine, < 1 MB)
```rust
struct ClusterState {
  version: u64, ca_roots: Vec<Cert>, nodes: BTreeMap<NodeId, NodeInfo>,
  aggregates: BTreeMap<AggId, AggState>, svms: BTreeMap<SvmId, SvmState>,
  luns: BTreeMap<LunId, LunIdentity>,            // NAA/NGUID, tpg ids, APTPL text
}
struct AggState { owner: NodeId, home: NodeId, epoch: u64,
  mode: AggMode,                                 // Mirrored | Unmirrored{granted_epoch} | Takeover{step} | Giveback{step} | Down
  media: Media }                                 // Shelf{pr_keys:[u64;2]} | Plex{copies:[NodeId;2], synced:bool}
struct SvmState { home_agg: AggId, on_node: NodeId, lifs: Vec<Lif>,
  nfs: NfsCfg /*scope, lease 30 s, grace 45 s*/, smb: SmbCfg, s3: S3Cfg /*endpoints, tls*/ }
enum ClusterOp { Takeover{agg, new_owner, new_epoch}, TakeoverProgress{agg, step: u8},
  Giveback{agg}, GrantUnmirrored{agg, epoch}, RevokeUnmirrored{agg}, PlaceSvm{svm, node},
  SetPr{lun, aptpl: String}, SetAna{lun, grp, state}, Config(ConfigDelta) }

#[repr(C, align(4096))] struct EpochPage { seq: AtomicU64, slots: [AggSlot; 60] } // 60×64 B
#[repr(C, align(64))]   struct AggSlot { agg: u64, epoch: u64, state: u32, _p: u32,
  lease_expiry_mono_ns: u64, pad: [u8; 32] }

trait FenceDevice { fn kind(&self) -> FenceKind;                 // Watchdog|Bmc|Pdu|ScsiPr
  async fn fence(&self, victim: NodeId) -> Result<Confirmed, Unconfirmed>; }
trait ControlLink { async fn send(&self, to: NodeId, m: CtrlMsg) -> io::Result<()>;
  fn paths(&self) -> Vec<PathHealth>; }                           // TcpMtls | RingMailbox
trait QuorumService { async fn renew_lease(&self, agg: AggId, holder: NodeId, epoch: u64)
  -> Result<LeaseGrant, LeaseRefused>; async fn propose(&self, op: ClusterOp) -> Result<LogId>; }
```
Never in Raft: lease renewals, heartbeats, NVLOG/ring traffic, CP decisions, per-I/O ALUA checks, SMB open handles, NFS state (it lives in nfsd per SVM and is rebuilt by grace). PR registrations and reservation holders *are* in Raft (tens of changes per day at most): with `force_pr_aptpl=1` LIO rewrites `/var/target/pr/aptpl_<serial>` on every change; inotify → `SetPr`; the standby node loads it into `res_aptpl_metadata` before exporting the LUN.

##### Leases (the safety argument)
- The owner sends `RenewLease{agg, epoch}` every 500 ms. The leader checks owner, epoch and `mode`, runs `ensure_linearizable()` (one heartbeat RTT to one peer, no fsync), records `last_grant[holder]` on its own monotonic clock, and replies. The holder sets `lease_expiry = t_send + 3 s` on CLOCK_MONOTONIC and publishes it in the epoch page. Safety needs bounded clock *rate* drift only (crystal ±100 ppm = 0.3 ms over 3 s; the 100 ms margin tolerates 3%).
- L1: a new leader waits TTL + margin (3.1 s) after its term starts before committing any `Takeover` (it does not know the old leader's last grants). L2: `Takeover{A→B}` commits only after max(partner-silent 2 s, `last_grant[A]` + 3.1 s). L3: the data path checks (epoch, state, lease) at submission *and at completion*; a failed check means BUSY / Transitioning on block, stall on NAS, never an ACK. L4: `otap-ctl` pets the watchdog only if every data-path worker checked in within 1 s *and* no owned aggregate has been export-active for more than 1 s past lease expiry. Watchdog 5 s + reboot ≈ 3 s = the 8 s bound, which caps how long a sick node can hold clients hostage; it is not on the survivor's critical path.

##### Failover state machine (per aggregate) and the ladder
States: OwnedMirrored, OwnedUnmirrored(grant), StandbySynced, StandbyStale, Takeover(step 0..9), Giveback(step), Down. Takeover is one Raft-committed plan; steps are idempotent, progress is committed (`TakeoverProgress`), a crash mid-plan resumes at the step. Ordering: fence → data → block flip → NAS → S3 → complete. Unplanned takeover, A dies silently at t0, A was Raft leader, LAN witness:

| t | step |
|---|---|
| 0.02-1.0 s | ring heartbeats missing (20 ms) → link dead at 1 s: evidence only |
| 1.0-1.2 s | B's election timeout (0.5-1 s) expires; B wins with W |
| 2.0 s | partner silent (20 missed control heartbeats on all paths; BMC "Off" is positive evidence, "On" is nothing) |
| 4.3 s | L1 satisfied; B commits `Takeover{X, B, e+1}` (+1 RTT, +5 ms fsync at W); B's TPG set to Transitioning |
| 4.4 s | shelf: PR PREEMPT-AND-ABORT on all members, parallel, ~100 ms; plex: nothing; BMC/PDU power-off runs in parallel, off the critical path |
| 4.5-5.0 s | epoch page flipped to (e+1, Owned); shadow dirty index promoted (the partner indexed continuously); B's TPG → Active/Optimized, ANA Optimized; PR table loaded before export |
| 5.0-7.0 s | SVM netns created, LIF IPs up with 3 gratuitous ARPs, nfsd started in grace with the client-tracking DB from the SVM root, smbd, S3 worker |
| 7.0 s | `TakeoverProgress{complete}`; CP interval shortened to 2 s while unmirrored |

If B was already leader, L1 is skipped and the commit lands at 3.1 s. Planned takeover (nightly rehearsal, giveback): B signals Transitioning, A drains in-flight I/O, forces a CP, hands the ring tail, epoch bumps, flips, NAS move; client-visible pause 2-4 s.

##### Fence policy by media (enforced, surfaced as "fence quality" in the UI)
| media | required | accelerators |
|---|---|---|
| Plex (node-local arrays) | lease + any watchdog (softdog accepted) | BMC/PDU |
| Shelf, SAS drives | lease + PR preempt-and-abort | hardware watchdog, BMC/PDU |
| Shelf, no PR | refuse to enable HA unless a confirmed power fence exists; otherwise takeover waits lease + md drain + SCSI timeout ≈ 15-35 s |

##### NAS and object placement
SVM = network namespace + IPs + nfsd (`--scope <svm>`, lease 30 s, grace 45 s, early end on RECLAIM_COMPLETE) + smbd + S3 worker + its volumes. Phase 1 rule: the SVM lives on the owner of its home aggregate; LIF-only moves (serving data over the cluster network, ONTAP's indirect path) are Phase 2. Block never moves IPs; NAS always does. The same plan executor runs both, with the block flip strictly before the NAS IP move so a client that sees the IP answer never finds its LUN still Standby.

##### Giveback
A returns, joins Raft as follower, publishes no ownership until synced (epoch page boots as "nothing owned"; LIO/nvmet exports created only after a grant; superblocks with epoch > granted are refused). Plex: A resyncs segments since its last CP (log-structured diff, ~1 GB/s). Shelf: A re-registers PR as non-holder. B forces a CP, streams the ring from that boundary, and when A's ring covers [last CP, now] the mirror is live again; `RevokeUnmirrored` commits; auto-giveback after 10 min stable or the nightly slot.

#### Bottlenecks
##### Takeover floor
Detection (2 s) overlaps the election (1-1.2 s), but the lease wait does not: TTL 3 s + margin 0.1 s after the new term = 4.3 s before anything can commit, plus 0.3-0.7 s of flips and replay = 5 s p50 for block, 7 s for NAS. Shortening TTL to 2 s buys 1 s and raises false-takeover exposure to any 2 s stall of `otap-ctl` (GC-free Rust helps; a swapless, pinned, mlocked daemon is mandatory). I would keep 3 s.

##### Client-side detection dominates the unplanned case
Our 5-7 s is invisible if the client cannot see the death quickly. ESXi iSCSI holds I/O ~35 s on a silent target (NoopOut 15 + timeout 10 + RecoveryTimeout 10), Windows iSCSI holds 15 s with MPIO (LinkDownTime) or 60 s without (MaxRequestHoldTime), Linux dm-multipath fails at 15 s unless surviving ports report Transitioning and the shipped `multipath.conf` sets `no_path_retry queue`. FC initiators get RSCN within a second. ESXi NFS: HeartbeatFrequency × HeartbeatMaxFailures + HeartbeatTimeout ≈ 125 s at the NetApp-recommended 12/10/5 (I/O is held, not failed); NFSv3 ESXi datastores see a 7 s pause as nothing. The RST-proxy trick (below) collapses the iSCSI numbers to our ladder.

##### Witness RTT
Steady state: commits need A+B only (0.2 ms). Degraded: each renewal = 1 RTT; at 100 ms RTT the 500 ms renewal still has 4× headroom; at 300 ms it does not. Takeover commit adds 2 RTT (0.2 s at 100 ms).

##### NFSv4 grace and SMB handles
Grace is 45 s of refused new OPENs unless early end works; a 2026 LKML report says the early-end path can be dead (duplicate reclaim records), so verify on 6.12 before promising sub-5 s NAS recovery; NFSv4.0 clients always prolong grace. SMB: no cross-node durable reconnect exists in Samba or ksmbd; files open at the moment of failover get errors (Samba/CTDB class behaviour). Idle sessions reconnect in 1-5 s after the RST.

##### Memory and CPU
Shadow dirty index on the partner: pointers only (≈ 64 B per 4 KiB record), 1.1 GB/s × 30 s = 33 GB payload → 0.5 GB index; the payload already lives in the ring. Control plane: 1 core per node, < 1% of a 10GbE node's work. Raft log: < 100 MB with snapshots.

##### Shelf media hazard (why PR is mandatory there)
Owner's md stripe cache (32768 × 4 KiB × 12 = 1.5 GB) drains for 1-2 s after its last submit; a hung drive stretches that to the SCSI timeout (30 s default, 10 s recommended with SCT ERC 7 s). The survivor's first CP allocates from the same free list. Without PR or the two-arena protocol the only safe wait is ≥ 15 s, which blows the budget.

#### Creative solutions
- **Leader-granted leases, no log writes.** `ensure_linearizable()` confirms leadership with a heartbeat round; the grant is a reply, not an entry. Witness fsync rate drops to zero in steady state, which is what makes a Raspberry Pi on microSD or a 100 ms cloud VM a legitimate witness.
- **Non-electable witness** via `enable_elect=false`, plus `trigger().elect()` on A/B as a guard; leadership always sits on a data node, so commits in the healthy state never cross the witness link.
- **RST proxy for a dead node's block IPs.** After the fence point, B briefly claims A's iSCSI/NVMe-TCP portal IPs (gratuitous ARP) and answers every segment with TCP RST, serving nothing there. Initiators drop the session immediately and fail over to the pre-logged-in Standby/Transitioning paths: ESXi's 35 s pause becomes ~6 s with no host tuning, Windows' 15-60 s likewise. Released when A returns. Block still never *serves* on a floating IP.
- **Two-arena allocation + max-select superblocks** (for Opus-Storage). The committed superblock designates a next-CP arena per node; an owner's uncommitted CP writes only into its own arena; the survivor's first CP uses the other arena, so late DMA from the old owner cannot hit anything the survivor wrote. The superblock is replicated across a full stripe with (epoch, cp_seq) and CRC; readers take the maximum, so a late lower-epoch superblock write is ignored even when it lands last. This makes shelf takeover safe at lease expiry, with PR as belt-and-braces.
- **Plex by log replay, not by block mirroring.** The partner already holds every acknowledged record in its ring; it applies the same NVLOG to its own array as a replicated state machine. No second network copy, no DRBD, no identical geometry (8×16 TB on A, 6×20 TB on B works), dual-power-loss resolution is a (epoch, cp_seq) comparison and a segment diff.
- **Rings and dirty index in hugetlbfs files.** A daemon crash on either node no longer loses the CP window; ublk USER_RECOVERY re-attaches, the ring is still mapped. Only kernel death or power loss costs the window. The same files are the io_uring fixed buffers and RDMA MRs.
- **LocalNvmeRing**: the slot-addressed, self-validating ring format is already a sequential log; a MirrorRing implementation that writes slots to a local NVMe namespace turns single-node and GrantUnmirrored operation into "locally persisted" at NVMe write latency (10-20 µs with PLP, 0.4-0.8 ms consumer).
- **SVM = network namespace.** One nfsd/mountd/smbd/S3 instance per SVM with its own scope and IPs; migration is "start the same netns on the partner", two SVMs can coexist on one node after a takeover, and nothing in `/var` is load-bearing.
- **Data-path liveness folded into the pet** and a stall-to-release policy: if any ublk/LIO request is older than 10 s while the control plane is healthy, the owner voluntarily proposes `Takeover` of itself (ONTAP's "I/O stall → panic", done gracefully).
- **Replay all CRC-valid records above the last CP**, in seq order; the invariant is "ACKed ⇒ placed ∧ slot retained until CP". The contiguous-prefix rule becomes a consistency check, not the cut.
- **The witness as synthetic client**: it mounts a canary NFS export and a canary iSCSI LUN and records the real client-visible pause of every nightly rehearsal into Raft, so the dashboard shows "last failover: 2.8 s, 03:00 today".

#### Holes in the plan
- **Prefix replay loses acknowledged writes** if ACKs are not cumulative per aggregate: record 8 placed and ACKed, record 7 lost in flight (never ACKed); the prefix rule discards 8. Either make client ACKs cumulative (adds head-of-line latency) or replay all valid records. Decide explicitly; I recommend the latter.
- **One MirrorTransport trait hides three ack semantics.** RDMA write-with-immediate and an FC target writing into tmpfs are receiver-passive: bytes land without the partner's daemon running. TCP, even with io_uring multishot recv and provided buffers, needs task_work to run; a SIGSTOPped or wedged partner leaves data in the socket buffer, which dies with the process, so the kernel ACK is not "placed". The trait must declare `AckLevel::{KernelAck, Placed, Indexed}` and the client ACK rule must be "placed survives the *sender's* death given the partner node is alive". Over TCP the partner's daemon liveness is in the write path and `can_reject` matters; over RDMA/FC it is not.
- **GrantUnmirrored policy must be chosen, not implied.** Mirror link dead → Stalled ≤ 2 s → either BUSY forever or ACK unmirrored under a committed grant that disables takeover. In a homelab the mirror is a single DAC; BUSY-forever turns one cable into a pair-wide outage. Recommend: grant, alarm loudly, shorten the CP interval to 2 s, write a LocalNvmeRing if present, and make forced takeover an operator command that prints the exact seconds of acknowledged data it will discard (last mirrored seq vs last ACKed seq, both reported in heartbeats). The quiet break: in this state a *single* further fault loses the window, not a double one; the UI must say so.
- **softdog is adequate only where the media is private.** It fires only if its CPU takes timer interrupts: an IRQ-off hard lockup on that CPU, a firmware/SMI hang or a paused VM defeats it; add the NMI hardlockup detector and `panic_on_oops`. Even when it fires, HBA DMA already queued and drive-side commands finish. So: plex mode accepts softdog; shelf mode requires PR on SAS drives or a confirmed power fence, and the product must refuse to call a SATA-interposer shelf HA. Consumer boards usually have iTCO, but the BIOS NO_REBOOT bit makes it silently inert; test it at install time by letting it fire once.
- **Shared shelf + node-local-only thinking.** The brief treats the shelf as the ONTAP-like default; for homelab hardware the fence story makes plex the safer default and the shelf the expert tier. Numbers: plex costs 8 extra 16 TB drives (~$1,200-1,600 used) for 96 TB usable but allows SATA and mismatched arrays; a shelf costs ~$300-400 (MD1200 + two 9300-8e) but SAS-only, 100-200 W idle, and PR-dependent safety.
- **openraft's "leader lease" is follower-side vote rejection, not a bounded-time leader step-down.** A partitioned leader may believe it leads indefinitely; our lease mechanism must never rely on Raft telling a node it lost leadership. `ensure_linearizable()` failing is the signal.
- **Debian 12's 6.1 vs trixie's 6.12 for HA**: 6.1 lacks ksmbd durable handles (6.9), io_uring NAPI busy-poll registration (6.9), nfsd netlink configuration (6.9/6.10, `nfsdctl`), the 2023-2025 qla2xxx target fixes, and recent ublk flags (USER_COPY 6.4); neither has ublk zero-copy (6.15) or nvmet reservations (post-6.12). Phase 1 should target trixie, with bookworm + backports 6.12 as the documented alternative.
- **XFS-on-LUN for NAS is an HA liability**: a mount on B needs XFS log replay (seconds, unbounded by us), must never coexist with a mount on A (exclusive-mount is yet another fence obligation), and doubles journals. The native inode layer makes NAS takeover a pointer flip and keeps filehandles stable by construction.
- **"Control heartbeats on every Ethernet path"** is not enough: when the LAN switch dies and the mirror is a direct cable, A and B need a control path over that cable or they both lose quorum and fence. The ring mailbox ControlLink is mandatory, and the install guide must say "mirror on a direct DAC, never through the LAN switch".
- **NAS LIF design is absent from the brief.** Migrating IPs without nfsd scope, client-tracking DB placement and Samba state placement yields silent lock loss and stale handles. The SVM-as-netns design above fills it.
- **Virtualized data nodes** (someone will run OpenTAP under Proxmox): leases on a paused VM are only safe if CLOCK_MONOTONIC jumps forward on resume; re-read the clock after every blocking call and refuse to ACK if the page's expiry is in the past.
- **Three protocols, three state homes** is how the N-Blade becomes three products. The HA rule: every piece of state that must outlive a node lives in exactly one of two places, Raft (tiny, slow, identities and placement) or the aggregate (everything else). Anything in process memory or `/var` is ephemeral by definition, and the UI must not promise what ephemeral state cannot deliver (SMB open handles).

#### Phase 1 recommendation
##### Build
A three-node testbed (two data nodes, one witness: a Proxmox VM and a Raspberry Pi both exercised) on trixie 6.12, with `otap-ctl` complete (openraft 0.9.25 on redb, leases, epoch page, fence executor, plan executor, watchdog petter, heartbeats on TCP and the ring mailbox), a stub D-Blade (ublk device, hugetlbfs ring, TCP MirrorRing, plex-by-replay onto a plain file or mdadm array), LIO iSCSI with ALUA and the PR load path, one SVM netns with nfsd (scope, 30/45 s) and Samba, and a canary S3 bucket on `s3s`. Fence devices: softdog, iTCO/ipmi_watchdog where present, one `fence_ipmilan` or `fence_redfish` BMC, one Shelly or Tasmota outlet, PR on a dual-attached SAS shelf if the lab has one.

##### Prove (all measured, all automated)
1. TLA+ (Apalache) model of leases, epochs, GrantUnmirrored and the replay cut with bounded clock drift: invariant "no two nodes ACK writes for one aggregate in overlapping real time" and "every ACKed write is in the survivor's replay" checked for 3 voters, 2 aggregates, 2 clients.
2. madsim or turmoil: 100,000 random schedules over the partition matrix (switch dead, mirror dead, both, witness one-sided, asymmetric, SIGSTOP wedge, stale reboot, dual power loss) with zero invariant violations; loom on the seqlock page.
3. Real gear, 100 unplanned takeovers (pull power, pull the DAC, kill the switch, SIGSTOP `otap-d`, `echo c > /proc/sysrq-trigger`): block I/O resumes p99 ≤ 6 s with the RST proxy on ESXi, Linux and Windows initiators (≤ 10 s without), NAS p99 ≤ 8 s, hard bound 15 s; Porcupine-port check of the canary LUN history shows zero acknowledged-write loss and no stale reads on every single-fault run.
4. Planned takeover and giveback: client-visible pause ≤ 3 s, 30 consecutive nightly rehearsals, measured by the witness as synthetic client; NFSv4.1 grace ends early (verify the 6.12 early-end path works or document the 45 s).
5. GrantUnmirrored engaged within 2 s of mirror loss, revoked within one CP of its return, with the forced-takeover command printing the correct data-loss figure.
6. Dual power loss: both plex and shelf modes converge to one consistent state with no operator input; the on-disk image equals the last committed CP (prefix-ordered loss) as seen by an ext4/NTFS guest fsck.
7. Witness at `tc netem` 100 ms ± 50 ms passes items 3-5; the microSD Pi logs < 100 fsyncs per day.
8. Fence-quality enforcement: shelf mode refuses to enable HA without PR-capable members or a confirmed power fence; the install-time watchdog self-test fires each driver once.

#### Open questions
- **Default media model for the product**: plex-by-replay (my recommendation: SATA allowed, softdog adequate, 50% capacity) or shared SAS shelf (capacity-efficient, PR-dependent)? Decides the fence tier, the install guide and half the Phase 1 test matrix.
- **GrantUnmirrored policy**: ACK unmirrored with takeover disabled (recommended) or BUSY until the mirror returns? Decides the degraded-mode UX and whether LocalNvmeRing is Phase 1.
- **Lease TTL 3 s vs 2 s**: 1 s faster takeover against false takeovers under any 2 s control-daemon stall. Decides the timer table.
- **Is softdog-only (consumer boards without a working iTCO or BMC) a supported HA tier**, limited to plex mode, or an unsupported "evaluation" mode? Decides UI wording and support policy.
- **SMB failover contract**: is "open handles at the instant of failover get errors, idle sessions reconnect" acceptable for v1 (it is the Samba/CTDB norm), or must we chase persistent handles, which neither Samba nor ksmbd offer across nodes? Decides whether SMB is advertised as HA.
- **Will Opus-Storage adopt the two-arena protocol and all-valid-records replay**, and does the D-Blade keep a continuously applied shadow index on the partner? Decides whether shelf takeover is safe at lease expiry and whether replay is sub-second.
- **One SVM per aggregate in Phase 1** (SVM follows the aggregate) versus LIF-only migration over the cluster network? Decides whether the N-to-D RPC must be remote-capable in Phase 1.
- **Witness placement the owner will actually run**: third box, Pi, or cloud (≤ 100 ms RTT)? Decides the default election and renewal timers we ship.

#### Load-bearing claims
- [CONSENSUS-1] (high) openraft 0.9.25 exposes Config.enable_elect and Config.enable_heartbeat and Raft::ensure_linearizable(); a voter configured with enable_elect=false never starts an election but still votes and counts toward quorum.
  - why it matters: The non-electable witness, zero-fsync leader-granted leases and the leadership-always-on-a-data-node property all depend on it.
- [CONSENSUS-2] (high) rpc.nfsd in nfs-utils supports -S/--scope to set the NFSv4.1 server scope so two nodes can present an identical scope for the same SVM, and nfsd/mountd can run per network namespace.
  - why it matters: SVM-as-netns migration with client state reclaim instead of silent lock loss.
- [CONSENSUS-3] (medium) Linux nfsd ends the grace period early once all NFSv4.1+ clients have sent RECLAIM_COMPLETE (or userspace writes /proc/fs/nfsd/v4_end_grace), enforces grace >= lease, and this early-end path works on Debian's 6.12 kernel.
  - why it matters: The NAS p99 <= 8 s target; if early end is broken, new NFSv4 opens are refused for the full 45 s grace after every failover.
- [CONSENSUS-4] (medium) LIO's per-device pr/res_aptpl_metadata attribute is writable and force_pr_aptpl=1 makes LIO write PR metadata on every change, so persistent reservations can be re-created on the standby node before the LUN is exported; the store refuses while fabric exports are active.
  - why it matters: SCSI-3 PR survival across takeover for Windows Failover Clustering on iSCSI without a native target.
- [CONSENSUS-5] (high) softdog in Linux 6.12 is an hrtimer on CLOCK_MONOTONIC whose expiry calls emergency_restart() (or panic() with soft_panic=1), so it cannot fire during an IRQ-disabled hard lockup on its CPU, a firmware/SMI hang or a paused VM, and it cannot stop HBA DMA already queued.
  - why it matters: The fence-quality policy: softdog accepted only in plex mode; shelf mode requires SCSI-3 PR or a confirmed power fence.
- [CONSENSUS-6] (medium) Debian 13 trixie ships Linux 6.12 LTS and its linux-image-amd64 builds softdog, iTCO_wdt, sp5100_tco, ipmi_watchdog and hpwdt as modules, while ksmbd durable handles v1/v2 (merged in 6.9) are therefore present in trixie and absent in bookworm's 6.1.
  - why it matters: Phase 1 kernel choice (trixie), watchdog availability on every supported board class, and the SMB failover contract.
- [CONSENSUS-7] (medium) Windows iSCSI defaults are MaxRequestHoldTime 60 s (non-MPIO) and LinkDownTime 15 s (MPIO); ESXi marks an NFS datastore unavailable after HeartbeatFrequency x HeartbeatMaxFailures + HeartbeatTimeout seconds (about 125 s at the NetApp-recommended 12/10/5) and holds rather than fails VM I/O; ESXi's stock iSCSI stack pauses about 35 s on a silent target.
  - why it matters: The client-visible failover analysis and the value of the RST proxy; if initiators detect silent death faster, the proxy is unnecessary, if slower, host tuning becomes mandatory.
- [CONSENSUS-8] (high) Neither Samba 4.17/4.22 nor ksmbd replicates SMB open-handle (durable or persistent) state across nodes, so after an IP migration a client's durable reconnect fails and files open at the instant of failover receive errors, while idle sessions reconnect transparently.
  - why it matters: Whether SMB can honestly be advertised as non-disruptive HA; it also fixes the ordering of block flips before NAS IP moves.

## Round 2: cross-examination

### By Opus-Fabrics

#### Opus-Fabrics on Opus-Storage
**1. Rings per (aggregate, direction) in hugetlbfs cannot be the inbound ring.** You concede fileio has no hugetlbfs write path, so the FC-receiving ring is tmpfs; and mid-stream link fallback (an FC put fails, the record is re-put on TCP) requires every link to land in the *same* file, so the inbound ring is tmpfs for all transports, not per transport. Per-aggregate rings also multiply RDMA MRs, io_uring registrations and FC LUNs, and partition DRAM statically: your "2 x 8 GiB" table assumes one aggregate per node; an HDD plus a flash aggregate per node means four rings. Instead: one inbound ring per node per direction (tmpfs `huge=always`, allocated and mlocked at boot by the ring-holder while memory is unfragmented, THP backing verified via `ShmemPmdMapped`), slots tagged by `aggregate_id`, per-aggregate quotas in the sender's allocator; hugetlbfs for the outbound arena only.

**2. "A daemon crash is a 2 s pause, never a takeover" ignores that the mirror link dies with the daemon.** PD, MR and QP are process objects: a receiver crash drives the sender's QP to ERR after retry exhaustion, so RDMA is not receiver-passive as built; a TCP crash RSTs the socket; a sender crash loses the ack state (which slots are Placed). Restarting inside my Stalled <= 2 s window means re-registering 8-16 GiB of MRs (fast only if THP/hugetlb-backed; 4 KiB pages take seconds and exhaust mlx4 MTTs), an rdma_cm reconnect, and re-putting the right slots. Instead: the ring-holder owns the verbs context, PD, MR and the *receiver* QP, and `dbladed` imports them ([ibv_import_mr](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man3/ibv_import_mr.3.html), rdma-core >= 30, in both Debian releases); the sender keeps a per-slot acked bitmap in its ring file; the 50-kill test must count mirror re-establishment inside the 2 s.

**3. Plex-by-reference turns the sender-owned ring into a credit channel.** A slot can be reused only after the *partner's* CP has written that segment, so the slower or rebuilding array governs the owner's ring, and you need a hard receiver-to-sender `Released{up_to_seq}` message (my only receiver signal today is advisory `Rejected`) plus the manifest stream (5% of 1 GB/s = 50 MB/s); on an FC mirror that feedback rides Ethernet. It also requires identical geometry, which Consensus's plex-by-replay explicitly denies. Instead: if plex is chosen, put the credit message in the ring contract now, size rings for max(own CP, partner CP) plus manifest latency, and give the manifest its own ControlLink stream, never the heartbeat channel.

**4. Split-role FC ports are right, but they cost the client port.** Port-0-initiator/port-1-target avoids the unproven dual-mode N2N and beats my S1. Mechanism, verified: load with `qlini_mode=dual` (mandatory for per-port control) and set `/sys/class/scsi_host/hostX/qlini_mode` per port to `enabled` or `disabled` ([commit](https://ayakael.net/forge/linux-uconsole/commit/0645cb8350cd)); switching resets the port, so do it before clients log in. Costs you omit: both ports of a dual-port QLE2562/2692 are consumed, so FC clients need a second or quad-port card; the module-wide dual load splits exchange resources on every port including client targets; the modprobe.d drop-in must precede `update-initramfs`. Instead: split-role N2N as the Phase 1 FC topology; dual mode only for "clients and mirror on one card".

**5. The 6.15 "request buffer sent to the partner directly" exists only for TCP and FC.** ublk zero-copy registers kernel bvecs as io_uring fixed buffers, usable by SEND_ZC and WRITE_FIXED to the FC LUN, but not by verbs, which needs a VA-backed MR; the RDMA leg, the latency tier, always puts from the arena. Instead: freeze `RingRef` (arena offset) as the only put source; the CP needs the arena copy anyway, as you say.

#### Opus-Fabrics on Opus-Protocols
**1. Hole 5 overstates the FC timer problem and prescribes the wrong fix.** Per-device `timeout` is settable to 1-2 s (integer seconds) by udev, with `fast_io_fail_tmo=1` and `dev_loss_tmo=5`; cable and partner-port death arrive via RSCN and `port_state` in under a second regardless of command timeouts; only a wedged partner target needs the command timeout, and 1-2 s suffices. Sub-second SG_IO/bsg timeouts would forfeit the io_uring path (no `URING_CMD` for sg). Keep block-layer writes from the ring; ring sizing stays transport-blind except for the quarantine term (R_A_TOV).

**2. "Engine ADD_DEVs the LUNs" at takeover contradicts pre-staged Standby paths.** LIO presents a LUN only with a configured backstore, and ALUA Standby rejects READ/WRITE before the backstore, so the ublk device and iblock backstore must exist on the standby node permanently or initiators have no path to fail over to. Instead: pre-create on both nodes; the standby daemon answers zeros and never writes; udev masks `ublkb*` from blkid; takeover is the ALUA/ANA flip only; DEL_DEV is only the 6.12 fence.

**3. Two writers on one qla2xxx tree.** The FC mirror LUN (fileio on tmpfs behind the partner's WWPN ACL), the client target ports, the per-port `qlini_mode` sysfs and the module-wide `dual` load all live in the tree `blade_lio` owns, and your note "dual only if a port must initiate" is wrong: the module must load `dual` for any per-port role control. Instead: a `BladeKind::FcMirror` in `BladeSpec`; Fabrics supplies the spec, `nbladed` is the sole configfs/sysfs writer and owns the modprobe.d policy.

**4. "Poll 50 us then sleep" reinvents io_uring NAPI, worse.** 6.12 has `IORING_REGISTER_NAPI` with `busy_poll_to` and `prefer_busy_poll` ([man page](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man3/io_uring_register_napi.3.html)): the wait loop busy-polls NAPI for the bounded time, then sleeps, in-kernel, without a userspace spin or the wakeup you pay on every quiet-then-busy transition. Instead: NAPI hybrid as the TCP default; RDMA (0 cores) where a ConnectX exists; "0.3 adaptive core" is a guess until measured.

**5. The Phase 1 lab has no RDMA NIC** ("10GbE for mirror and clients") although the owner has CX-3/4/5, RDMA is the only honest 100G and latency leg, and the card decides the crate (CX-3 forces `sideway`). Instead: add a CX-3 and a CX-4 pair with DACs; nvmet-rdma clients and the RoCE mirror on separate ports; mirror direct-attached, no PFC.

**6. iSCSI data digest "one more core" is 5-10x too high.** LIO's digest is crypto-API crc32c (crc32c-intel/pclmul, well above 10 GB/s per core): about 0.1 core at 10GbE. Decide digest by client profile, not CPU budget.

#### Opus-Fabrics on Opus-Consensus
**1. The ring mailbox crosses a process boundary and inherits the leg's semantics.** `otap-ctl` is a separate pinned process; the sender QP, FC fd and socket live in `dbladed`, so a mailbox through `MirrorRing` makes control liveness depend on `dbladed`, the dependency the design exists to avoid. Over FC it inherits the 1-2 s command timeout and the 10 s R_A_TOV; over receiver-passive legs the reply latency is the scanner period; a wedged `dbladed` looks like a dead peer. Instead: `otap-ctl` opens its own tiny channel on the same fabric: its own QP on a holder-imported PD with a 64 KiB MR for RoCE/IB; its own fd on the partner's mirror LUN writing reserved blocks for FC. Model it as a second `ControlLink` implementation with its own 100 ms cadence, not as ring slots, and state that the WWPN ACL or the direct cable is the trust boundary, since there is no mTLS there.

**2. The 4.3 s ladder and the RST proxy assume a silent node.** A wedged node whose kernel, NIC and HBA are alive still owns its IPs and FC logins: the proxy's gratuitous ARP fights a live host, and FC initiators get no RSCN until A's port drops at the watchdog reboot, about 8-9 s. For wedged nodes the 8 s bound *is* on the client-visible path, and FC clients have no RST equivalent. A rebooting A also raises its IPs before `otap-ctl`, so the proxy creates duplicate IPs unless portal addresses are raised only by `nbladed` under Raft state. Instead: gate the proxy on positive fence evidence (BMC Off, PDU state, watchdog deadline passed); publish two bounds (silent about 6 s, wedged about 10 s); block LIFs never in ifupdown.

**3. Replay-all is right, but it needs inbound-ring fencing you do not specify.** Posted RDMA WRs and queued FC exchanges complete without the old owner's CPU, so old-epoch records can land in the survivor's ring during replay (harmless: unacked, epoch-filtered) and after slots are reused at giveback (a correctness hazard). The ladder must tear down the old-epoch inbound path (QP to ERR; LUN ACL drop plus ABTS, unknown fate up to 10 s on FC) before slots are reused, and the receiver must filter by (epoch, lap, cp_seq). Your `AckLevel` triad also collapses: my TCP leg never surfaces the kernel ack (Placed = the receiver's readv into the tmpfs ring) and Indexed is scanner state; `placed_is_hw_ack` is the one bit that matters.

**4. Stall-to-release at 10 s is too late and unreachable with default FC timeouts.** qla2xxx EH escalation stalls a put 30-60 s while Raft stays healthy; 10 s already busts the 10 s p99. Instead: the fabric reports put-stuck at the Stalled boundary (2 s), self-takeover is proposed at about 5 s, the udev timeout rules are product configuration, and the watchdog pet consumes link health.

**5. LocalNvmeRing and my spill-on-silence are one `LocalLink` implementation; define it once.** Both write a ring (own outbound, or the partner's inbound) to a local device; they differ in trigger (GrantUnmirrored vs partner-silent) and durability (yours needs FUA per put on non-PLP drives, 0.4-0.8 ms, so it must be optional). Your shadow-index arithmetic also assumes 30 s of ring retention; Storage's 8 GiB ring holds about 7 s at 10GbE.

#### Cross-cutting conflicts (Opus-Fabrics)
1. **Ring file type and count.** Opus-Storage: hugetlbfs per (aggregate, direction). Opus-Fabrics: one tmpfs inbound ring per node. fileio needs tmpfs and mid-stream fallback needs one file; both designs cannot be built.
2. **FC topology.** Fabrics S1 (dual-mode N2N, one mirror and one client port per card) vs Storage split-role (both ports to the mirror) vs Protocols (a QLE2562 pair for clients, no FC mirror in the lab). On dual-port cards the owner gets two of the three.
3. **Descriptor granularity.** My "universal whole-block rewrite" vs my own `header_granularity=64` vs Storage's 1 M records/s flash target (unbatched 4 KiB rewrites = 4 GB/s of descriptor traffic). Resolution: link-private blocks always, per-link write size (64 B on RDMA/TCP, 4 KiB on FC).
4. **Plex model.** Storage by-reference (identical geometry, manifest) vs Consensus by-replay (geometry-free, partner allocator) vs Protocols (shelf first, plex Phase 3) vs my "plex doubles link load" (wrong under either variant: 1x link, but receiver credits). Either variant changes the ring contract.
5. **Replay rule.** Digest item 9's contiguous prefix vs Consensus's replay-all; my per-put acks make the prefix rule lose acknowledged writes.
6. **ControlLink.** Fabrics: Ethernet-only mTLS, FC cannot carry it. Consensus: mailbox over any ring. Protocols: a second NIC. Three shapes for one trait.
7. **Mirror threading.** Protocols poll-then-sleep, Fabrics isolated busy-poll core, Storage thread-per-core `SINGLE_ISSUER` reactor: three models for one ring.
8. **Takeover bound.** Consensus 6 s p99 with the RST proxy vs Protocols 10 s p99 vs the 8 s watchdog for wedged nodes; Protocols' ADD_DEV-at-takeover vs pre-staged Standby paths.
9. **NFS timers.** Protocols lease 10 s / grace 20 s vs Consensus 30 s / 45 s.
10. **Ring retention and memory.** Storage 2 x 8 GiB (one aggregate per node), Protocols 8 + 8 GiB, Consensus 30 s of retention (33 GB). Not the same product.
11. **Phase 1 fabric scope.** Only Fabrics orders RDMA hardware; Storage, Protocols and Consensus all build on TCP, while Fabrics ranks RDMA ahead of FC and Storage/Protocols lean on the FC ring-as-LUN.

#### Questions for the lead architect (Opus-Fabrics)
1. One inbound tmpfs ring per node shared by all links and aggregates, yes or no, and is hugetlbfs for the outbound arena worth a second file type?
2. FC port budget per node: both ports to the mirror (split-role N2N, S1 off the critical path) or one card serving clients and mirror (dual mode, S1 on it)? Is the FC mirror Phase 1 or Phase 2?
3. Plex by-reference, by-replay, or neither in Phase 1, and are receiver credits (slot freed at the partner's CP) accepted into the ring contract?
4. Ratify replay-all with per-record acks, plus mandatory old-epoch inbound teardown before giveback reuses slots?
5. Does `otap-ctl` get its own fabric channel (own QP, own LUN fd), and is a WWPN ACL or a direct cable an acceptable trust boundary without mTLS?
6. TCP poller policy (io_uring NAPI hybrid) and core floor (8C/16T with 1-2 isolated cores), or RDMA-first wherever a ConnectX exists?
7. Is ConnectX-3 supported (forces `sideway` and legacy `ibv_post_send`), and is RDMA hardware in the Phase 1 order?
8. What client-visible takeover promise per death class (silent vs wedged), and must the RST proxy wait for positive fence evidence?
9. Is `nbladed` the sole owner of the qla2xxx/LIO configfs and sysfs tree and of the module-wide `qlini_mode=dual` policy?
10. NFS lease/grace: 10/20 or 30/45?
11. Does the daemon-crash success criterion include mirror re-establishment inside 2 s (holder-owned PD/MR/QP), or is a daemon crash allowed to cost a Stalled-to-BUSY episode?

### By Opus-Storage

#### Opus-Storage on Opus-Fabrics
1. **Link-private descriptor blocks rewritten whole.** With contiguous slot allocation and `max_inflight` 256, consecutive puts from one link share a descriptor block, and SCSI simple-tag commands or separate RDMA WRs complete in any order: an older block image landing after a newer one erases the header of a record already reported Placed and acked to the client, while both CRCs still validate. It also doubles the bytes of a 4 KiB put and widens quarantine to 64 slots. Instead: one private 4 KiB descriptor block per put (per batch), written once and never rewritten, carrying the batch seq; the scanner walks blocks in slot order. Ordered tags with one outstanding descriptor write per block would also work but caps IOPS.

2. **"The ring fills and the honest behaviour is BUSY."** No such status exists on this path: ublk has no busy completion, LIO iblock maps a bio error to CHECK CONDITION (LU communication failure), nvmet to an NVMe error, and ESXi treats either as a hard error. Back-pressure is my throttle holding requests (single delay capped at 2 s) and the CP interval shrinking; ring sizing follows the throttle model, not link arithmetic.

3. **The TCP leg puts the partner's dbladed in the write path.** My re-attach design makes a dbladed crash a 2 s pause; over TCP that pause is a sender-side stall as long as the Stalled threshold, so a partner restart becomes a GrantUnmirrored event. Instead: receive in a minimal `otap-ringrecv` process (readv into the tmpfs ring, ack) living with the ring-holder unit, with the index scanner passive as on FC and RDMA. All three legs are then receiver-passive with respect to the engine.

4. **"Plex doubles interconnect load; needs a second bulk link."** True only for ONTAP-Select segment shipping, which nobody proposes: plex-by-reference ships a manifest (about 5% of CP bytes) because the partner's inbound ring already holds every payload, and Consensus' replay variant ships nothing. Plex costs partner disk bandwidth and CPU, not link.

5. **Spill-on-silence** matters only in manual-failover mode (shelf and plex run a takeover CP instead), and "a spill file" is the wrong shape: 8 GiB written to an HDD aggregate while the survivor is shortening its own CP interval steals 5-7 s of drain. Write it through `Backstore::write_segment` as foreign-tagged, rate-limited segments and replay it through my plex-rejoin path (seq order, dedupe against the partner's committed `cp_seq`, which the ring heartbeat must carry).

#### Opus-Storage on Opus-Protocols
1. **One ublk queue thread per device.** 6.12 binds each queue to one task, not each task to one queue; SPDK's ublk target serves queues of several devices from one thread (per-io daemons arrive in 6.16). Sixteen LUN threads plus CP, GC, mirror poller and otap-ctl do not fit eight cores, and every handoff is the hop my per-core reactor exists to remove. Instead: queue threads = data cores, all LUNs of an aggregate fetched by the reactor that also runs ingest, CRC and the index; `BlockService::submit` becomes a call on the same thread.

2. **systemd slices with MemoryMax around pinned rings.** tmpfs pages are charged to whichever cgroup first touches them (an LIO kthread or the ring holder), hugetlbfs to the separate hugetlb controller, and mlocked shmem is unreclaimable, so a slice holding a ring that hits MemoryMax gets its ring holder OOM-killed. "NAS page cache 8-16 GB" also collides with my fixed footprint on a 64 GB box. Instead: rings in a dedicated slice without MemoryMax, tmpfs `size=` caps, hugepages reserved at boot, per-SVM MemoryMax derived from DRAM minus rings, map cache and stripe caches.

3. **XFS-on-LUN interactions unaccounted for.** (a) XFS rewrites metadata in place by LBA, which the log turns into a new PBA each time, so a physical-address cache misses every metadata rewrite until CP write-through refills it, and a Time Machine or rsync run exhausts the 1 DWPD write-through budget (12 MB/s per TB of cache); add a per-LUN metadata-heavy cache class. (b) Thick reservation (hole 9) must include the GC reserve and snapshot deadlists, or write-stop at 95% is still an XFS shutdown. (c) Hourly fsfreeze snapshots pin partially dead segments and bring the 85-90% cliff earlier; the UI needs per-volume snapshot usage. (d) UNMAP is a map hole in my engine (no device I/O) and XFS batches discards per log commit, so online `-o discard` is cheap and lets GC see freed space; weekly fstrim is the HDD-era answer.

4. **"Plex halves ingest, Phase 3; node-local disks make Standby a lie."** Under plex-by-reference the partner holds a replica and a standby index, so Standby paths are real; what halves is per-array drain (each array absorbs two aggregates' CPs), not link. Single-node targets without a partner are right only for manual mode.

5. **In-process ublk makes a dbladed crash an all-LUN recovery.** On 6.12 that is START_USER_RECOVERY, re-FETCH of every tag of every device, END_USER_RECOVERY, with LIO commands hanging meanwhile; my 2 s re-attach budget must include it for 16 x 128 tags, and REISSUE dedupe needs `req_id` derived from (device, queue, tag, generation), not a counter that restarts with the daemon.

#### Opus-Storage on Opus-Consensus
1. **Two-arena allocation does not make an md shelf safe without PR.** It covers data and my log-structured metadata, but md's own metadata sits at fixed addresses: the md superblock is rewritten at every dirty-clean transition (`safe_mode_delay` 200 ms, up to twice per idle period) and the write-intent bitmap on every stripe set and clear. A wedged owner's late superblock (older events count) or bitmap write (clearing bits for stripes the survivor is writing) silently corrupts the survivor's array state. PR preempt-and-abort on SAS members, or a confirmed power fence, stays mandatory on md shelves; two-arena is defence in depth, and I adopt it with per-node superblock slots (A0/A1, B0/B1, max-select over all four) so a late write can only tear the dead node's own slots. Two-arena alone suffices only under the Phase 3 native LS-RAID, where nothing has a fixed address.

2. **Plex-by-log-replay with independent geometry is a second D-Blade.** If B's copy has different geometry, B runs its own allocator, map, CP and GC for A's aggregate (double CPU and metadata), and the giveback text ("resyncs segments since its last CP") is false: there are no common segments, so rejoin and dual-power-loss reconciliation are logical (map walk by `birth_cp`, extent copy over the link). The budget ("shadow index, pointers only") omits all of this, and its "30 s of ring" does not exist on a 64 GB box (8 GiB is about 7 s at 1.1 GB/s). Choose: identical geometry with one CP program executed twice (manifest, physical resync) or geometry independence at double allocator cost. I recommend the former for Phase 2.

3. **The takeover ladder omits storage steps on the shelf.** Between PR preempt (4.4 s) and AO (5.0 s) sit `mdadm --assemble` (1-3 s), superblock A/B read and verification, segment-table load (40 MiB at 100 TB), the residual scan and the start of the takeover CP: 6-8 s p50 on the shelf, 5 s only for plex. And L3 must gate my CP submits, above all the superblock write, not only ublk completions: a CP straddling lease expiry abandons its commit.

4. **Stall-to-release self-takeover ping-pongs on a shared shelf.** A hung member stalls the CP, the ring fills, the throttle hard-waits, requests age past 10 s, the owner proposes its own takeover, and the survivor inherits the same hung drive. Classify the stall: member timeout means SCT ERC 7 s, SCSI timeout 10 s, md kicks the drive, continue degraded; daemon or HBA stall means self-takeover. Plex is unaffected.

5. **LocalNvmeRing** is a second log with its own replay-source ordering and doubles write bytes on a flash pool: 1 GB/s unmirrored is 86 TB/day onto a device whose 1 DWPD budget is 44 MB/s continuous (3.84 TB). PLP enterprise or Optane only, Phase 3 opt-in; the default degraded mode is `unmirrored_flush=cp` with 2 s CPs.

6. **"XFS exclusive mount is another fence obligation."** It is the same one: the engine serves no LUN of an aggregate it does not own, so a second mount cannot exist. XFS log replay is bounded by the log (at most 2 GiB, typically 64-512 MiB, read sequentially): seconds. The native inode layer buys pointer-flip takeover at a multi-year cost and stays off the Phase 1-2 roadmap.

#### Cross-cutting conflicts (Opus-Storage)
- **Plex model.** Storage: by-reference manifest, identical geometry, 1x link, flash pools. Consensus: log replay, independent geometry, 50% capacity, the default for every pool. Fabrics: segment shipping at 2x link needing a second bulk link. Protocols: "halves ingest, Phase 3". One allocator cannot serve all four, and the default media model (Consensus plex-default/shelf-expert, Storage shelf-for-HDD/plex-for-flash, Protocols shelf in Phase 1) is three different lab purchases.
- **ControlLink over the mirror ring.** Fabrics: Ethernet-only, FC never the sole link. Consensus: ring mailbox mandatory so A and B keep quorum when the LAN switch dies; Protocols wants a second NIC. A mailbox in "reserved slots" also collides with sender-owned slots, CP-commit slot freeing and whole-block descriptor rewrites; it needs its own region of the ring file.
- **Degraded flush policy.** Storage `unmirrored_flush = placed|cp`; Fabrics `allow|busy|sync-to-disk`; Protocols "1-2 s CPs plus flush-forces-CP, or pause"; Consensus "grant, alarm, 2 s CPs, LocalNvmeRing". One knob, four vocabularies, three defaults.
- **BUSY.** The round-1 timer table ("Stalled at most 2 s then BUSY"), Fabrics' ring sizing, Protocols' NAS path and Consensus' "BUSY forever" all assume a status that LIO iblock, nvmet and ublk cannot emit; only held requests bounded by initiator timeouts, or errors, exist.
- **Replay rule.** Round-1 contiguous prefix versus Consensus' all-valid records. I accept the latter for takeover replay (legal because FLUSH acks are Placed-gated, so every hole is unacked) while normal CPs keep the prefix cut (the owner's own ring has no holes). The standby index and the checker change; needs sign-off.
- **Descriptor layout.** Round-1 shared 64 B headers versus Fabrics' whole-block rewrite versus my per-slot lap and 4 KiB-aligned CP writev. Per-batch private descriptor blocks reconcile them, but nobody owns the record format.
- **Core and memory budgets.** Protocols one thread per LUN, Fabrics one core per TCP link per side, Consensus a dedicated core for otap-ctl, Storage 5-6 ingest cores at 25GbE, all on "8C/16T minimum"; MemoryMax slices versus 16-32 GiB of pinned rings; DRAM floor 32 GB (Storage) versus 64 GB (Protocols, Fabrics).
- **Native inode layer.** Consensus wants it for NAS HA; Protocols and Storage keep XFS-on-LUN through Phase 2.
- **Takeover numbers.** Consensus 5 s block p50 versus my 6-8 s on the shelf (assembly) versus Protocols' 10 s p99: consistent only for plex.

#### Questions for the lead architect (Opus-Storage)
1. Which plex: identical geometry with one replicated CP program (manifest), or geometry-independent replay with two allocators? And the default media model per pool type, which fixes the lab purchase?
2. Is md on a shared shelf permitted only with PR-capable SAS members (two-arena as defence in depth), and is native LS-RAID scheduled as the way to relax that?
3. One degraded-mode policy: name, values, default (`placed` or `cp`), and which persona owns the flush-semantics document?
4. Define BUSY: maximum hold per request per protocol, what follows (TRANSITIONING, NOT READY, error), and whether unmirrored BUSY-forever is ever allowed?
5. Adopt all-valid-record takeover replay with Placed-gated FLUSH as the stated invariant?
6. Who owns the record format, and are per-batch descriptor blocks the layout?
7. Is the ring-mailbox ControlLink a Phase 1 deliverable (which makes FC a control path and adds a mailbox region to the ring)?
8. Who owns the core and memory budget table; is the DRAM floor 32 or 64 GB; is thread-per-core the rule across ublk, mirror and CP?
9. Is trixie-backports (6.15+ ublk zero-copy, 6.16 per-io daemons) allowed in the product?
10. Shared-shelf stall policy: member timeout means md eviction, daemon stall means self-takeover; who enforces SCT ERC and SCSI timeouts at pool creation?

### By Opus-Protocols

#### Opus-Protocols on Opus-Fabrics
**1. The end-to-end latency criteria are unreachable through any 6.12 kernel target.** "4 KiB mirrored write acked to the client in <60 us on 25GbE RoCE, <120 us on 10GbE TCP, including the ublk hop" ignores that iSCSI costs 50-70 us of wire plus LIO *before* the ublk hop and NVMe/TCP 40-60 us; a 5-8 us RoCE put is 5-10% of what the client sees. State the measurement point (in-process generator vs. a real initiator) and let the client-visible targets be the N-Blade's (iSCSI p50 <= 150 us, NVMe/TCP <= 120 us). Consequence: in a homelab RoCE buys a core, not latency, which weakens the "RDMA before TCP" ordering argument.

**2. Whole-block descriptor rewrites double the command count exactly where it hurts.** "Put = two writes issued concurrently (payload, then descriptor block)" means every *unbatched* put is two commands. Batching needs queue depth; the QD1 fsync class (XFS log, NFS COMMIT, database redo, S3 PUT) has none. On 8GFC that halves the ISP25xx IOPS ceiling to ~50-75k puts/s (0.2-0.3 GB/s at 4 KiB); on TCP it doubles bytes for small records. Instead: on FC make a record one contiguous WRITE ([header page | payload], payload still 4 KiB-aligned) and keep the split layout only where a second WR is free (RDMA); add `layout` to `Caps`.

**3. "FC is never the sole link" strands FC and IB clients.** Their own partition matrix admits a shared LAN switch dying fences both nodes while FC clients are fine. The trait already has a dedicated heartbeat slot; generalize it to a receiver-scanned mailbox so otap-ctl can carry leases and Raft over FC/RDMA (Consensus asks for exactly this). Without it "no single-fabric lock-in" is false for the control plane.

**4. Port roles are under-specified for the EH domain they worry about.** `qlini_mode=dual` is module-wide; the per-port sysfs (`/sys/class/scsi_host/hostX/qlini_mode`, commit 0645cb83, values enabled/disabled/dual/exclusive) must set client-facing ports `disabled` so they never PLOGI into the partner's client port or join an initiator EH domain; only the mirror port is `dual`. And SCSI EH on the mirror sd device quiesces that whole scsi_host for 2-10 s, so the 1 s link-dead flip must be driven by the heartbeat timer with in-flight puts resolving `Unknown`, never by waiting for EH to finish.
