**5. Receiver-passive is the FC/RDMA story, but the Phase 1 homelab default is TCP** (10GbE, no RDMA NIC), where the receive thread is in the ack path and dies with the daemon. The "partner daemon kill with no client I/O error" criterion must be stated for TCP, which makes it depend on Storage's re-attach budget, not on the transport.

#### Opus-Protocols on Opus-Storage
**1. "Never fail a write, delay capped at 2 s, hard wait at 100%" ignores what LIO can say to an initiator.** Through iblock a ublk error is CHECK CONDITION (hardware-error class; only BLK_STS_RESV_CONFLICT maps to RESERVATION CONFLICT), never BUSY or TASK SET FULL, so an error is a guest I/O error on ESXi. A hold is bounded by initiator timeouts: Linux fires ABORT TASK at 30 s, LIO cannot cancel a bio so the TMR waits, then LUN RESET, then host reset; ESXi goes APD. Their own hung-disk case ("a 3 s CP becomes 60 s") therefore ends in EH storms, not "degraded service". The only *retriable* signal is ALUA Transitioning / ANA change on the owner's own TPG (initiators retry for `implicit_trans_secs` / ANATT). Instead: Stalled at 2 s drives `set_access(Transitioning)`, the hold is hard-stopped near 20 s, and the round-1 "then BUSY" line is deleted because it is unimplementable on this stack.

**2. ROLLBACK is only safe for clients that crashed.** ESXi retries VM I/O indefinitely through APD and resumes when a path returns; Linux with `no_path_retry queue` (the multipath.conf Consensus ships) and Windows within MaxRequestHoldTime do the same. A VM that survived a two-minute dual power loss (hosts on UPS, storage not: the common homelab layout) resumes against a disk that lost 15 s of *acknowledged* writes while its journal and page cache are ahead: silent corruption no fsck finds. ONTAP's answer is `nvfail`: LUNs offline, NFS stale handles, until the admin clears `in-nvfailed-state`. Instead: ROLLBACK sets the aggregate's LUNs Unavailable/Inaccessible and keeps NAS LIFs down until acknowledged (auto-clear as an opt-in); the power-on UNIT ATTENTION is decoration.

**3. 512e-everywhere pushes patch records into the NAS path.** XFS on a 512 B-sector LUN writes a 512 B-sector log, so every NFS COMMIT, SMB flush and S3 fsync becomes the sub-4 KiB record that costs a 10 ms read at CP time on HDD. Instead: internal NAS/S3 LUNs are 4Kn (`mkfs.xfs -s size=4096`), 512e only for LUNs exported to ESXi and Windows; ublk params per LUN class.

**4. "A daemon crash is a 2 s pause, never a takeover" is unbudgeted and collides with Consensus's L4.** The restart includes process start, hugetlbfs re-attach, the 8 GiB CRC scan (1-2 s by their numbers), rebuilding up to 1 M index entries, `START/END_USER_RECOVERY`, and the TCP mirror socket that died with the process (link dead 1 s plus fallback): 3-5 s. Meanwhile L4 pets the watchdog only if every data-path worker checked in within 1 s, so the node reboots at 5 s. Instead: L4 reads ublk request age from the ring-holder page, not daemon check-ins; the restart budget enters the timer table; knfsd, smbd and LIO stalls during it are fine but must not trip the 10 s stall-to-release rule.

**5. ublk server in `nbladed` plus `dbladed` per aggregate puts an IPC hop in every I/O.** USER_COPY pread/pwrite run in the process holding `/dev/ublkcN`: either nbladed copies into dbladed's ring and wakes it per request (3-10 us, two failure domains for REISSUE duplicates) or the fd lives in dbladed and nbladed is control-plane only, which is my design. Choose the latter; the "unified RPC" stays a trait call.

**6. `unmirrored_flush=cp` makes NAS and S3 unusable while degraded.** XFS flushes its log per fsync; Veeam fsyncs per object across 8-16 streams: at one CP (0.5-3 s) per flush that is roughly 8 MB/s. Instead: Consensus's LocalNvmeRing (10-20 us with PLP) is the degraded-mode durability device; `cp` only when no local NVMe exists.

#### Opus-Protocols on Opus-Consensus
**1. PR survival and pre-staged standby paths cannot both be had on LIO.** Their own CONSENSUS-4 says `res_aptpl_metadata` is refused while fabric exports exist; round 1 requires standby paths pre-logged-in on the partner. So B cannot refresh PR metadata while the LUN is mapped in Standby; at takeover it must unlink the LUN, load, relink, and for that window the only live path answers 05h/25h/00h LOGICAL UNIT NOT SUPPORTED, ESXi's PDL sense (PDL needs all paths, so the likely outcome is a failed failover attempt and a retry, but it is the worst code to emit mid-takeover), while Windows and Linux see a LUN inventory change. Instead: per-LUN policy, `standby_paths` (default; PRs lost at takeover, WSFC fails its roles over) or `pr_survival` (LUN mapped on the partner only at takeover), stated in the UI. nvmet has no reservations on 6.12 regardless.

**2. The RST proxy neither reaches 6 s nor is safe before the watchdog bound.** After a RST, ESXi's session enters recovery and the active path is marked dead at RecoveryTimeout (10 s default, tunable to 1 s), so the gain is 35 to ~10 s unless hosts are tuned, which the proposal claims to avoid. And a node whose otap-ctl died but whose kernel and LIO still answer NOP-Out keeps its IP for up to the 8 s watchdog bound; B claiming it earlier produces ARP flapping with half the segments going to a stalled target. Instead: the proxy runs only after a confirmed power fence or the 8 s bound (so the bound *is* on the critical path); the shipped ESXi recipe (NoopOutInterval 5, NoopOutTimeout 10, RecoveryTimeout 5) and multipath.conf are the baseline; and A's boot must not raise block portal IPs until Raft grants ownership (nbladed owns LIF addresses, never the distro network config).

**3. NFS 30/45 s and "NAS p99 <= 8 s" are incompatible as a criterion.** knfsd refuses non-reclaim OPENs for the whole grace period and the early-end path may be broken on current kernels (their own caveat), so new opens, including VM power-on on an NFS datastore, stall 20-45 s. Instead: split the criterion (reconnect plus I/O on reclaimed state <= 8 s; new-OPEN latency equals grace unless early lift is verified on 6.12), pick one lease/grace pair for `SvmState` (I propose 10/20; knfsd's lease floor is 10), NFSv4.1-only exports by default (4.0 and NLM prolong grace), fsid pinned per volume.

**4. Stall-to-release ping-pongs on a shared shelf.** "Any request older than 10 s proposes Takeover of self": on a shelf the survivor inherits the hung drive and does the same; and under back-to-back CPs with the throttle at hard wait a 10 s request age is reachable with no fault at all. Instead: fail the drive, not the node (SCT ERC 7 s, SCSI timeout 10 s, md marks faulty); self-takeover only when the stall is node-local (ublk queues stuck while the backstore progresses), coupled to the Transitioning flip in my Storage point 1.

**5. s3s is a frontend, not an S3 server.** Veeam-grade immutability needs Object Lock (compliance and governance, retention, legal hold), versioning with delete markers, multipart persistence, bucket policies and ListObjectVersions, all behind the s3s ops trait: 1-1.5 engineer-years that versitygw 1.8.0 ships today. A "canary bucket on s3s" in Phase 1 quietly commits the roadmap to building that. Instead: versitygw per SVM; s3s in Phase 3 only if the Go child process is vetoed.

**6. "XFS-on-LUN is an HA liability" is overstated.** Exclusive mount is already enforced: the ublk device exists only on the node Raft granted the aggregate, so there is nothing to mount on the partner; log replay is bounded by log size (seconds on HDD for a 2 GiB log, less on flash) and is the same work a native layer's replay is; filehandles are stable across nodes via pinned fsid plus XFS inode numbers. The native inode layer is a 10+ engineer-year product that would push NAS to Phase 4; Storage and I both say keep it off the roadmap until block ships.

#### Cross-cutting conflicts (Opus-Protocols)
- **Who runs the ublk queue handler and what the N-to-D path is.** Protocols: in-process in dbladed, a trait call. Storage: ublk server in nbladed, dbladed per aggregate (an IPC hop per I/O). Consensus: a \"stub D-Blade\" under otap-ctl. Only one process can hold `/dev/ublkcN` and do USER_COPY.\n- **Who owns slot allocation and the ring layout.** Storage: per-queue bump allocator with lap counters. Fabrics: link-private 64-slot descriptor blocks rewritten whole. Protocols: per-LUN `reserve_slot`. One slot map, one owner; and the inbound ring is either one tmpfs file per node with three writers (Fabrics) or hugetlbfs per (aggregate, direction) with tmpfs only for FC (Storage); an FC-to-TCP fallback must not change the file.\n- **Back-pressure vocabulary.** Round 1: \"Stalled <= 2 s then BUSY\". Storage: never fail, hold indefinitely. Protocols: BUSY is unimplementable via LIO iblock; Transitioning/ANA-change is the only retriable signal and the hold has a hard stop. The timer table cannot keep \"BUSY\".\n- **Degraded-mode durability knob.** Storage `unmirrored_flush=placed|cp`, Fabrics `allow|busy|sync-to-disk`, Consensus LocalNvmeRing, Protocols \"1-2 s CPs or pause\": four designs for one policy, and the WCE/VWC semantics every blade advertises depend on which wins.\n- **ControlLink transport.** Fabrics: Ethernet-only, FC never the sole link. Consensus and Protocols: a ring mailbox over FC/RDMA so A+B keep quorum when the LAN switch dies. Fabrics' `MirrorLink` has no message channel today.\n- **Ack semantics in one trait.** Fabrics calls TCP `Placed` after readv; Consensus needs `AckLevel::{KernelAck, Placed, Indexed}` because the TCP receiver daemon is in the write path and FC/RDMA are not.\n- **PR survival vs. pre-staged standby paths** (Consensus and Protocols both assumed both; LIO allows one per LUN).\n- **Plex model and the shelf fence.** Storage: identical geometry plus `CpManifest`, PR preempt-abort on members. Consensus: geometry-independent log replay, two-arena allocator plus max-select superblocks, plex as the product default. Protocols: shelf in Phase 1 because it is the only configuration where block ALUA takeover is real.\n- **Takeover orchestration ownership.** Consensus: an imperative step plan in otap-ctl with `TakeoverProgress` in Raft. Protocols: a declarative reconciler in nbladed. One must call the other, and the fence order (gate, Standby flip, ublk delete, < 50 ms) must live in exactly one place. Consensus's `EpochPage` and my `CompletionGate` are the same object and must be one format.\n- **Daemon-restart tolerance.** Storage: 2 s pause, no takeover. Consensus L4: no watchdog pet unless every worker checked in within 1 s, and self-takeover at 10 s request age.\n- **NFS timers.** Consensus 30/45 s versus Protocols 10/20 s, with incompatible success criteria.\n- **Latency criteria.** Fabrics' <60/<120/<250 us \"end to end\" versus Protocols' and Storage's client-visible 120-200 us through LIO/nvmet and ublk."

#### Questions for the lead architect (Opus-Protocols)
1. **Reference HA topology:** shared dual-EMM SAS shelf, plex (manifest or log-replay), or manual failover for the owner's lab and the product default? This decides whether pre-staged standby paths, PR, spill-on-silence and the plex allocator exist in Phase 1.\n2. **Where does the ublk handler live** (dbladed in-process trait call, or nbladed with IPC), and is the N-to-D RPC wire format deferred until a third node exists?\n3. **Who owns slot allocation and the ring files:** one inbound tmpfs ring per node or one per (aggregate, direction); link-private descriptor blocks or dense shared ones; interleaved records on FC?\n4. **Block back-pressure contract:** hold, then ALUA Transitioning/ANA change at 2 s, hard stop near 20 s; delete \"BUSY\" from the timer table?\n5. **Degraded-mode durability default** (placed, LocalNvmeRing, cp, pause) and whether LocalNvmeRing is Phase 1; this fixes the FLUSH semantics the blades advertise.\n6. **ROLLBACK handling:** nvfail-style offline of LUNs and NAS LIFs until acknowledged, or auto-clear for users who accept the loss?\n7. **Per-LUN PR policy:** standby paths by default with PR survival opt-in for WSFC LUNs, or the reverse?\n8. **ControlLink over the mirror ring:** required in Phase 1 (FC/IB client shops) or Phase 2?\n9. **Takeover orchestration:** otap-ctl plan executor calling nbladed's reconciler, or the reverse; and what liveness source L4 reads (ublk request age vs. daemon check-ins), with the daemon-restart budget in the timer table.\n10. **NFS lease/grace pair and the NAS success criterion** (reclaimed-state I/O vs. new-OPEN latency), NFSv4.1-only default, and who verifies early grace lift on 6.12.\n11. **S3 blade:** versitygw (Go child process) in Phase 1, or a native s3s gateway with the 1-1.5 engineer-year cost made explicit?\n12. **Kernel floor:** trixie 6.12 only (with bookworm-backports as a documented alternative), trixie-backports 6.16+ opt-in for ublk zero-copy?"

### By Opus-Consensus

#### Opus-Consensus on Opus-Fabrics
1. **Ethernet-only ControlLink ("FC cannot carry it, no IPFC").** Their own matrix concedes the result: shared switch dies, "nobody has quorum, both fence, full outage", remedied by a second NIC and a witness on another switch. In a one-switch homelab that is the most probable partition and the remedy is hardware. A ring mailbox needs no IPFC: Raft frames are ordinary self-validating records in reserved slots of any MirrorRing, FC included, answered by the partner's `otap-ctl` through its own outbound ring, so A+B keep 2-of-3 with no LAN. An answered RPC is daemon liveness (Storage's "ring heartbeats are never liveness" is true of hardware acks only). Make the mailbox a required ControlLink implementation; the second NIC stays optional.

2. **The kernel cmdline breaks the 8 s bound.** `panic=5` means reboot 5 s *after* panic, so softdog `soft_panic=1`, `hardlockup_panic`, `softlockup_panic` and `panic_on_oops` all fence at 5+5+3 ≈ 13 s. `nowayout=1` turns every planned `otap-ctl` restart into a reboot; an unclean close already keeps the timer running, nowayout only kills the magic close. Use `panic=-1`, softdog without soft_panic (`emergency_restart`), nowayout=0, and have `otap-ctl` refuse the magic close while it owns an aggregate.

3. **"Lease lost ⇒ A fences at ~8 s" (Ethernet-down/FC-up row).** A healthy owner that cannot renew should stop exporting at TTL (L3) and keep petting; the reboot exists only for a data path that cannot stop. Rebooting the node whose inbound ring is complete adds a cold boot to giveback. Lease loss = Standby plus alarm; watchdog starvation only on stuck workers.

4. **S4's pass criterion ("zero gaps, zero duplicates" via maximal-contiguous-prefix dedupe) passes the failing case.** Record 7 Unknown on FC and re-put on TCP, 8 Placed and ACKed, A dies: prefix replay drops 8 and S4 reports success. Replay every CRC-valid record above the last CP in seq order and judge S4 against the client ACK history (the Porcupine-port checker), not gap-freeness.

5. **Spill-on-silence has no return path.** Nothing stops a rebooted A (hugetlbfs rings gone) from assembling at its old superblock and serving while B's spill is orphaned. The silence that triggers the spill must commit `NeedsReplayFrom{B, seq range}` in Raft, and A's epoch page refuses exports until it is applied. Scope it to the manual tier: under plex the partner already applies the window to its own array.

6. **`quiesce` ordering at takeover is unspecified.** Late FC exchanges from a wedged A land in B's inbound ring after the residual scan; harmless only if indexing of epoch-e records stops before promotion. Order: unmap LUN / QP→ERR, residual scan, promote; the scanner rejects `owner_epoch` below current.

#### Opus-Consensus on Opus-Storage
1. **"A daemon crash is a 2 s pause, never a takeover" collides with the timer table.** CRC-rescanning two 8 GiB rings plus rebuilding a 1 M-entry index is 2-3 s; L4 pets only if every data-path worker checked in within 1 s and Stalled→BUSY is 2 s, so as proposed a `dbladed` crash starves the watchdog and becomes the 8 s fence it was meant to avoid. A supervised restart (systemd reports exit and new PID to `otap-ctl`) should suspend the check-in for ≤ 5 s and raise Stalled to 5 s for that aggregate; persist the index or a slot bitmap beside the ring so re-attach needs no scan; ublk holds I/O meanwhile.

2. **The CP cut "all records with seq ≤ S" is not Placed-gated, so plexes diverge.** Record 7 inserted locally but never Placed (put failed, re-put pending), 8 Placed: A's CP commits 7, B's plex never sees it. Harmless on a shelf, a silent divergence under plex. Advance S only through Placed records (or with the mirror formally off under GrantUnmirrored); the same rule makes the survivor's replay set equal the owner's disk.

3. **Plex-by-reference puts the partner's array in the owner's slot-lifetime loop with no back-pressure path.** "Slot free is a CP-commit event": whose CP? If B lags (slower NVMe, GC by manifest, a rebuilding member) it cannot free slots until its own CP; RDMA and FC receivers cannot reject, so A overwrites un-applied slots and the plex is silently incomplete, and takeover must apply an unbounded backlog before serving. Identical geometry also excludes the typical mismatched pair. Gate the owner's slot reuse on the partner's acknowledged CP over ControlLink, BUSY past ring/2 of lag, report the backlog at takeover; treat geometry-independent plex-by-replay as the general case and by-reference as the matched-geometry optimization.

4. **"Survivor cannot get GrantUnmirrored without the witness, so fall ControlLink back to the mirror link's IP."** With the partner dead *and* the witness unreachable the survivor holds 1 of 3 votes; no path fixes that, and FC has no IP. BUSY is the correct two-fault answer; make the witness cheap to place on independent power and switch, and ship an explicit operator override that prints the split-brain risk and the unmirrored seconds.

5. **Dual-power-loss recovery is specified for the shelf only.** Under plex, A and B hold different arrays with different (epoch, cp_seq); max-select on one array says nothing about authority. Authority is the Raft-committed owner epoch (on redb and the witness); the other node resyncs by segment diff before it may serve. The two-arena protocol is still needed on shelves whose EMMs do not pass PR to the drives, which nobody has verified.

#### Opus-Consensus on Opus-Protocols
1. **Fence order "gate → ALUA Standby → DEL_DEV_ASYNC" hands errors to guests.** On 6.12 a ublk delete either waits on the stuck requests while the daemon lives or, once the daemon is killed, fails them with IOERR; LIO iblock turns that into CHECK CONDITION / LOGICAL UNIT COMMUNICATION FAILURE, which ESXi NMP reports to the VM rather than failing the path (only ALUA/NOT READY sense and NO_CONNECT host status trigger failover, per Broadcom KB 345174). Gate plus Standby covers commands not yet started; for commands stuck in ublk the only clean fence is silence: `otap-ctl` stops renewing, proposes Takeover of itself, the watchdog reboots, sessions drop, initiators retry on the surviving path. DEL_DEV is teardown, never the fence.

2. **"ADD_DEVs the LUNs" at takeover.** A Standby path needs the LUN on B before takeover with identical VPD and capacity; creating the ublk device and LUN mid-takeover makes initiators watch a LUN appear and fails the 50 ms flip target. Provision devices and LUNs on both nodes; the standby engine answers nothing (gate closed); takeover is a group-state flip only.

3. **NAS writes bypass the mirrored-DRAM promise.** With XFS-on-LUN, NFS UNSTABLE and SMB writes sit in the owner's page cache (8-16 GB budgeted) until writeback. NFS is protocol-safe (verifier change, client resends); SMB clients discard data on reply, so an unplanned takeover loses up to `dirty_expire` (30 s) of acknowledged SMB writes, worse than the site-loss clause. Set `dirty_expire_centisecs=200`, `dirty_writeback_centisecs=100`, keep `strict sync`, and print "SMB ≤ 2 s on unplanned takeover" in the share-class table.

4. **NFS lease 10 s / grace 20 s and NFSv4-only exports.** A 10 s lease expires client state during any 10 s stall (laptop sleep, vMotion, backup congestion), paying in steady state for a rare grace window; ONTAP ships 30/45. NFSv3 for ESXi and Proxmox datastores carries no server state (ESXi uses no NLM), so it is the fastest NAS takeover: pause equals the block ladder, no grace. Default 30/45, verify early lift on 6.12, recommend v3 for datastores and v4.1 for home directories.

5. **CompletionGate is node-wide; the takeover unit is the aggregate.** One {lease_deadline, owner_epoch} pair cannot express "X lost, Y still owned", the normal state after a partial giveback or with two aggregates. Make the gate a pointer to the aggregate's slot in the epoch page, checked at submission and completion.

6. **PR shipping has a regression window.** LIO answers PREEMPT with GOOD in-kernel before inotify→Raft commits; A dying in that millisecond gives B the pre-preempt table and the WSFC client cluster a split brain. Either document "PR may regress one operation on unplanned takeover" or do not advertise WSFC (I proposed the same path, so this is ours to fix).

#### Cross-cutting conflicts (Opus-Consensus)
- **Control path when the LAN dies.** Fabrics (Ethernet-only ControlLink, second NIC, witness on another switch), Protocols (direct second NIC), Storage (fall back to the mirror link's IP; ring heartbeats never liveness) and Consensus (ring mailbox over any MirrorRing, FC included) cannot all ship; the install guide, the witness placement and the both-links-down outcome differ by choice.\n- **What lease loss means on a healthy owner.** Fabrics and Storage: reboot at ~8 s. Consensus: stop exporting at TTL, stay up, reboot only when workers are stuck. Giveback RTO and the L4 pet rule depend on which.\n- **HA tier in Phase 1.** Storage: shelf for HDD, plex-by-reference for flash (identical geometry), manual otherwise. Protocols: shelf only, plex in Phase 3, manual = single-node targets. Consensus: plex-by-replay default, shelf as the SAS-pro tier. Fabrics: spill-on-silence for manual. Phase 1 proof of HA on SATA or consumer boards exists only under plex; shelf HA rests on unverified PR passthrough through MD1200 EMMs.\n- **Replay and cut rules.** Fabrics' prefix replay versus Storage's and Consensus's all-valid replay; Storage's seq ≤ S cut versus the Placed-gated cut plex needs; partner-CP gating of slot reuse on non-rejecting transports is specified by nobody.\n- **Fence on a hung-but-alive owner.** Protocols' DEL_DEV versus Consensus's stall-to-release and reboot; the former produces guest-visible errors.\n- **Daemon-crash budget.** Storage's 2 s re-attach versus L4's 1 s worker check-in and the 2 s Stalled threshold.\n- **FC mirror topology.** Fabrics: dual-mode N2N on one port, clients on the other, FC in Phase 2. Storage: port 0 initiator, port 1 target, no dual mode (consumes both ports). Protocols asks whether FC mirror is Phase 1 at all.\n- **NFS timers and versions.** Protocols 10/20 and v4-only versus Consensus 30/45 and v3 for datastores.\n- **Planned giveback pause.** Consensus's 2-4 s assumed a native inode layer; with XFS-on-LUN plus md assemble it is 10-20 s plus any grace. The number must change or the layer must.\n- **Kernel cmdline and watchdog ownership.** Fabrics' `panic=5 nowayout=1` contradicts the 8 s bound in the one timer table.\n- **Gate shape.** Protocols' node-wide CompletionGate versus the per-aggregate epoch page.

#### Questions for the lead architect (Opus-Consensus)
1. Which HA tier is Phase 1: plex-by-replay on any geometry, plex-by-reference on matched geometry, shared SAS shelf, or manual? Must HA be provable on SATA and consumer boards in Phase 1?\n2. Is the ring mailbox ControlLink mandatory (one-switch homelab survives), or do we require a second NIC and a witness on a separate switch and power?\n3. On lease loss with a healthy data path: stop exporting and stay up, or reboot?\n4. Replay all CRC-valid records or the contiguous prefix? Is the CP cut Placed-gated, and is the owner's slot reuse gated on the partner's CP in plex mode?\n5. Fence for a hung owner: watchdog reboot after stall-to-release, or ublk delete? Is any guest-visible I/O error acceptable during takeover?\n6. How long may `dbladed` be absent in a supervised restart before it counts as a stall (2 s or 5 s), and does the index persist beside the ring?\n7. NAS durability contract: is \"SMB acknowledged writes may lose ≤ 2 s on unplanned takeover\" acceptable, or must SMB shares run write-through at the cost of per-write latency?\n8. NFS defaults: 30/45 or 10/20; v3 recommended for datastores?\n9. FC mirror: Phase 1 or 2, and dual-mode N2N or cross-cabled initiator/target ports?\n10. Is a one-operation PR regression on unplanned takeover acceptable for a WSFC support claim?\n11. Who owns the single timer table including `panic=`, `soft_margin`, lease TTL and the NFS values, so the four proposals stop carrying different numbers?

## Fact-check verdicts (32 claims)

### [FABRICS-1] CONFIRMED (claimed with high confidence by Opus-Fabrics)
- Claim: Debian trixie's linux 6.12.107-1 debian/config/config sets CONFIG_TCM_QLA2XXX=m, CONFIG_TCM_USER2=m, CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK=m, CONFIG_NVME_TARGET_{FC,TCP,RDMA,FCLOOP,LOOP}=m, CONFIG_SMB_SERVER=m, CONFIG_NFSD_V4=y, CONFIG_MD_CLUSTER=m, CONFIG_MLX4_INFINIBAND=m, CONFIG_RDMA_RXE=m and CONFIG_SOFT_WATCHDOG=m.
- Evidence: Fetched salsa tag debian/6.12.107-1, file debian/config/config (the arch-independent common config): CONFIG_TCM_QLA2XXX=m, CONFIG_TCM_USER2=m, CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK=m, CONFIG_NVME_TARGET=m with _FC/_TCP/_RDMA/_FCLOOP/_LOOP=m, CONFIG_SMB_SERVER=m, CONFIG_NFSD=m + CONFIG_NFSD_V4=y, CONFIG_MD_CLUSTER=m, CONFIG_MLX4_INFINIBAND=m, CONFIG_RDMA_RXE=m, CONFIG_SOFT_WATCHDOG=m; also CONFIG_TARGET_CORE=m, CONFIG_ISCSI_TARGET=m, CONFIG_LOOPBACK_TARGET=m, CONFIG_TCM_FC=m, CONFIG_SCSI_QLA_FC=m (https://salsa.debian.org/kernel-team/linux/-/raw/debian/6.12.107-1/debian/config/config). Neither debian/config/kernelarch-x86/config nor debian/config/amd64/config at that tag overrides any of these symbols, so they hold for the amd64 flavour. debian/changelog at the tag: "linux (6.12.107-1) trixie-security; urgency=high" — i.e. it was a trixie-security upload, not the base trixie pocket. Minor caveats: tracker.debian.org today shows trixie=6.12.94-1, trixie-security=6.12.111-1, bookworm-backports=6.12.95-1~bpo12+1, trixie-backports=7.2.6-1~bpo13+1, so 6.12.107-1 is already superseded; the config symbols above are unchanged across the 6.12 trixie series. For bookworm's 6.1 (tag debian/6.1.176-1) the common config has MD_CLUSTER, RDMA_RXE, MLX4_INFINIBAND, NVME_TARGET_FC/TCP=m, but CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK is absent from the drivers/block section (ublk not shipped in bookworm's 6.1), so the ublk path needs bookworm-backports 6.12 or trixie.
- Design impact: The in-box LIO (qla2xxx target, TCMU, tcm_loop, iSCSI, FC), nvmet (all transports), ublk, ksmbd, NFSv4, md-cluster, rxe/mlx4 and softdog strategy on trixie is sound with stock Debian kernels; no custom kernel build is needed. Pin the minimum supported kernel as Debian 6.12 (trixie or bookworm-backports), since bookworm's stock 6.1 lacks ublk; cite the config as "trixie 6.12.x series" rather than one point release.

### [FABRICS-2] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with high confidence by Opus-Fabrics)
- Claim: Debian bookworm's linux 6.1.176-1 config files contain no CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK line and leave CONFIG_NVME_TARGET_FCLOOP and CONFIG_NVME_TARGET_LOOP unset, so plain bookworm kernels have no ublk_drv.
- Correction: The conclusion (no ublk_drv on plain bookworm) is right; the supporting details are partly wrong/irrelevant. Debian linux 6.1.176-1 (tag debian/6.1.176-1) config fragments debian/config/config, amd64/config and kernelarch-x86/config contain no BLK_DEV_UBLK line; since 6.1's drivers/block/Kconfig gives BLK_DEV_UBLK no default, the built kernel has it 'n' (the generated /boot/config-6.1.0-* file will show "# CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK is not set", so a "no line" test against /boot/config would fail). CONFIG_NVME_TARGET_LOOP and NVME_TARGET_FCLOOP are indeed "not set" in bookworm, but they are nvmet loopback/test transports and have nothing to do with ublk. What matters for OpenTAP: bookworm 6.1 already ships CONFIG_NVME_TARGET_FC=m, NVME_TARGET_TCP=m, NVME_TARGET_RDMA=m, TARGET_CORE=m and TCM_QLA2XXX=m. Debian 6.12.57-1 (trixie and bookworm-backports share this source) ships CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK=m, NVME_TARGET_LOOP=m, NVME_TARGET_FCLOOP=m, NVME_TARGET_TCP_TLS=y, plus the same FC/TCP/RDMA/TCM_QLA2XXX modules.
- Evidence: Fetched Debian kernel-team configs directly: https://salsa.debian.org/kernel-team/linux/-/raw/debian/6.1.176-1/debian/config/config (no UBLK; "# CONFIG_NVME_TARGET_LOOP is not set", "# CONFIG_NVME_TARGET_FCLOOP is not set", NVME_TARGET_FC/TCP/RDMA=m, TARGET_CORE=m, TCM_QLA2XXX=m); .../debian/6.1.176-1/debian/config/amd64/config and .../kernelarch-x86/config (no UBLK, no overrides); https://salsa.debian.org/kernel-team/linux/-/raw/debian/6.12.57-1/debian/config/config (CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK=m, NVME_TARGET_LOOP=m, NVME_TARGET_FCLOOP=m, NVME_TARGET_TCP_TLS=y, TCM_QLA2XXX=m). Debian builds from fragments; unlisted symbols take Kconfig defaults, and BLK_DEV_UBLK (tristate, select IO_URING, no default in 6.1) therefore resolves to n. Kconfig entry: https://cateee.net/lkddb/web-lkddb/BLK_DEV_UBLK.html. Not independently verified against a /boot/config excerpt from a running bookworm box.
- Design impact: Debian 12 stock 6.1 is indeed disqualified for a ublk-based D-Blade, but only because of ublk: LIO/tcm_qla2xxx and nvmet FC/TCP/RDMA are all present on bookworm, so an LIO-backed path (e.g. via tcmu or a kernel-resident backstore) could run there if ever needed. Baseline should be Debian 13 (6.12) or bookworm + bookworm-backports 6.12, which gives ublk_drv plus nvmet TCP-TLS; drop the FCLOOP/LOOP argument from the rationale.

### [FABRICS-3] CONFIRMED (claimed with high confidence by Opus-Fabrics)
- Claim: Debian's python3-rtslib-fb (2.1.75-2.1 bookworm, 2.1.76-3 trixie) carries a single patch that only changes the saveconfig path to /etc/rtslib-fb-target/saveconfig.json, so targetcli-fb 1:2.1.53 exposes the upstream qla2xxx fabric.
- Evidence: Versions match: python-rtslib-fb is 2.1.75-2.1 in bookworm and 2.1.76-3 in trixie (https://tracker.debian.org/pkg/python-rtslib-fb, https://sources.debian.org/src/python-rtslib-fb/). Both source trees have exactly one patch in debian/patches, fix-path-of-etc-saveconfig.json.patch (https://sources.debian.org/api/src/python-rtslib-fb/2.1.75-2.1/debian/patches/ and .../2.1.76-3/debian/patches/). The patch only rewrites /etc/target -> /etc/rtslib-fb-target in rtslib/root.py (default_save_file and _preferred_dbroot, i.e. the configfs dbroot attribute also points at /etc/rtslib-fb-target) and scripts/targetctl; no fabric code is touched. rtslib/fabric.py in both versions defines Qla2xxxFabricModule(name 'qla2xxx', kernel_module 'tcm_qla2xxx', wwn_types ('naa',), features ('acls',)) and registers it in fabric_modules, so configfs path is /sys/kernel/config/target/qla2xxx/<naa WWPN>/tpgt_1 (https://sources.debian.org/src/python-rtslib-fb/2.1.76-3/rtslib/fabric.py/). targetcli-fb is 1:2.1.53-1.1 (bookworm) and 1:2.1.53-1.3 (trixie); it carries its own use-rtslib-fb-target-instead-of-etc-target.patch (plus two small functional patches in trixie: fix-mapping-the-new-lun-to-the-node-acl, target-remove-the-reference-to-max_lun) and no fabric changes (https://tracker.debian.org/pkg/targetcli-fb, https://sources.debian.org/api/src/targetcli-fb/1:2.1.53-1.3/debian/patches/). Debian kernel config (debian/latest) sets CONFIG_TCM_QLA2XXX=m, CONFIG_SCSI_QLA_FC=m, CONFIG_TARGET_CORE=m, CONFIG_TCM_FC=m (https://salsa.debian.org/kernel-team/linux/-/raw/debian/latest/debian/config/config); bookworm 6.1 / trixie 6.12 configs not separately verified but Debian has shipped tcm_qla2xxx as a module for years. Minor nits only: the claim omits the targetcli-fb Debian revision suffixes and that targetcli-fb has its own path patch; the rtslib patch also moves dbroot, not only saveconfig.
- Design impact: Stock Debian targetcli-fb/rtslib-fb can drive the qla2xxx fabric on both bookworm and trixie; OpenTAP's daemon should write /etc/rtslib-fb-target/saveconfig.json (not /etc/target/) if it wants coexistence with rtslib-fb-targetctl.service restore, and use configfs paths /sys/kernel/config/target/qla2xxx/<naa.WWPN>/tpgt_1 with NAA-format WWNs. Note sid/forky have jumped to rtslib-fb 2.2.3 and targetcli-fb 3.0.2, so Debian 14 will differ.

### [FABRICS-4] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with high confidence by Opus-Fabrics)
- Claim: rdma-core's mlx4 provider omits IBV_QP_INIT_ATTR_SEND_OPS_FLAGS from MLX4_CREATE_QP_SUP_COMP_MASK and implements no ibv_wr_* functions, while the jonhoo ibverbs 0.9.2 crate uses only ibv_create_qp_ex with the ibv_wr_* API; sideway 0.4.3's BasicQueuePair uses ibv_create_qp and ibv_post_send.
- Correction: Two of three sub-claims hold; the ibverbs-crate sub-claim is backwards. (1) rdma-core mlx4 (master, 2026): MLX4_CREATE_QP_SUP_COMP_MASK = IBV_QP_INIT_ATTR_PD | XRCD | CREATE_FLAGS | MAX_TSO_HEADER; IBV_QP_INIT_ATTR_SEND_OPS_FLAGS is absent and providers/mlx4/{verbs.c,qp.c} define no ibv_wr_* / qp_ex fill functions, only mlx4_post_send/mlx4_post_recv. So ibv_create_qp_ex with SEND_OPS_FLAGS fails on ConnectX-3 (same in Debian 12's rdma-core 44 and Debian 13's). (2) jonhoo ibverbs 0.9.2 (latest, 2025-03-08) does NOT use ibv_create_qp_ex or ibv_wr_*: QueuePairBuilder::build() calls ffi::ibv_create_qp and QueuePair::post_send calls the context ops.post_send pointer (ibv_post_send). It is therefore the legacy-API crate and works on mlx4. (3) sideway 0.4.3 (latest, 2026-06-04): QueuePairBuilder::build() -> BasicQueuePair via ibv_create_qp, BasicPostSendGuard::post -> ibv_post_send; build_ex() -> ExtendedQueuePair via ibv_create_qp_ex + ibv_qp_to_qp_ex with comp_mask including IBV_QP_INIT_ATTR_SEND_OPS_FLAGS, posting via ibv_wr_send/ibv_wr_rdma_write/ibv_wr_complete. Only build_ex() is incompatible with mlx4.
- Evidence: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/linux-rdma/rdma-core/master/providers/mlx4/verbs.c (MLX4_CREATE_QP_SUP_COMP_MASK enum; no ibv_wr_/qp_ex symbols); https://raw.githubusercontent.com/linux-rdma/rdma-core/master/providers/mlx4/qp.c (only mlx4_post_send/recv); https://raw.githubusercontent.com/jonhoo/rust-ibverbs/v0.9.2/ibverbs/src/lib.rs (ffi::ibv_create_qp in build(), ops.post_send in post_send; zero hits for ibv_create_qp_ex/ibv_wr_*); https://crates.io/api/v1/crates/ibverbs/versions (0.9.2 newest); https://docs.rs/crate/sideway/0.4.3/source/src/ibverbs/queue_pair.rs and https://docs.rs/sideway/0.4.3/sideway/ibverbs/queue_pair/struct.BasicQueuePair.html (build vs build_ex, ibv_post_send vs ibv_wr_complete); https://crates.io/api/v1/crates/sideway/versions (0.4.3 newest).
- Design impact: ConnectX-3/mlx4 remains viable as an RDMA mirror NIC with either crate, as long as OpenTAP's transport uses the legacy ibv_create_qp/ibv_post_send path (ibverbs 0.9.2 does this exclusively; sideway needs build() not build_ex()). Do not reject jonhoo ibverbs on the stated grounds; choose on API ergonomics (sideway offers both paths plus rdmacm), and gate the ibv_wr_* fast path at runtime by probing ibv_create_qp_ex with SEND_OPS_FLAGS, falling back to ibv_post_send on mlx4.

### [FABRICS-5] CONFIRMED (claimed with high confidence by Opus-Fabrics)
- Claim: In Linux 6.12 target_core_file opens the backing file O_RDWR|O_CREAT|O_LARGEFILE|O_DSYNC, fd_buffered_io=1 clears O_DSYNC and forces emulate_write_cache=1, writes go through vfs_iter_write on a bvec iterator (one kernel copy), and FD_MAX_BYTES limits one command to 8 MiB; hugetlbfs provides no write_iter so it cannot back a fileio device.
- Evidence: Checked against v6.12 source (drivers/target/target_core_file.c and .h, fs/hugetlbfs/inode.c on raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/...). fd_configure_device: "flags = O_RDWR | O_CREAT | O_LARGEFILE | O_DSYNC;", then under FDBD_HAS_BUFFERED_IO_WCE "flags &= ~O_DSYNC;" and later "dev->dev_attrib.emulate_write_cache = 1;" (fileio_ops has no get_write_cache, so the attribute is also user-settable). fd_do_rw: iov_iter_bvec(&iter, is_write, bvec, sgl_nents, len) then vfs_iter_write/vfs_iter_read; one memcpy from the SGL pages into page cache. target_core_file.h: FD_MAX_BYTES 8388608 ("Limited by the number of iovecs (2048) per vfs_[writev,readv] call"), hw_max_sectors = FD_MAX_BYTES / fd_block_size, and sbc_parse_cdb rejects larger commands, so 8 MiB per command regardless of block size. hugetlbfs_file_operations has read_iter, mmap, fsync, fallocate but no write_iter, so vfs_iter_write returns -EINVAL; nothing in fd_configure_device checks for write_iter, so the device configures and every WRITE fails. Same code in Debian 12's 6.1 (FD_MAX_BYTES identical) and in bookworm-backports/trixie 6.12. Caveats that do not change the verdict: the optional aio=1 path bypasses vfs_iter_write and calls file->f_op->write_iter with IOCB_DIRECT (tmpfs's shmem_file_write_iter ignores IOCB_DIRECT and still does generic_perform_write, so still one copy); on tmpfs O_DSYNC is effectively a no-op either way since shmem never writes back.
- Design impact: The tmpfs-backed fileio inbound ring is viable on all three Debian kernels: HBA DMA into SGL pages, one memcpy into tmpfs pages, then userspace mmaps the ring with no further copy. Use fd_buffered_io=1 (or just accept O_DSYNC being inert on tmpfs), size the ring for 8 MiB max per command, and do not use hugetlbfs; if huge pages matter, use tmpfs mounted with huge=always instead.

### [FABRICS-6] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with high confidence by Opus-Fabrics)
- Claim: Linux 6.12's qla2xxx loads ISP24xx/25xx firmware via request_firmware before flash and ISP81xx/27xx from flash before request_firmware; Debian firmware-qlogic 20250410-2 ships ql2100..ql2500_fw.bin only (ql2500_fw.bin = 8.08.207), so QLE2672/QLE2692 run flash-resident firmware.
- Correction: Load order: correct, but incomplete. In Linux 6.12 qla_init.c, qla24xx_load_risc (used by qla24xx_isp_ops and qla25xx_isp_ops) tries request_firmware blob first, then flash at flt_region_fw. qla81xx_load_risc (used by qla81xx_isp_ops, qla83xx_isp_ops AND qla27xx_isp_ops, the latter also chosen for IS_QLA28XX) is flash first, blob second, golden-flash third. QLE2672 is ISP2031 (IS_QLA83XX), QLE2692 is ISP2261 (IS_QLA27XX); both are flash-first, but the claim's "ISP81xx/27xx" omits the 83xx family that QLE2672 belongs to. ql2xfwloadbin=2 forces blob-first; =1 forces flash-first. The driver's blob table names ql2600/ql8300/ql2700/ql2800_fw.bin, but linux-firmware has never shipped them, and MODULE_FIRMWARE declarations stop at ql2500_fw.bin. Debian: firmware-qlogic 20250410-2 (trixie; bookworm-backports has 20250410-2~bpo12+1; bookworm has 20230210-5) does ship ql2100/2200/2300/2322/2400/2500_fw.bin as the only qla2xxx blobs (plus qed, Brocade and ISP1040-era files). Version is wrong: ql2500_fw.bin 8.08.207 MIDQ entered linux-firmware only on 2025-09-26 (MR !707, WHENCE 8.07.00 MIDQ -> 8.08.207 MIDQ), so the 20250410 snapshot carries 8.07.00; 8.08.207 is only in sid's 20260916-1 (not independently verified). ql2400_fw.bin remains 8.07.00.
- Evidence: qla_init.c v6.12 (qla24xx_load_risc "1) request-firmware" vs qla81xx_load_risc "1) Firmware residing in flash"): https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/drivers/scsi/qla2xxx/qla_init.c. isp_ops table, probe chain, qla_fw_blobs[] and MODULE_FIRMWARE: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/drivers/scsi/qla2xxx/qla_os.c. IS_QLA83XX/IS_QLA27XX macros: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/drivers/scsi/qla2xxx/qla_def.h. PCI IDs 2031=ISP8324 16Gb, 2261=ISP2722 16/32Gb: https://admin.pci-ids.ucw.cz/read/PC/1077. Debian package contents: https://packages.debian.org/trixie/all/firmware-qlogic/filelist. Suite versions: https://tracker.debian.org/pkg/firmware-nonfree. 8.08.207 commit dated 2025-09-26 with WHENCE diff: https://kernel.googlesource.com/pub/scm/linux/kernel/git/firmware/linux-firmware.git/+/e8fbd7daabfa74a5e3b2151ca0aa4fc2bb9f2d24%5E1..e8fbd7daabfa74a5e3b2151ca0aa4fc2bb9f2d24 and https://gitlab.com/kernel-firmware/linux-firmware/-/merge_requests/707.
- Design impact: The core conclusion survives: 16G QLE2672/QLE2692 (and 32G 27xx/28xx) run whatever firmware is in their flash, and no Debian package can raise it, so OpenTAP must enforce a minimum flash firmware (read via sysfs fw_version) and document Marvell flash-update tooling as a prerequisite. The 8G QLE25xx-class fallback story should cite 8.07.00 on trixie/bookworm-backports, not 8.08.207, unless OpenTAP pins a newer firmware-qlogic or ships the blob itself.

### [FABRICS-7] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with high confidence by Opus-Fabrics)
- Claim: qla2xxx in 6.12 exposes the qlini_mode module parameter with values exclusive, disabled, dual and enabled (default enabled), and its target-mode PLOGI/PRLI handlers contain N2N_TOPO branches, but there is no public report of Linux-to-Linux dual-mode N2N between two qla2xxx ports.
- Correction: Driver facts hold for v6.12: qla_os.c declares `qlini_mode = QLA2XXX_INI_MODE_STR_ENABLED` ("enabled" default); qla_target.h defines strings exclusive/disabled/enabled/dual; qlt_parse_ini_mode() accepts exactly those four (case-insensitive). N2N_TOPO branches exist in qlt_handle_login (ELS_PLOGI: WWPN comparison sets vha->d_id) and qlt_24xx_handle_els (ELS_PRLI routed to qlt_handle_login). Debian's kernel-team config (debian/latest, the 6.12 line) builds CONFIG_SCSI_QLA_FC=m, CONFIG_TCM_QLA2XXX=m, CONFIG_TARGET_CORE=m, so no custom kernel is needed on trixie or bookworm-backports. The "no public report" half is overstated: Linux-to-Linux switchless N2N with qla2xxx has been publicly reported since 2015 (Chris Boot, Debian target QLE2464 ↔ Linux initiator QLE2460, direct fibre, no fabric, worked with initiator kernel ≤4.0, firmware instability with 4.1/4.2), and the 2019 YADRO series fixed N2N target-mode login (higher-WWPN target, LOCAL_LOOP_UPDATE, PORT UPDATE flap) with tcm_qla2xxx. What is genuinely unreported is both ports in qlini_mode=dual on a direct link; I found no such report either, so that narrower statement stands but only as absence of evidence, not as a known failure.
- Evidence: v6.12 sources: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/drivers/scsi/qla2xxx/qla_os.c, .../qla_target.c (qlt_parse_ini_mode, N2N_TOPO in qlt_handle_login and qlt_24xx_handle_els), .../qla_target.h (INI_MODE_STR defines). Debian config: https://salsa.debian.org/kernel-team/linux/-/raw/debian/latest/debian/config/config. Linux-to-Linux N2N reports: https://lkml.iu.edu/hypermail/linux/kernel/1510.2/02202.html (2015 direct link); YADRO N2N target fixes https://lkml.kernel.org/linux-scsi/139c4a1040a9077fff39486f47134960d543ca53.camel@suse.de/t/ and https://lkml.org/lkml/2019/12/20/517; dual-mode PRLI fix referenced in https://bugs.launchpad.net/ubuntu/+source/linux/+bug/1770003. Searches for dual+N2N on both ends returned nothing (OSNEXUS dual-mode doc is SCST, not LIO).
- Design impact: Switchless FC is plausible: target-only↔initiator-only N2N has upstream fixes and public history. The only unexercised combination is symmetric dual mode on both ends, which an HA pair mirroring in both directions would need; keep S1 (dual-mode N2N validation on real hardware, including the WWPN-ordering login path) as a Phase 1 critical-path spike, with an asymmetric fallback (two links, each target→initiator) rather than assuming failure.

### [FABRICS-8] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with medium confidence by Opus-Fabrics)
- Claim: A 4 KiB mirror put reaches Placed in roughly 5-10 us on 25GbE RoCE (sender CQE after responder ACK), 25-40 us on busy-polled 10GbE TCP, and 50-100 us on 8GFC via LIO (two fabric round trips plus two firmware traversals plus the fileio copy), so FC is the isolation tier, not the latency tier.
- Correction: The ranking (RoCE < busy-polled TCP < 8GFC/LIO) holds, but the FC mechanism and number are off. A 4 KiB RC RDMA write with CQE-after-ACK on 25GbE RoCE (ConnectX-4 Lx/5, one switch hop) is ~6-12 us including post/poll overhead; 5 us is the floor, not typical. Busy-polled 10GbE TCP app-level RTT for 4 KiB is ~20-35 us median (1-byte TCP_RR busy-poll is 9-16 us; add 3.3 us serialization, two copies, 1-3 segments), with p99 several times higher. An FCP write is two fabric round trips = four frames (FCP_CMND, XFER_RDY, DATA, FCP_RSP), i.e. eight HBA firmware traversals, not two, plus 3-4 kernel interrupt/completion hops (qla2xxx initiator, tcm_qla2xxx ATIO/CTIO, backstore). "Fileio copy" is the wrong assumption: the put must land in OpenTAP's user-space DRAM cache, so the backstore is TCMU (target_core_user, uio ring, ~10-30 us extra) or a custom module. Realistic QD1 4 KiB Placed latency on ISP25xx 8GFC via LIO is ~80-200 us; 50-100 us is optimistic. No published 8GFC+LIO ramdisk latency was found, so the FC figure is estimate-grade.
- Evidence: ib_write_lat 4096 B typical 6.09 us on ConnectX-3 FDR (faster wire than 25GbE): https://habr.com/en/companies/croc/articles/191306 ; ib_write_lat reports one-way (half RTT): https://ramcloud.atlassian.net/wiki/x/VIBo . TCP busy-poll 1-byte TCP_RR 16.25 -> 8.79 us (cxgb4 patch): https://android-kvm.googlesource.com/linux/+/3a336cb17183b29827fdffaffb5e62f8912f5ca1 . 8GFC 4 KiB serialization 5.1 us/direction (800 MB/s). Marvell gives no HBA microsecond figures: https://jp.marvell.com/content/dam/marvell/en/public-collateral/fibre-channel/marvell-real-world-expectations-from-fibre-channel-hbas-white-paper.pdf . Debian kernel config (salsa debian/latest): CONFIG_TCM_QLA2XXX=m, CONFIG_SCSI_QLA_FC=m, CONFIG_TARGET_CORE=m, CONFIG_TCM_USER2=m, CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK=m: https://salsa.debian.org/kernel-team/linux/-/raw/debian/latest/debian/config/config (same symbols =m in bookworm 6.1). qla2xxx qlini_mode must be "dual" for a port to be initiator and target (both nodes mirror both ways): https://codebrowser.dev/linux/linux/drivers/scsi/qla2xxx/qla_target.c.html
- Design impact: Phase ordering (RDMA, TCP, then FC) and "FC is the isolation tier" stand. Set FC mirror-put latency success criteria at ~100-200 us QD1, not 50-100, and plan the FC receive path around TCMU or a custom LIO backstore (not fileio) with qlini_mode=dual on every HA port; RoCE criteria should be ~10 us median rather than 5.

### [STORAGE-1] CONFIRMED (claimed with high confidence by Opus-Storage)
- Claim: Debian 12 bookworm's stock kernel (linux 6.1.176-1) has no CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK entry in its config and ships no ublk_drv.ko in any linux-image package, while Debian trixie (6.12.94 and 6.12.107) and bookworm-backports (6.12.43 to 6.12.95) ship ublk_drv.ko.xz.
- Evidence: Debian kernel config for bookworm (salsa branch debian/6.1/bookworm-security, debian/config/config, read in full across both halves): drivers/block/Kconfig section lists CONFIG_BLK_DEV_NBD=m, CONFIG_BLK_DEV_LOOP=m but no CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK line at all (https://salsa.debian.org/kernel-team/linux/-/raw/debian/6.1/bookworm-security/debian/config/config). The 6.12 bookworm-security branch (same path) and debian/latest both have CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK=m. packages.debian.org contents search for "ublk" in bookworm returns only uapi headers (linux-libc-dev, linux-headers-6.1.0-47/50-common) and linux-doc-6.1; no ublk_drv.ko/.ko.xz in any linux-image package (https://packages.debian.org/search?searchon=contents&keywords=ublk&mode=filename&suite=bookworm&arch=any). bookworm-backports amd64 contents: every linux-image-6.12.{43,57,69,73,74,85,86,88,90,94,95}+deb12-{amd64,cloud-amd64,rt-amd64} ships /lib/modules/.../kernel/drivers/block/ublk_drv.ko.xz, plus include/config/BLK_DEV_UBLK in headers. Trixie contents show ublk_drv.ko in 6.12.94+deb13 and 6.12.107+deb13 image-dbg packages across all archs. Version strings check out against tracker.debian.org: oldstable 6.1.176-1 (security 6.1.187-1, also without UBLK), stable 6.12.94-1 (security 6.12.111-1, same config), oldstable-backports 6.12.95-1~bpo12+1. Note the driver exists upstream since 6.0, so Debian 6.1 merely never enabled it; a self-built linux-source-6.1 could, but no stock package does.
- Design impact: Baseline recommendation stands: require trixie (6.12.x) or bookworm + bookworm-backports kernel; stock bookworm cannot run the ublk-backed N-to-D path without a custom kernel build or DKMS-style out-of-tree module, which OpenTAP should explicitly refuse to support. Also note trixie's security kernel is now 6.12.111 and bookworm's is 6.1.187, so version pins in docs should be stated as ranges, not exact numbers.

### [STORAGE-2] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with high confidence by Opus-Storage)
- Claim: Debian trixie's 6.12.107 kernel config sets CONFIG_TCM_QLA2XXX=m, CONFIG_TCM_USER2=m, CONFIG_SMB_SERVER=m, CONFIG_NVME_TARGET_FC/TCP/RDMA/FCLOOP=m, CONFIG_MD_CLUSTER=m, CONFIG_DM_LOG_WRITES=m, CONFIG_HUGETLBFS=y and CONFIG_SOFT_WATCHDOG=m, and bookworm's 6.1.176 config matches except that NVME_TARGET_FCLOOP is not set.
- Correction: Every symbol listed is correct as stated: Debian linux 6.12.107-1 (trixie) sets CONFIG_TCM_QLA2XXX=m, TCM_USER2=m, SMB_SERVER=m, NVME_TARGET_{FC,TCP,RDMA,FCLOOP,LOOP}=m, MD_CLUSTER=m, DM_LOG_WRITES=m, HUGETLBFS=y, SOFT_WATCHDOG=m (plus TARGET_CORE/ISCSI_TARGET/TCM_FC/INFINIBAND_SRPT/BLK_DEV_UBLK=m); bookworm 6.1.176-1 matches except "# CONFIG_NVME_TARGET_FCLOOP is not set". Two omitted caveats matter: (1) bookworm's 6.1 config has NO CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK at all (and NVME_TARGET_LOOP is also "not set"), so the ublk front end does not exist on stock bookworm; bookworm-backports 6.12.95-1~bpo12+1 has UBLK=m and FCLOOP=m, matching trixie. (2) The cloud flavour (linux-image-cloud-amd64, config.cloud) disables SCSI_QLA_FC and therefore tcm_qla2xxx; use the generic amd64 image. Version note: 6.12.107-1 is in trixie proper; trixie-proposed-updates is at 6.12.111-1 and bookworm-security at 6.1.187-1 (same config).
- Evidence: Debian source configs read directly: https://sources.debian.org/data/main/l/linux/6.12.107-1/debian/config/config and .../6.12.94-1/... (all claimed symbols =m/=y, UBLK=m, FCLOOP=m); https://sources.debian.org/data/main/l/linux/6.1.176-1/debian/config/config (TCM_QLA2XXX/TCM_USER2/SMB_SERVER/MD_CLUSTER/DM_LOG_WRITES=m, HUGETLBFS=y, SOFT_WATCHDOG=m, FCLOOP and NVME_TARGET_LOOP not set, BLK_DEV_UBLK absent from drivers/block/Kconfig section); https://sources.debian.org/data/main/l/linux/6.12.95-1~bpo12+1/debian/config/config (UBLK, FCLOOP, LOOP all =m); https://sources.debian.org/data/main/l/linux/6.12.94-1/debian/config/config.cloud (SCSI_QLA_FC not set). Per-arch overrides (kernelarch-x86/config, amd64/config) contain none of these symbols, so the top-level values are final. Versions from https://tracker.debian.org/pkg/linux and https://sources.debian.org/api/src/linux/.
- Design impact: The in-box module story holds for trixie and bookworm-backports without a kernel rebuild, but the ublk front end (and nvmet-loop / fcloop test paths) require trixie or bookworm with the 6.12 backport kernel, so OpenTAP should state a minimum of 6.12 rather than "Debian 12 or 13" generically. Ship on the generic linux-image-amd64, never the cloud flavour, or tcm_qla2xxx is missing.

### [STORAGE-3] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with high confidence by Opus-Storage)
- Claim: md raid456 refuses DISCARD on RAID4/5/6 arrays unless the module parameter raid456.devices_handle_discard_safely=Y is set, which is only safe when every member reliably returns zeroes after discard.
- Correction: Core claim is true in both Debian kernels: raid5.c has `static bool devices_handle_discard_safely = false; module_param(..., 0644)` and gates discard with `if (!devices_handle_discard_safely || max_discard_sectors < stripe || discard_granularity < stripe)` -> v6.1 `blk_queue_max_discard_sectors(q, 0)` (line 8053-8056), v6.12 `lim.max_hw_discard_sectors = 0` in raid5_set_limits (7760-7763). Caveats: (1) "unless the parameter is set" is necessary but not sufficient — every member must also advertise discard with max_discard_sectors >= one full stripe (roundup_pow_of_two(data_disks*chunk)). (2) The kernel never verifies RZAT/DRAT (discard_zeroes_data checks were removed in 4.12); the parameter is purely an operator attestation. (3) It is evaluated only in raid5_run, so a runtime write to /sys/module/raid456/parameters has no effect until the array is stopped/re-assembled; Debian builds CONFIG_MD_RAID456=m (6.1.176-1 and 6.12.107-1 configs), so it belongs in /etc/modprobe.d + update-initramfs. (4) Even when enabled, raid456 discards only whole stripes: make_discard_request rounds the start up and end down to stripe_sectors and silently completes (bio_endio) the remainder — partial-stripe UNMAP is dropped with success. (5) With the parameter unset, discards are not "silently dropped": max_discard_sectors=0 makes BLKDISCARD/fallocate(PUNCH_HOLE) return EOPNOTSUPP, which OpenTAP's user-space flusher will see. (6) raid456 also sets max_write_zeroes_sectors=0, so WRITE_ZEROES cannot be used as a safe substitute. (7) dm-raid (LVM RAID5) has an identical, separate parameter dm-raid.devices_handle_discard_safely.
- Evidence: Source read directly: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.1/drivers/md/raid5.c (lines 67-69, 8027-8056) and https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/drivers/md/raid5.c (lines 66-68, 7716-7763, 8037); comment: "We only allow DISCARD if the sysadmin has confirmed that only safe devices are in use by setting a module parameter." dm-raid.c v6.12 lines 33, 2985-2987, 4152-4154. Debian configs: https://sources.debian.org/data/main/l/linux/6.1.176-1/debian/config/config and .../6.12.107-1/... both CONFIG_MD_RAID456=m, CONFIG_DM_RAID=m. Origin patch (NeilBrown 2014): https://listman.redhat.com/archives/dm-devel/2014-October/022195.html. Bookworm 6.1, bookworm-backports 6.12, trixie 6.12 behave identically; only the API (queue flag vs limits struct) changed.
- Design impact: Installer must ship `options raid456 devices_handle_discard_safely=Y` (and dm-raid if LVM RAID is used), rebuild the initramfs, and verify RZAT via hdparm -I / nvme id-ctrl before enabling, since the kernel will not check it. The CP flusher must coalesce UNMAP into full-stripe-aligned ranges (and pick chunk size so a stripe is a power of two) or discards are silently swallowed even with the parameter set; treat EOPNOTSUPP from BLKDISCARD as "parameter missing" and alert rather than assume capacity is reclaimed.

### [STORAGE-4] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with high confidence by Opus-Storage)
- Claim: hugetlbfs implements no write(2) path (its contents change only through mmap, truncate and hole punch), so a kernel target such as LIO fileio can land data only in a tmpfs-backed ring, not a hugetlbfs-backed one, while both can be mlocked and registered with io_uring and RDMA.
- Correction: Core claim holds on 6.1 (bookworm) and 6.12 (bookworm-backports, trixie): hugetlbfs_file_operations has read_iter, mmap, fsync, get_unmapped_area, llseek, fallocate (6.12 adds fop_flags=FOP_HUGE_PAGES) and no write/write_iter, so write(2)/pwrite/vfs_iter_write return -EINVAL; contents change only via mmap stores, truncate and fallocate (prealloc/hole-punch). LIO fileio (target_core_file.c fd_do_rw) writes with vfs_iter_write, so a hugetlbfs-backed fileio LUN would open (O_RDWR|O_CREAT, no fs-type check, needs fd_dev_size=) but every WRITE would fail; reads would work. Two caveats: (1) mlock(2) on a hugetlb VMA is explicitly skipped in mlock_fixup (VM_LOCKED not set, not counted in locked_vm); the call succeeds but is a no-op, harmless since hugetlb pages are never swappable. (2) The design corollary is overstated: tmpfs mounted huge=always (shmem THP, plus mTHP sizes in 6.12) accepts write(2) and can back both the daemon ring and the LUN with large folios, so a single file type can serve both, at the cost of best-effort rather than guaranteed huge pages. io_uring registered buffers and RDMA MRs (pin_user_pages) work on both; 6.12 io_uring coalesces huge-page registered buffers.
- Evidence: fs/hugetlbfs/inode.c v6.1 and v6.12 (raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/fs/hugetlbfs/inode.c, .../v6.1/...): file_operations lack write/write_iter. fs/read_write.c v6.12: vfs_write and vfs_iter_write return -EINVAL without write_iter. drivers/target/target_core_file.c v6.12: fd_do_rw uses vfs_iter_write; fd_configure_device opens with filp_open(O_RDWR|O_CREAT|O_LARGEFILE|O_DSYNC) and imposes no fs-type check. mm/mlock.c v6.12 mlock_fixup: is_vm_hugetlb_page(vma) -> goto out, "don't set VM_LOCKED or VM_LOCKONFAULT and don't count". Debian 12/13 ship these upstream files unchanged for this code.
- Design impact: The hugetlbfs-ring / tmpfs-LUN split is a valid choice, but not forced: OpenTAP could standardize on one tmpfs (huge=always, swapoff, io_uring-registered) file type for both rings and drop hugetlbfs reservation management, trading guaranteed 2M/1G pages for simpler ops. Do not rely on mlock for the hugetlbfs ring; rely on hugetlb's inherent non-swappability and pinned registrations instead.

### [STORAGE-5] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with medium confidence by Opus-Storage)
- Claim: io_uring coalesces registered buffers backed by huge pages into one bvec per huge page (since roughly 6.3) and IORING_REGISTER_CLONE_BUFFERS (6.12) lets several rings share one buffer registration.
- Correction: Huge-page coalescing arrived in two steps, not one: 6.3 (commit 57bebf80, Feb 2023) coalesces a registered buffer into a single bvec only when the whole buffer lies within ONE huge page/folio; a buffer spanning many huge pages (e.g. a 16 GiB arena of 2 MiB or 1 GiB pages) still became PAGE_SIZE bvecs until 6.12, when Chenliang Li's multi-hugepage coalescing landed (requires all folios the same size and fully covered except first/last, so use hugetlbfs/MAP_HUGETLB, not best-effort THP). IORING_REGISTER_CLONE_BUFFERS is correct: 6.12; the dst ring must have no buffers registered (-EBUSY) until 6.13 added IORING_REGISTER_DST_REPLACE/SRC_REGISTERED and offset cloning. Cloning shares the pinned pages by reference (no re-pin). Extra constraint: each registered iovec is capped at 1 GiB (io_buffer_validate), so a 16 GiB arena is registered as >=16 iovecs/indices, not one buffer. Debian: bookworm 6.1 has neither feature; bookworm-backports 6.12 and trixie 6.12 have both (but not the 6.13 replace/offset flags).
- Evidence: 6.3 single-folio coalescing: https://lkml.iu.edu/2302.2/06456.html (Begunkov series, applied Feb 2023 for-next = 6.3) and commit 57bebf807e2a. Multi-hugepage coalescing listed under 6.12 io_uring changes: https://kernelnewbies.org/Linux_6.12 ("rsrc: coalescing multi-hugepage registered buffers"); patch discussion https://lkml.iu.edu/hypermail/linux/kernel/2405.0/04658.html. Clone buffers "Available since kernel 6.12", DST_REPLACE/SRC_REGISTERED "since 6.13", dst-must-be-empty rule: https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man3/io_uring_clone_buffers.3.html. Cross-MM accounting quirk when cloning: https://lkml.iu.edu/hypermail/linux/kernel/2501.1/09275.html. The 1 GiB per-iovec limit is from io_uring/rsrc.c io_buffer_validate (long-standing, not re-verified by fetch).
- Design impact: The thread-per-core shared-arena design is viable only on 6.12+ (bookworm-backports or trixie); bookworm's stock 6.1 kernel gets neither coalescing nor cloning, so it must be dropped or given a fallback. Allocate the arena from hugetlbfs with uniform page size and register it as a table of <=1 GiB entries; clone into each ring at startup, before any other buffer registration, since in-place replacement needs 6.13.

### [STORAGE-6] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with high confidence by Opus-Storage)
- Claim: ESXi releases before 9.0 do not natively support 4Kn storage devices, so OpenTAP block LUNs for ESXi must be 512e (512-byte logical, 4 KiB physical) and sub-4 KiB writes must be merged by the D-Blade.
- Correction: Broadcom's own wording is "4Kn storage devices are not natively supported on ESXi versions prior to 9.0", and the 512e/4Kn FAQ says external arrays are fine only if "LUNs presented to ESXi initiators use 512 logical sector size (READ_CAPACITY reports 512)". So for ESXi 6.x/7.x/8.x SAN LUNs the design conclusion holds. Caveats: (1) ESXi 6.7+ does support local 4Kn SAS/SATA HDDs through software emulation ("4K SWE", presented as 512e); only 4Kn SSD/NVMe, RDM and SAN 4Kn LUNs are unsupported pre-9.0. (2) ESXi 9.0 adds 4Kn end-to-end for local SSD/NVMe and "4Kn SCSI SSDs for local VMFS and external storage" (not HDD-class); a 9.0 host might accept a 4Kn OpenTAP LUN only if the LUN advertises non-rotational and the HBA/array are on the VCG; local 4Kn SCSI HDDs are still forced to 512e even on 9.0. (3) Only 512 logical is mandatory; reporting 4 KiB physical (512e) vs 512n is optional. (4) "Sub-4 KiB writes must be merged" is a design inference, not a VMware statement: guests with 4 KiB-cluster filesystems issue mostly aligned I/O, but 512-byte partial writes do occur (MBR LBA-63 disks, database logs, VMFS metadata), so an RMW path is needed but is a minority path.
- Evidence: Broadcom KB 453249 "High Storage Latency and KAVG Seen When Using 4Kn Storage on pre-9.0 Build of ESXi" (https://knowledge.broadcom.com/external/article/453249/): "not natively supported prior to 9.0", "not supported at all on flash prior to 9.0", resolution "re-format with 512 sector size or upgrade to ESXi 9". Broadcom FAQ 327012 (ex-KB 2091600, https://knowledge.broadcom.com/external/article/327012/): 4Kn HDD support since 6.7 (local, 512n VMDKs to guests, no RDM); "does not apply to external arrays as long as LUNs use 512 logical sector size". Broadcom KB 426428 (https://knowledge.broadcom.com/external/article/426428/): ESX 9.0 supports 4Kn VMDKs on local 4Kn SSD/NVMe; local 4Kn SCSI HDD "always presented as 512e"; esxcli "4K SWE" = emulation. VMware blog June 2025 (https://blogs.vmware.com/cloud-foundation/2025/06/19/vsphere-and-vcf-9-core-storage-whats-new/): 9.0 "4Kn SCSI SSDs for local VMFS and external storage".
- Design impact: Keep 512-byte logical sectors as the default for ESXi-facing LUNs (ublk basic params logical_bs_shift=9, physical_bs_shift=12 so LIO/nvmet report 512e), and keep the sector-mask patch-record RMW path, counter and read-cache pinning, but treat RMW as an exception path, not the hot path. Add a per-LUN "4Kn" option gated to ESXi 9.0+/Linux/Windows initiators, and do not assume ESXi 9 accepts a 4Kn HDD-backed LUN.

### [STORAGE-7] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with medium confidence by Opus-Storage)
- Claim: A 12-drive md RAID6 of modern 16 to 24 TB CMR drives absorbs 1.2 to 2 GB/s of full-stripe log writes before garbage collection and 0.7 to 1.0 GB/s at 80% full, while a 7.2K drive delivers 80 random IOPS at QD1 and 150 to 210 at QD32.
- Correction: Per-drive numbers are right: 16-24 TB CMR drives spec 255-285 MB/s at the outer diameter (Exos X24: 285 MB/s), roughly half at the inner diameter; QD1 random read is ~80 IOPS (8.5 ms seek + 4.2 ms rotation); vendor QD16 random-read ratings are 168-170 IOPS and StorageReview measures 205-214 IOPS at 16T/32Q, so "150-210 at QD32" holds for reads (random writes with write cache enabled are 340-550). The empty-array figure of 1.2-2 GB/s for 10 data drives is plausible (raw 2.5-2.85 GB/s OD, 1.3-1.6 GB/s ID, minus md raid5d/stripe-cache overhead; group_thread_cnt must be raised). The "0.7-1.0 GB/s at 80% full" figure is unsupported: it implies only 1.4-2x loss, but classic LFS analysis gives write cost ~6x for greedy cleaning at 80% utilization under uniform overwrites (2/(1-u) with cleaned-segment u well below average), and GC reads share the same spindles. The honest statement is: 0.7-1.0 GB/s only for append-mostly/cold data where victim segments are nearly empty; 0.3-0.5 GB/s for uniform random-overwrite (VM) workloads at 80% utilization; the claim also conflates LBA fill (inner-track slowdown) with log utilization (GC cost), which are separate penalties.
- Evidence: Exos X24 spec and measurements (285 MB/s OD, 168 IOPS QD16 read, 205-214 IOPS at 16T/32Q): https://www.storagereview.com/review/seagate-exos-x24-24tb-hdd-review and https://www.storagereview.com/review/seagate-exos-m-30tb-review-hamr-ing-storage-density. raid5d single-thread bottleneck for HDD sequential writes (Shaohua Li patch comment): https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/panfrost/linux/blob/765d704db1f583630d52dc14c1ea573db6783459/drivers/md/raid5.h. LFS cleaning cost 2/(1-u), greedy giving ~6x at 80%: https://www.cs.utexas.edu/~witchel/380L/lectures/lfs.pdf and https://arxiv.org/pdf/2005.00044. Arithmetic: 10 x 285 MB/s = 2.85 GB/s raw OD; at 6x write amplification 1.2-2 GB/s drains ~0.2-0.33 GB/s of new data.
- Design impact: CP duration and the ring/2 interval must be sized on a worst-case drain of ~0.3-0.5 GB/s (not 0.7-1.0) or the pool must cap utilization near 70% and use cost-benefit/age-aware cleaning with hot/cold segregation. The "25GbE drain-bound" conclusion strengthens, and 10GbE pools also become drain-bound under random-overwrite workloads at high fill, so the write-cache burst budget needs the pessimistic number.

### [STORAGE-8] CONFIRMED (claimed with high confidence by Opus-Storage)
- Claim: libublk 0.4.8 (ublk-org, published 2026-08-29) and crc-fast 1.10 (SIMD CRC32C/CRC64) are current, actively maintained crates on crates.io.
- Evidence: crates.io API (https://crates.io/api/v1/crates/libublk): max_version 0.4.8, published 2026-08-29T14:17Z, repo github.com/ublk-org/libublk-rs, not archived, 766 commits, 60 stars, 6 open issues; README states Linux 6.0+ (CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK), UBLK_F_PER_IO_DAEMON needs 6.16+. Release cadence is active (0.4.5 Oct 2025, 0.4.6 May 2026, 0.4.8 Aug 2026) but 9 of ~25 versions are yanked, including 0.4.7 (two weeks before 0.4.8), 0.4.3, 0.4.1, 0.4.0: pre-1.0 API churn is real. crates.io API (https://crates.io/api/v1/crates/crc-fast): max_version 1.10.0, published 2025-12-31, repo github.com/awesomized/crc-fast-rust, 260 commits, 94 stars, 11 open issues/11 open PRs, no deprecation notice, MSRV policy stable-2. docs.rs CrcAlgorithm enum (https://docs.rs/crc-fast/latest/crc_fast/enum.CrcAlgorithm.html) includes Crc32Iscsi (CRC32C) and Crc64Nvme. A rust-crc-fast package sits in Debian's NEW queue (https://dfsg-new-queue.debian.org/reviews/rust-crc-fast), a sign of downstream interest. Caveat: crc-fast's last release is ~9.5 months old as of 2026-10-12, so "current" holds but "actively maintained" rests on repo activity rather than releases; neither crate is in Debian 12/13 stable, so both are vendored via cargo regardless of kernel (6.1 bookworm has ublk but no zero-copy/auto-buf-reg; 6.12 bookworm-backports/trixie has UBLK_F_USER_COPY and later flags; 6.16+ features are unavailable on any Debian kernel).
- Design impact: Both dependencies stand; no in-house ublk control protocol or CRC kernels needed. Pin exact versions (libublk =0.4.8, crc-fast =1.10.0) with Cargo.lock and vendor them, because libublk yanks releases often and is pre-1.0, and plan for ublk feature flags gated by the 6.1 vs 6.12 Debian kernel rather than libublk's 6.16 capabilities.

### [PROTOCOLS-1] CONFIRMED (claimed with high confidence by Opus-Protocols)
- Claim: Debian trixie's linux 6.12.107-1 config sets CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK=m, CONFIG_TCM_QLA2XXX=m, CONFIG_TCM_USER2=m, CONFIG_NVME_TARGET_{TCP,RDMA,FC,FCLOOP,LOOP}=m, CONFIG_NVME_TARGET_AUTH=y, CONFIG_NVME_TARGET_TCP_TLS=y, CONFIG_SMB_SERVER=m, CONFIG_NFSD_V4=y and CONFIG_MD_CLUSTER=m.
- Evidence: Checked the Debian source package linux 6.12.107-1 (listed for trixie/forky/sid at https://sources.debian.org/api/src/linux/) generic config at https://sources.debian.org/data/main/l/linux/6.12.107-1/debian/config/config. Every symbol matches the claim verbatim: CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK=m, CONFIG_TCM_QLA2XXX=m, CONFIG_TCM_USER2=m, CONFIG_NVME_TARGET=m with _TCP/_RDMA/_FC/_FCLOOP/_LOOP=m, CONFIG_NVME_TARGET_AUTH=y, CONFIG_NVME_TARGET_TCP_TLS=y (also NVME_TARGET_PASSTHRU=y, NVME_HOST_AUTH=y, NVME_TCP_TLS=y, TLS=m, DLM=m), CONFIG_SMB_SERVER=m, CONFIG_NFSD_V4=y, CONFIG_MD_CLUSTER=m. The amd64 and kernelarch-x86 override files (.../debian/config/amd64/config, .../kernelarch-x86/config) contain none of these symbols, so the generic values stand for the amd64 build. bookworm-backports 6.12.95-1~bpo12+1 (.../6.12.95-1~bpo12+1/debian/config/config) has identical values for all of them. Caveats: (1) 6.12.107-1 is already superseded in trixie by 6.12.111-1 (trixie-proposed-updates/security; packages.debian.org/trixie/linux-config-6.12 shows 6.12.111-1), though option values are not expected to change within a stable series; (2) bookworm's stock 6.1.176-1 config has NO BLK_DEV_UBLK, NO NVME_TARGET_AUTH, NO NVME_TARGET_TCP_TLS, and NVME_TARGET_LOOP/FCLOOP are "not set" (it does have TCM_QLA2XXX=m, TCM_USER2=m, SMB_SERVER=m, NFSD_V4=y, MD_CLUSTER=m, NVME_TARGET_TCP/RDMA/FC=m); (3) =y on NVME_TARGET_AUTH/TCP_TLS are bool sub-options compiled into the nvmet module, not built-in code, and TLS needs CONFIG_TLS=m plus userspace ktls-utils (tlshd) to actually work.
- Design impact: The in-box assumption holds for trixie and bookworm-backports 6.12, so no custom kernel is needed for ublk, tcm_qla2xxx, nvmet-tcp/rdma/fc, DH-HMAC-CHAP or NVMe/TCP TLS. OpenTAP must, however, hard-require the 6.12 line: a plain bookworm 6.1 install lacks ublk and nvmet auth/TLS entirely, so the installer should refuse or auto-pull linux-image-amd64 from bookworm-backports, and TLS additionally needs ktls-utils/tlshd packaged.

### [PROTOCOLS-2] CONFIRMED (claimed with medium confidence by Opus-Protocols)
- Claim: Debian bookworm's linux 6.1.176-1 config contains no CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK entry in debian/config/config, kernelarch-x86/config or amd64/config, so the stock Debian 12 kernel ships no ublk driver, while bookworm-backports provides linux-image-amd64 6.12.95-1~bpo12+1.
- Evidence: Versions: tracker.debian.org/pkg/linux (fetched 2026-10-12) lists oldstable 6.1.176-1, oldstable-security 6.1.187-1, oldstable-backports 6.12.95-1~bpo12+1, stable (trixie) 6.12.94-1 / stable-security 6.12.111-1, stable-backports 7.2.6-1~bpo13+1. So the quoted version strings are exact. Config: sources.debian.org/data/main/l/linux/6.1.176-1/debian/config/config (both halves, ~172 KB), .../kernelarch-x86/config and .../amd64/config contain no "UBLK" string at all; the drivers/block/Kconfig block lists CONFIG_BLK_DEV_NBD=m and CONFIG_BLK_DEV_RBD=m but no UBLK line. Since CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK is a tristate with Kconfig default n, an unlisted option resolves to "not set" in Debian's config generation, so the stock 6.1 bookworm kernel has no ublk_drv. By contrast sources.debian.org/data/main/l/linux/6.12.94-1/debian/config/config has CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK=m right after CONFIG_BLK_DEV_RBD=m, so trixie's 6.12 and the identical-config bookworm-backports 6.12.95-1~bpo12+1 ship ublk as a module. Minor nuances, not errors: the security-updated bookworm kernel is 6.1.187-1 (its config was not fetchable on sources.debian.org, but a security point release does not enable new drivers); and the 6.12.94-1 config check covered the first 100 KB of the file, which includes the whole drivers/block section.
- Design impact: The kernel floor of 6.12 stands: OpenTAP's ublk-based LIO/nvmet front end cannot run on stock Debian 12 (6.1), so the support matrix must require bookworm-backports 6.12.x~bpo12 or Debian 13 trixie (6.12), and the installer should probe /boot/config-* or /sys/module/ublk_drv (modprobe ublk_drv) and refuse on 6.1. Note trixie-backports is already on 7.2, so testing against a 7.x ublk (newer UBLK_F_* features) should be on the roadmap.

### [PROTOCOLS-3] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with high confidence by Opus-Protocols)
- Claim: Samba 4.22 grants SMB2 durable handles only when kernel oplocks, kernel share modes and posix locking are all off, negotiates SMB2 leases only with kernel oplocks off, and kernel share modes require a filesystem-specific VFS module that XFS does not provide.
- Correction: Samba 4.22 (Debian 13 trixie) grants SMB2 durable handles only on shares with kernel oplocks = no and kernel share modes = no (both are the defaults since 4.16), plus durable handles = yes (default). "posix locking = no" is NOT an unconditional requirement: vfs_default_durable_cookie() refuses durability only when posix locking = yes AND the handle currently holds byte-range locks (fsp->current_lock_count > 0); lock-free handles are durable with the default posix locking = yes. Delete-on-close handles are never durable. SMB2 leases are granted only with oplocks = yes and kernel oplocks = no, enforced per share in delay_for_oplock() (grant degrades to SMB2_LEASE_NONE). "kernel share modes = yes" calls the VFS filesystem_sharemode hook; vfs_default returns ENOTSUP (every open then fails with a sharing violation), the pre-4.16 flock/mandatory-lock path was removed, and in-tree only vfs_gpfs implements it (vfs_ceph_new registers a stub that logs "unsupported"). XFS, ext4 and btrfs have no module. Kernel oplocks (F_SETLEASE exclusive leases) still work on XFS, giving cross-protocol cache coherence at the cost of leases and durables.
- Evidence: smb.conf(5) current (4.25, same wording as 4.22): durable handles "only enabled if kernel oplocks = no, kernel share modes = no, and posix locking = no"; smb2 leases "only available with oplocks = yes and kernel oplocks = no"; kernel share modes "requires a file system module that supports file system sharemodes" (https://www.samba.org/samba/docs/current/man-html/smb.conf.5.html). Samba 4.16 release notes: mandatory-lock share modes dropped, default changed to no (https://www.samba.org/samba/history/samba-4.16.0.html). Source v4-22-stable: source3/smbd/durable.c (posix check gated on current_lock_count > 0), source3/smbd/open.c delay_for_oplock() (lease refused if lp_kernel_oplocks), source3/modules/vfs_default.c (vfswrap_filesystem_sharemode: errno=ENOTSUP), vfs_gpfs.c (.filesystem_sharemode_fn real), vfs_ceph_new.c (stub).
- Design impact: The two-share-class split stands, but the SMB-only class need not disable posix locking: leave it on and only byte-range-locked handles lose durability, keeping NFS/local lock coherence for the rest. Cross-protocol share modes remain out of scope on XFS unless OpenTAP ships its own Samba VFS module implementing filesystem_sharemode_fn against its own arbiter (what GPFS does), a possible later feature rather than a Samba limitation.

### [PROTOCOLS-4] CONFIRMED (claimed with high confidence by Opus-Protocols)
- Claim: LIO's EXTENDED COPY implements only LID1 with 0xE4 target and 0x02 segment descriptors between devices mapped to the same session on the same node, and has no ODX (POPULATE TOKEN / WRITE USING TOKEN) support.
- Evidence: Checked drivers/target/target_core_xcopy.c at v6.1 (bookworm) and v6.12 (bookworm-backports / trixie) and target_core_sbc.c at mainline master; the code is functionally identical across these versions. (1) Only service action 0 is accepted: pr_err("EXTENDED_COPY(LID4) not supported"); list_id is read but ignored, list_id_usage only logged, and operating parameters set SNLID=1. (2) Only target descriptor 0xE4 (NAA IEEE Registered Extended, LUN-associated) and segment descriptor 0x02 (block-to-block, len 0x18) are parsed; RECEIVE COPY RESULTS advertises exactly p[44]=0x02, p[45]=0xE4 and implements only the operating-parameters service action (copy status / receive data / failed segment return TCM_INVALID_CDB_FIELD). Max two CSCDs, one segment descriptor, transfers capped at XCOPY_MAX_BYTES. (3) target_xcopy_locate_se_dev_e4() walks sess->se_node_acl->lun_entry_hlist, i.e. only LUNs mapped to the requesting initiator's node ACL in that TPG, and requires emulate_3pc=1 on both devices (post-CVE-2020-28374 fix, commit 2896c93811, kernel 5.10.7+). The data movement is done in-kernel via the fake "xcopy-pt" fabric issuing READ_16/WRITE_16 to local backstores, so it is inherently same-node. (4) No POPULATE TOKEN / WRITE USING TOKEN / RECEIVE ROD TOKEN INFORMATION / ODX / ROD handling exists in target_core_sbc.c, target_core_spc.c or target_core_xcopy.c; 0x9E handles only READ CAPACITY(16) and REPORT REFERRALS. Sources: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/drivers/target/target_core_xcopy.c , https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.1/drivers/target/target_core_xcopy.c , https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/master/drivers/target/target_core_sbc.c , https://cve.circl.lu/cve/CVE-2020-28374 , https://lkml.iu.edu/hypermail/linux/kernel/1308.2/04138.html (original 2013 series explicitly excluded token/ODX).
- Design impact: OpenTAP can promise VMware VAAI XCOPY (ESXi uses the LID1 0xE4/0x02 form) only between LUNs that the same initiator ACL sees on the same node with emulate_3pc=1, so cross-node or cross-aggregate-on-partner clones fall back to host copies; Windows/Hyper-V ODX is unavailable on every Debian 12/13 kernel and would require an out-of-tree LIO patch or a userspace (tcmu/ublk-side) SCSI target, so it should be documented as unsupported.

### [PROTOCOLS-5] CONFIRMED (claimed with medium confidence by Opus-Protocols)
- Claim: ublk gained USER_RECOVERY and USER_RECOVERY_REISSUE in 6.1, USER_COPY in 6.4, USER_RECOVERY_FAIL_IO in 6.13, zero-copy buffer registration in 6.15 and AUTO_BUF_REG plus PER_IO_DAEMON in 6.16, so trixie's 6.12 has recovery and user copy but no fail-io flag and no zero copy.
- Evidence: Checked upstream include/uapi/linux/ublk_cmd.h at each tag: v6.0 has only ZERO_COPY/COMP_IN_TASK/NEED_GET_DATA; v6.1 adds USER_RECOVERY and USER_RECOVERY_REISSUE; v6.3 lacks USER_COPY, v6.4 has it (bit 7); v6.12 tops out at ZONED (bit 8), no FAIL_IO, no REGISTER_IO_BUF; v6.13 docs (https://docs.kernel.org/6.13/block/ublk.html) and v6.14 header have USER_RECOVERY_FAIL_IO (bit 9); v6.14 lacks UBLK_U_IO_REGISTER_IO_BUF, v6.15 has REGISTER/UNREGISTER_IO_BUF (0x23/0x24); v6.15 lacks AUTO_BUF_REG/PER_IO_DAEMON, v6.16 has AUTO_BUF_REG (bit 11), QUIESCE (12), PER_IO_DAEMON (13), plus UPDATE_SIZE (10). Sources: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/include/uapi/linux/ublk_cmd.h (and v6.0, v6.1, v6.3, v6.4, v6.14, v6.15, v6.16). v6.12 drivers/block/ublk_drv.c explicitly clears UBLK_F_SUPPORT_ZERO_COPY in ublk_ctrl_add_dev ("driver is not ready to support zero copy"), so 6.12 silently falls back to copy even if the flag is requested. Debian: debian/config/config at tag debian/6.12.43-1 has CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK=m (https://salsa.debian.org/kernel-team/linux/-/raw/debian/6.12.43-1/debian/config/config); trixie and bookworm-backports 6.12 images ship ublk_drv.ko (packages.debian.org contents search). Bookworm's stock 6.1 config has no CONFIG_BLK_DEV_UBLK line and no package in bookworm contains ublk_drv.ko, so Debian 12 without backports has no ublk at all despite upstream 6.1 having recovery.
- Design impact: The 6.12 assumptions hold: Phase 1 write path pays one kernel-to-user copy (USER_COPY via pread/pwrite on the char device, or the default mmap-copy path), and fencing must be done by device delete/stop since FAIL_IO is unavailable; also no online resize (UPDATE_SIZE) or QUIESCE on 6.12. Add one requirement: Debian 12 hosts must run the bookworm-backports 6.12 kernel, because the stock bookworm 6.1 kernel does not build ublk_drv at all.

### [PROTOCOLS-6] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with medium confidence by Opus-Protocols)
