- Claim: nfs-utils 2.8.3 in trixie provides rpc.nfsd --scope, knfsd captures the NFSv4 server scope per network namespace from the starting process's utsname nodename at startup, and nfsdcld accepts -p pipefsdir and -s storagedir, so one knfsd instance per SVM namespace with a migratable scope and client database is possible.
- Correction: Trixie ships nfs-utils 1:2.8.3-1 (bookworm: 1:2.6.2-4+deb12u1, no --scope; it arrived in 2.6.3 via NeilBrown's Nov-2022 patch). rpc.nfsd -S/--scope (and nfs.conf [nfsd] scope) exists in 2.8.3, but it is NOT a netlink attribute: utils/nfsd/nfsd.c implements it as unshare(CLONE_NEWUTS)+sethostname() in the rpc.nfsd process before starting threads, and it is skipped entirely if nfsd is already running in that netns. The kernel side (Scott Mayhew, "nfsd: set the server_scope during service startup", merged 2020, present in 6.1 and 6.12) copies utsname()->nodename of the calling process into per-netns nn->nfsd_name inside nfsd_svc(), i.e. at thread start, not at netns creation, and it is re-copied on every nfsd_svc() call that gets past the early return (6.12 uses `scope ? scope : utsname()->nodename`, scope only non-NULL via the netlink path, which 2.8.3 does not use). nfsd_name is used for both server_scope and major_id in EXCHANGE_ID. nfsdcld in 2.8.3 accepts -p/--pipefsdir and -s/--storagedir (defaults /var/lib/nfs/rpc_pipefs, /var/lib/nfs/nfsdcld). The per-SVM design is therefore feasible, with conditions: run rpc.nfsd, nfsdcld, and a private rpc_pipefs + nfsd mount inside the SVM netns; set scope either via --scope or by giving the SVM its own utsns hostname (equivalent); never adjust thread counts of a running SVM nfsd from a process in another UTS ns, or the scope silently flips to that host's nodename.
- Evidence: packages.debian.org/source/trixie/nfs-utils (2.8.3-1); manpages.debian.org/bookworm/.../rpc.nfsd.8 (no --scope in 2.6.2) vs trixie page (has -S/--scope); raw.githubusercontent.com/linux-nfs/nfs-utils/nfs-utils-2-8-3/utils/nfsd/nfsd.c (unshare(CLONE_NEWUTS)+sethostname, skipped when nfssvc_inuse); mirrors.edge.kernel.org/.../2.6.3-Changelog (commit 580ac005, NeilBrown 2022-11-21); github.com/torvalds/linux/commit/7627d7dc (nfsd_svc strlcpy utsname()->nodename into nn->nfsd_name, used for scope and major_id); raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.1 and v6.12 fs/nfsd/nfssvc.c (unguarded strscpy on each nfsd_svc); manpages.debian.org/trixie/.../nfsdcld.8 (-p, -s); kernel v6.10 netlink scope attr (Jeff Layton series) exists but 2.8.3 rpc.nfsd does not use it.
- Design impact: The SVM = netns + utsns model holds, but OpenTAP should set the SVM hostname in its own utsns itself (making --scope redundant) and must mount nfsd and rpc_pipefs per SVM netns and run one nfsdcld per SVM with -p/-s pointing at the SVM's pipefs and a replicated storagedir. Any thread-count change must be issued from inside the SVM's UTS namespace, since each nfsd_svc() call rewrites the scope and major_id from the caller's nodename.

### [PROTOCOLS-7] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with high confidence by Opus-Protocols)
- Claim: versitygw v1.8.0 (Go, Apache-2.0) implements S3 object lock, bucket versioning (--versioning-dir), multipart uploads, bucket policies and several IAM backends on its POSIX backend but not lifecycle or object ACLs, whereas Garage has no released versioning or object lock and MinIO's community repository is archived (2026-04-25) and source-only.
- Correction: versitygw v1.8.0 (released 2026-09-04; Go; Apache-2.0) does support multipart uploads, bucket policies, and six IAM backends (standalone IAM service, --iam-dir flat file, LDAP, HashiCorp Vault, S3-backed, FreeIPA); PutBucketLifecycleConfiguration is "recognized but not implemented" (NotImplemented on all built-in backends) and PutObjectAcl is unsupported (bucket ACLs are supported, --disable-acl optional). Caveats: (1) POSIX versioning via --versioning-dir is documented as "currently experimental", needs a directory outside the gateway root and per-bucket enablement; (2) object lock/retention/legal hold on POSIX is conditional on a non-"none" --object-lock-mode (flock/fcntl/local; this flag is publish/conditional-write locking, not S3 retention itself) and retention/legal-hold metadata depends on xattrs (unreliable under --nometa). Garage: latest stable is v2.3.0 (April 2026); official compatibility page says "does not (yet) support object versioning" and all object-lock APIs are Missing; versioning+object lock is a WIP PR (#1479, June 2026) targeting next-v3, unreleased. Garage does have partial lifecycle (Expiration, AbortIncompleteMultipartUpload). MinIO: github.com/minio/minio archived 2026-04-25, README "NO LONGER MAINTAINED"; community binaries/Docker images stopped Oct 2025, so source-only is accurate.
- Evidence: versitygw releases (v1.8.0 2026-09-04, v1.1–v1.6 object-lock/versioning fixes, no lifecycle entries): https://github.com/versity/versitygw/releases ; S3 API reference (lifecycle unsupported, PutObjectAcl No, object lock conditional on --object-lock-mode, versioning conditional on --versioning-dir, multipart and bucket policy Yes): https://github.com/versity/versitygw/wiki/S3-API-reference ; Global Options (IAM backends): https://github.com/versity/versitygw/wiki/Global-Options ; POSIX Backend ("Versioning support is currently experimental", --object-lock-mode semantics, --nometa warning): https://github.com/versity/versitygw/wiki/POSIX-Backend ; Incompatibilities (object ACLs not implemented): https://github.com/versity/versitygw/wiki/Incompatibilities-With-AWS-S3 ; Garage compat page: https://garagehq.deuxfleurs.fr/documentation/reference-manual/s3-compatibility/ ; Garage WIP PR: https://git.deuxfleurs.fr/Deuxfleurs/garage/pulls/1479 ; MinIO archive banner: https://github.com/minio/minio ; source-only context: https://pinggy.io/blog/minio_archived_self_hosted_s3_alternatives/
- Design impact: versitygw remains the right Phase 1 pick, but Veeam immutability must be qualified on versitygw's own "experimental" versioning label and validated against Veeam's Object Lock compliance tests; OpenTAP must run it with xattr-capable storage (no --nometa), an explicit --object-lock-mode, and a --versioning-dir on the same filesystem outside the bucket root. Garage could be revisited if v3 ships versioning/object lock; MinIO stays excluded.

### [PROTOCOLS-8] CONFIRMED (claimed with medium confidence by Opus-Protocols)
- Claim: Windows Server 2025 GA and Windows 11 have no inbox NVMe-oF initiator; an in-box NVMe/TCP and NVMe/RDMA initiator exists only in Windows Server Insider preview builds and lacks multipathing.
- Evidence: Microsoft's "What's new in Windows Server 2025" (updated Jan 2026) lists only local NVMe performance work; no NVMe-oF/NVMe-TCP initiator appears (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/get-started/whats-new-windows-server-2025). The inbox initiator was announced March 2026 only for Windows Server vNext Insider builds (29558+; still preview in build 29651, Aug 2026), with NVMe/TCP and NVMe/RDMA (RoCE/iWARP), CLI-only via nvmeofutil.exe, no PowerShell cmdlets, and multipathing "not yet implemented" (Microsoft Tech Community https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/windowsservernewsandbestpractices/introducing-the-windows-nvme-of-initiator-preview-in-windows-server-insiders-bui/4501344; https://www.ntcompatible.com/story/windows-server-vnext-build-29651-adds-trusted-launch-nvmeof-and-refs-boot/; Pure/Everpure blog states explicitly "Not available in released Windows Server 2025", "No multipathing", "Limited network fault recovery": https://blog.everpuredata.com/purely-technical/nvme-over-fabrics-nvme-of-with-windows-server-initiator-and-everpure/; https://storageioblog.com/server-vnext-nvmeof-host-initiator/; Lightbits interop note June 2026 confirms Insider-only). No source shows any Windows 11 (client) build, Insider or GA, shipping the initiator. Minor caveats not affecting the verdict: a third-party StarWind NVMe-oF initiator (TCP/RoCE, v2.0 Sept 2026, with MS DSM MPIO integration) exists for Windows and Windows 11 (https://www.starwindsoftware.com/release-notes-nvme-over-fabrics-initiator), and the Microsoft preview has no NVMe/FC or InfiniBand transport.
- Design impact: iSCSI (LIO) stays mandatory and first-class for Windows Server 2025 and Windows 11 clients, including MPIO-based HA failover across the OpenTAP pair; NVMe/TCP via nvmet serves ESXi, Proxmox and Linux initiators. Plan a future Windows NVMe/TCP path (single-path first, ANA/multipath later) once the vNext initiator ships GA, and optionally document StarWind's initiator as a supported third-party option rather than a dependency.

### [CONSENSUS-1] CONFIRMED (claimed with high confidence by Opus-Consensus)
- Claim: openraft 0.9.25 exposes Config.enable_elect and Config.enable_heartbeat and Raft::ensure_linearizable(); a voter configured with enable_elect=false never starts an election but still votes and counts toward quorum.
- Evidence: openraft 0.9.25 is the latest 0.9.x on crates.io (published 2026-07-28; 0.10.0 is still alpha): https://crates.io/api/v1/crates/openraft/versions. At tag v0.9.25, openraft/src/config/config.rs defines Config.enable_tick, Config.enable_heartbeat ("Whether a leader sends heartbeat log to following nodes") and Config.enable_elect ("Whether a follower will enter candidate state if it does not receive message from the leader for a while"): https://raw.githubusercontent.com/databendlabs/openraft/v0.9.25/openraft/src/config/config.rs. openraft/src/raft/mod.rs at the same tag has `pub async fn ensure_linearizable(&self) -> Result<Option<LogId>, RaftError<CheckIsLeaderError>>` (is_leader is deprecated in its favour since 0.9.0), plus Raft::runtime_config().elect()/heartbeat() for runtime toggling (the older Raft::enable_elect()/enable_heartbeat() methods exist but are deprecated since 0.8.4): https://raw.githubusercontent.com/databendlabs/openraft/v0.9.25/openraft/src/raft/mod.rs. In core/raft_core.rs, enable_elect is consulted only in handle_tick_election() (early return); RaftMsg::RequestVote -> handle_vote_request -> engine.handle_vote_req is not gated by it, and membership/quorum is determined solely by the voter set in Membership, which the flag does not touch. So a non-electing voter still grants votes and counts toward quorum. Caveats: (1) Trigger::elect() (ExternalCommand::Elect) bypasses enable_elect, so an operator call can still make the witness campaign; (2) ensure_linearizable() in 0.9 is a read-index that round-trips heartbeats to a quorum, not a lease read, so any "zero-fsync lease" is OpenTAP's own layer on top, not an openraft guarantee; (3) semantics are documented only by code, not by a stability promise, and 0.10 restructures these APIs.
- Design impact: The non-electable witness design is sound on openraft 0.9.25: set enable_elect=false on the witness via Config or runtime_config().elect(false), never expose Trigger::elect() to it, and pin to 0.9.x. Treat the lease/zero-fsync read path as OpenTAP-owned logic validated against ensure_linearizable(), which itself costs a quorum heartbeat round-trip per call.

### [CONSENSUS-2] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with high confidence by Opus-Consensus)
- Claim: rpc.nfsd in nfs-utils supports -S/--scope to set the NFSv4.1 server scope so two nodes can present an identical scope for the same SVM, and nfsd/mountd can run per network namespace.
- Correction: rpc.nfsd -S/--scope (and nfs.conf [nfsd] scope=) exists only since nfs-utils 2.6.3 (NeilBrown commit 580ac005, 2022-11-21; released 2023-04-20). Debian 13 trixie ships nfs-utils 2.8.3, which has it; Debian 12 bookworm ships 1:2.6.2-4+deb12u1, whose rpc.nfsd has no -S (tagged source lacks "scope"; man page lacks it) and there is no bookworm-backports nfs-utils. The kernel side (nfsd_net->nfsd_name taken from the starting process's UTS nodename, used for both server_scope and server_owner.major_id) has existed since v5.7, so on bookworm 6.1 the same effect is obtained with `unshare -u; hostname <scope>; rpc.nfsd` (what rpc.nfsd does internally). On 6.12 with nfs-utils 2.8, scope goes over nfsd netlink (NFSD_A_SERVER_SCOPE) but only from nfs.conf via nfsdctl autostart, not from the `nfsdctl threads` CLI. Scope is per-netns and fixed while nfsd runs in that netns. Per-netns nfsd is real (nfsd_net, nfsd fs keyed to netns, per-netns DRC since 5.3), and rpc.mountd/rpcbind have no netns flag: they simply must be started inside the netns with their own /proc/fs/nfsd mount and state dir. Identical scope alone does not yield reclaim: the nfsdcld client-tracking DB must be replicated and a grace period entered on the takeover node.
- Evidence: nfs-utils master nfsd.c: `if (scope) { unshare(CLONE_NEWUTS); sethostname(scope,...) }` (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/linux-nfs/nfs-utils/master/utils/nfsd/nfsd.c); adding commit https://github.com/linux-nfs/nfs-utils/commit/580ac005d11ddb9a9622e380ba8e947ad743a38f; 2.6.2 tag source has no scope (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/linux-nfs/nfs-utils/nfs-utils-2-6-2/utils/nfsd/nfsd.c); bookworm man page lacks it (https://manpages.debian.org/bookworm/nfs-kernel-server/nfsd.8.en.html); Debian versions https://tracker.debian.org/pkg/nfs-utils; kernel v5.7 commit 7627d7dc (https://patchwork.kernel.org/patch/11392443/); v6.1 nfs4xdr.c major_id/server_scope = nn->nfsd_name; v6.12 Documentation/netlink/specs/nfsd.yaml has server.scope; nfsdctl.c uses NFSD_A_SERVER_SCOPE from nfs.conf only; man page https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man8/rpc.nfsd.8.html.
- Design impact: Design holds on trixie; on bookworm OpenTAP must set the scope itself (UTS unshare + hostname before rpc.nfsd, or pin trixie as the base). One netns per SVM is mandatory since scope is per-netns, and reclaim additionally requires replicating /var/lib/nfs/nfsdcld (and stable fsid= exports) to the partner, which is the real load-bearing piece, not the flag.

### [CONSENSUS-3] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with medium confidence by Opus-Consensus)
- Claim: Linux nfsd ends the grace period early once all NFSv4.1+ clients have sent RECLAIM_COMPLETE (or userspace writes /proc/fs/nfsd/v4_end_grace), enforces grace >= lease, and this early-end path works on Debian's 6.12 kernel.
- Correction: On Linux 6.1 (bookworm), 6.12 (bookworm-backports, trixie) and 6.12-stable: (1) nfsd ends grace early in inc_reclaim_complete() only when client tracking is nfsdcld (nn->track_reclaim_completes is set in nfs4_cld_state_init; never with nfsdcltrack or the legacy recovery dir) AND nr_reclaim_complete == reclaim_str_hashtbl_size, i.e. every client recorded in the previous nfsdcld epoch must return and send RECLAIM_COMPLETE. One absent client, or any NFSv4.0 client (never sends RECLAIM_COMPLETE), forces the full grace, extensible to 2x grace by clients_still_reclaiming(). Debian bookworm (nfs-utils 1:2.6.2) and trixie (1:2.8.3) ship nfsdcld and nfs-server.service has Wants=/After=nfsdcld.service, so the path is active by default. (2) Writing Y/y/1 to /proc/fs/nfsd/v4_end_grace ends nfsd's grace (EBUSY if nfsd not running) but only nfsd's manager. (3) grace >= lease is NOT enforced: nfsctl.c says "we don't try to enforce this"; only a 10..3600 s range check, and both are writable only while nfsd is stopped. (4) locks_in_grace() is a per-netns list shared with lockd: if NFSv3 is enabled, lockd's grace (nlm_grace_period, default nlm_timeout*5 = 50 s; rpc.nfsd --grace-time also writes it) keeps non-reclaim NFSv4 OPENs failing with NFS4ERR_GRACE regardless of nfsd's early end. (5) If the takeover node's nfsdcld DB has no records, grace is skipped entirely and returning clients get NFS4ERR_NO_GRACE and lose locks. A May-2026 fix (dedup nfs4_client_to_reclaim, Fixes: 362063a595be) claims the fast path could never fire, but on 6.12 the only nfsdcld insert path is the GraceStart enumeration from an id-PRIMARY-KEY table, so that failure looks legacy-only; verify in testing.
- Evidence: v6.12 fs/nfsd/nfs4state.c (inc_reclaim_complete, clients_still_reclaiming, nfs4_state_start_net skip_grace, nfs4_client_to_reclaim), fs/nfsd/nfs4recover.c (nfs4_cld_state_init sets track_reclaim_completes; GraceStart downcall is the only nfsdcld insert), fs/nfsd/nfsctl.c (write_gracetime comment, __nfsd4_write_time, write_v4_end_grace), fs/nfs_common/grace.c, fs/lockd/svc.c, include/linux/lockd/lockd.h (LOCKD_DFLT_TIMEO 10): https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/fs/nfsd/... Debian: https://packages.debian.org/trixie/amd64/nfs-kernel-server/filelist, https://sources.debian.org/data/main/n/nfs-utils/1:2.8.3-1/systemd/nfs-server.service, .../utils/nfsd/nfssvc.c, .../utils/nfsdcld/sqlite.c. 2026 patches: https://lkml.indiana.edu/2605.3/08864.html, https://lkml.indiana.edu/2605.3/10703.html (Chuck Lever review).
- Design impact: The 8 s p99 cannot rest on the RECLAIM_COMPLETE tally: it is defeated by one dead or v4.0 client, by lockd's 50 s grace if NFSv3 is on, and the 10 s floor on grace already exceeds 8 s. OpenTAP must replicate /var/lib/nfs/nfsdcld to the partner, run NFSv4-only (or set nlm_grace_period equally), configure grace=lease explicitly (the kernel will not), and have the failover controller write v4_end_grace once its own tally of live clients has reclaimed, accepting that this risks lock loss for stragglers.

### [CONSENSUS-4] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with medium confidence by Opus-Consensus)
- Claim: LIO's per-device pr/res_aptpl_metadata attribute is writable and force_pr_aptpl=1 makes LIO write PR metadata on every change, so persistent reservations can be re-created on the standby node before the LUN is exported; the store refuses while fabric exports are active.
- Correction: Mechanics are right, but details are wrong/missing. (1) The export gate is on the device's export_count (any LUN mapping of the backstore in any TPG), not "fabric exports active" in general; force_pr_aptpl has the same export_count gate, so both must be set before the backstore is mapped. (2) res_aptpl_metadata is write-only in effect: show() returns the fixed string "Ready to process PR APTPL metadata.."; the real metadata lives in a file $dbroot/pr/aptpl_<unit_serial> (dbroot defaults to /var/target), written synchronously by filp_open(O_RDWR|O_CREAT|O_TRUNC) inside the PR OUT command path; if the open/write fails the PR OUT returns LOGICAL UNIT COMMUNICATION FAILURE. OpenTAP must replicate that file to the standby itself (nothing in LIO does), and the standby backstore must carry the same vpd_unit_serial. (3) Each write to res_aptpl_metadata loads one registration (one PR_REG_START..PR_REG_END block, comma-joined tokens, as rtslib's _config_pr_aptpl already does on targetcli restore); the entry stays pending in aptpl_reg_list and is activated only when a matching LUN ACL (initiator name, target WWN, tpgt, target LUN, mapped LUN) is created via core_dev_add_initiator_node_lun_acl. (4) PR generation is not saved. (5) For iSCSI the saved initiator_sid is restored with isid_present_at_reg=1, and __core_scsi3_locate_pr_reg then requires the new session's ISID to match; a reconnecting Windows initiator with a different ISID will not find its registration. Omit the initiator_sid token on replay (registration then matches any session of that ACL). All of this is identical in Debian 12's 6.1 and 6.12 (bookworm-backports/trixie); force_pr_aptpl dates from 3.19.
- Evidence: Kernel v6.12 and v6.1 target_core_configfs.c: target_pr_res_aptpl_metadata_store returns -EINVAL "while active fabric exports exist" when dev->export_count != 0; force_pr_aptpl_store same gate; show prints "Ready to process..". target_core_pr.c: target_scsi3_emulate_pr_out sets aptpl=1 if force_pr_aptpl; __core_scsi3_write_aptpl_to_file path "%s/pr/aptpl_%s" of db_root; update_and_write_aptpl returns TCM_LOGICAL_UNIT_COMMUNICATION_FAILURE on write failure; alloc_aptpl_registration restores ISID; __core_scsi3_locate_pr_reg strcmp isid; check_aptpl_registration matches on iport/tport/tpgt/lun/mapped_lun. target_core_device.c: core_dev_add_initiator_node_lun_acl calls core_scsi3_check_aptpl_registration. rtslib-fb tcm.py _config_pr_aptpl replays dbroot/pr/aptpl_<wwn>. Commit 92404e609a2d (Bellinger, Oct 2014) states the H/A active/passive intent. https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/drivers/target/target_core_configfs.c ; .../target_core_pr.c ; .../target_core_device.c ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/open-iscsi/rtslib-fb/master/rtslib/tcm.py ; https://android-kvm.googlesource.com/linux/+/92404e609a2dffc55a9a22540ed48b6f0edc9c59
- Design impact: OpenTAP must own the replication: watch/sync $dbroot/pr/aptpl_<serial> (or intercept it via a dedicated dbroot on a replicated path) to the partner with every PR change, keep identical unit serials, and on takeover set force_pr_aptpl and replay the blocks (stripping initiator_sid for iSCSI) before creating the LUN ACLs. The synchronous file write sits in the PR OUT data path, so dbroot must be on fast, always-present local storage, and PR generation/stale ISID registrations are accepted losses.

### [CONSENSUS-5] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with high confidence by Opus-Consensus)
- Claim: softdog in Linux 6.12 is an hrtimer on CLOCK_MONOTONIC whose expiry calls emergency_restart() (or panic() with soft_panic=1), so it cannot fire during an IRQ-disabled hard lockup on its CPU, a firmware/SMI hang or a paused VM, and it cannot stop HBA DMA already queued.
- Correction: Mechanics are right for 6.1, 6.12-backports and trixie 6.12: softdog.c arms a CLOCK_MONOTONIC HRTIMER_MODE_REL hrtimer; softdog_fire() does panic() if soft_panic, else emergency_restart(). Caveats: (1) if soft_reboot_cmd is set it first schedules an orderly kernel_restart() and only falls back to emergency_restart() one more soft_margin later. (2) The hrtimer is not PINNED, so it migrates between CPUs on each ping (get_target_base); only a hard lockup on whichever CPU currently holds it blocks it, and an IRQ-enabled soft lockup does not block it (hard-IRQ expiry). On Debian's -rt flavour (PREEMPT_RT) non-HARD hrtimers expire in softirq/ktimers context and can be starved by RT tasks. (3) soft_panic=1 on Debian is a trap: CONFIG_PANIC_TIMEOUT=0, so the node hangs forever (HBA stays logged into the fabric, posted DMA completes) unless kernel.panic>0 is set. (4) emergency_restart() does no machine_shutdown/PCI/DMA quiesce, but a successful platform reset (ACPI/KBC/CF9/triple fault) does reset the HBA within ms; the unbounded case is only when the reset itself fails. (5) The IRQ-off hard-lockup gap is partly closable: Debian ships CONFIG_HARDLOCKUP_DETECTOR=y with BOOTPARAM_HARDLOCKUP_PANIC unset; kernel.hardlockup_panic=1 + kernel.panic=N gives an NMI-driven path softdog lacks. SMI and paused-VM gaps stand.
- Evidence: softdog.c v6.12 (hrtimer_init CLOCK_MONOTONIC/REL, softdog_fire body, soft_reboot_cmd): https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/drivers/watchdog/softdog.c. hrtimer migration and PREEMPT_RT soft expiry: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/kernel/time/hrtimer.c. native_machine_emergency_restart skips machine_shutdown: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.12/arch/x86/kernel/reboot.c. Debian config (SOFT_WATCHDOG=m, PANIC_TIMEOUT=0, HARDLOCKUP_DETECTOR=y, HARDLOCKUP_PANIC unset): https://salsa.debian.org/kernel-team/linux/-/raw/debian/latest/debian/config/config (debian/latest branch; these values have been unchanged across bookworm/trixie). Lockup-watchdog semantics: https://docs.kernel.org/admin-guide/lockup-watchdogs.html.
- Design impact: The plex-only policy for softdog is sound, but OpenTAP must ship a sysctl/modprobe profile: never soft_panic=1 without kernel.panic=N, never soft_reboot_cmd, and set kernel.hardlockup_panic=1 to close the IRQ-off case softdog cannot. Shelf mode still needs SCSI-3 PR or power fencing for SMI/VM-pause/failed-reset cases, and the partner takeover delay should be budgeted as soft_margin plus platform-reset time, not softdog alone.

### [CONSENSUS-6] CONFIRMED (claimed with medium confidence by Opus-Consensus)
- Claim: Debian 13 trixie ships Linux 6.12 LTS and its linux-image-amd64 builds softdog, iTCO_wdt, sp5100_tco, ipmi_watchdog and hpwdt as modules, while ksmbd durable handles v1/v2 (merged in 6.9) are therefore present in trixie and absent in bookworm's 6.1.
- Evidence: Trixie kernel: sources.debian.org lists linux 6.12.107-1 in trixie (6.12.111-1 in proposed-updates); bookworm has 6.1.176-1 and bookworm-backports 6.12.95-1~bpo12+1 (https://sources.debian.org/api/src/linux/). Debian config for the debian/6.12/trixie branch (https://salsa.debian.org/kernel-team/linux/-/raw/debian/6.12/trixie/debian/config/config): CONFIG_WATCHDOG=y, CONFIG_SOFT_WATCHDOG=m, CONFIG_ITCO_WDT=m, CONFIG_SMB_SERVER=m; and kernelarch-x86/config: CONFIG_SP5100_TCO=m, CONFIG_IPMI_WATCHDOG=m, CONFIG_HP_WATCHDOG=m. All five watchdog drivers are modules on amd64, and ksmbd is built. Durable handles: Phoronix and the 6.9 ksmbd pull ("ksmbd: add support for durable handles v1/v2", Namjae Jeon) confirm merge in 6.9 (https://www.phoronix.com/news/Linux-6.9-KSMBD-Durable-Handles). Bookworm 6.1 config (https://sources.debian.org/data/main/l/linux/6.1.176-1/debian/config/config) has CONFIG_SMB_SERVER=m, so ksmbd exists there but its 6.1 code predates durable handles. Minor notes, not errors: bookworm-backports' 6.12 kernel also carries the durable-handle code, so trixie is not strictly required for it; ksmbd-tools in trixie is 3.5.3-1; the durable v2 path has 2026 CVEs (CVE-2026-23427 UAF in durable v2 replay, CVE-2026-52996 leak in parse_durable_handle_context) that trixie point releases must carry.
- Design impact: Phase 1 on trixie is sound: every named watchdog loads as a module and ksmbd with durable v1/v2 handles is available without a custom kernel. Two design caveats remain: ksmbd durable handles only let a client reconnect to the same server instance within the timeout, not transparently to the HA partner (no persistent/CA handles or cross-node state), so the SMB failover contract must be "reconnect with reopen", not "transparent"; and the durable-v2 CVEs mean OpenTAP should track trixie-security kernels closely if ksmbd is exposed.

### [CONSENSUS-7] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with medium confidence by Opus-Consensus)
- Claim: Windows iSCSI defaults are MaxRequestHoldTime 60 s (non-MPIO) and LinkDownTime 15 s (MPIO); ESXi marks an NFS datastore unavailable after HeartbeatFrequency x HeartbeatMaxFailures + HeartbeatTimeout seconds (about 125 s at the NetApp-recommended 12/10/5) and holds rather than fails VM I/O; ESXi's stock iSCSI stack pauses about 35 s on a silent target.
- Correction: Numbers are right; the framing conflates detection with hold time. (1) Windows: MaxRequestHoldTime default 60 s, LinkDownTime default 15 s (used only when MPIO is installed) are confirmed by Microsoft's iSCSI timer reference. But they are how long I/O is queued AFTER the TCP connection is known lost; the Windows initiator sends no NOP-Out by default (EnableNOPOut=0, and even then only after 2 min idle), so a silent half-open target is detected by TCP retransmission exhaustion or the disk TimeOutValue (60 s) + SRBTimeoutDelta (15 s), not by an iSCSI timer. After the hold time the disk is removed ("error no device"), not merely an I/O error. (2) ESXi NFSv3: Broadcom KB 323325 gives defaults 12/10/5 (plus HeartbeatDelta 5) and the formula min = 12*(10-1)+5 = 113 s, max = 113+5+12 = 130 s; "125 s" is Cormac Hogan's rounded figure. These are ESXi stock defaults, not NetApp-specific tuning. Holding VM I/O is correct: under APD, ESXi fails only non-VM I/O after Misc.APDTimeout (140 s) and retries VM I/O indefinitely. NFS 4.1 uses a different (session/lease) mechanism. (3) ESXi software iSCSI: NoopOutInterval 15 + NoopOutTimeout 10 + RecoveryTimeout 10 = 35 s is the worst case to mark a path dead (10-35 s depending on phase). The "35 s pause" is observed only when an alternate live path exists; if all paths are silent the LUN enters APD and VM I/O is held, same as NFS.
- Evidence: Microsoft iSCSI timers reference (MaxRequestHoldTime 60, LinkDownTime 15/MPIO, EnableNOPOut 0, SRBTimeoutDelta 15): https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/archive/blogs/san/microsoft-iscsi-software-initiator-and-isns-server-timers-quick-reference ; NetApp KB confirming MaxRequestHoldTime ignored with MPIO: https://kb.netapp.com/on-prem/ontap/da/SAN/SAN-KBs/What_are_the_parameters_that_control_how_MS_iSCSI_survives_lost_TCP_connections_without_causing_applications_harm ; Broadcom KB 323325 NFS heartbeat defaults and 113/130 s formula: https://knowledge.broadcom.com/external/article/323325 ; Cormac Hogan 125 s derivation: https://cormachogan.com/2012/11/27/nfs-best-practices-part-2-advanced-settings/ ; Dell KB 000418707 on the 35 s ESXi iSCSI pause (15+10+10): https://www.dell.com/support/kbdoc/en-au/000418707/esxi-hosts-using-iscsi-protocol ; VMware vSphere 8 iSCSI adapter params: https://docs.vmware.com/en/VMware-vSphere/8.0/vsphere-storage/GUID-7FCA31F2-FA13-4BFD-8057-5A36DC3FBC14.html
- Design impact: The RST proxy keeps its value: ESXi iSCSI detects silence in 10-35 s but Windows (no NOP-Out) relies on TCP retransmit/SCSI timeouts, so an immediate RST on takeover is what makes Windows clients reconnect inside their 15/60 s hold windows instead of losing the disk. Budget Windows+MPIO failover at under 15 s (or document raising LinkDownTime/PDORemovePeriod), and treat ESXi APD as hold-not-fail for both NFS and all-paths-dead iSCSI, so a failover of up to ~110 s is survivable for ESXi but not for untuned Windows.

### [CONSENSUS-8] PARTIALLY-CORRECT (claimed with high confidence by Opus-Consensus)
- Claim: Neither Samba 4.17/4.22 nor ksmbd replicates SMB open-handle (durable or persistent) state across nodes, so after an IP migration a client's durable reconnect fails and files open at the instant of failover receive errors, while idle sessions reconnect transparently.
- Correction: True for the versions named and for everything Debian 12/13 ship, but the blanket "neither Samba" is now outdated. Samba 4.17.12 (bookworm), 4.22.6/4.22.10 (bookworm-backports/trixie) and 4.24.7 (trixie-backports) do not implement SMB3 persistent handles; durable handles are "single-server, not intended for clusters" (in CTDB the smbXsrv_open_global record is a volatile clustered DB, so a reconnect on another node is attempted after a planned ctdb moveip but is lost on a node crash), and CA shares are not advertised by default. Samba >=4.20 (so trixie's 4.22) does ship the MS-SWN Witness service (rpcd_witness, CTDB-only), which only speeds client re-targeting. Samba 4.25.0 (2026-09-24; Debian sid/forky only) adds experimental SMB3 persistent handles whose state is replicated to every CTDB node ("persistent handles durability" = partial_outage/full_outage), surviving node crashes; requires CTDB, "continuous availability" shares with kernel oplocks/kernel share modes/posix locking = no (no NFS/POSIX interop), and an fsync per handle change. ksmbd (6.1: no durable handles; 6.9+/6.12: durable v1/v2) lists persistent handles as "planned" and has no clustering at all.
- Evidence: Samba 4.25 WHATSNEW (https://salix.mirror.garr.it/samba/rc/samba-4.25.0rc1.WHATSNEW.txt) and smb.conf(5) "persistent handles" / "persistent handles durability" (https://www.samba.org/samba/docs/current/man-html/smb.conf.5.html); 4.20 notes on witness and "persistent handles not implemented" (https://www.samba.org/samba/history/samba-4.20.0.html); Debian versions (https://tracker.debian.org/pkg/samba); CTDB failover user-visible errors, persistent handles absent pre-4.25 (https://lists.samba.org/archive/samba/2024-May/248670.html); durable reconnect across CTDB nodes attempted (https://bugzilla.samba.org/show_bug.cgi?id=13596); SambaXP 2017 "durable handles not for clusters"; ksmbd feature table (https://www.kernel.org/doc/html/latest/filesystems/smb/ksmbd.html); ksmbd durable v1/v2 in 6.9 (https://mail.phoronix.com/news/Linux-6.9-KSMBD-Durable-Handles).
- Design impact: With Debian-packaged Samba or ksmbd, SMB failover stays disruptive for files open at the instant of the IP move, so the "block flips before NAS IP moves" ordering and the honest "disruptive SMB" labelling stand. A credible non-disruptive path exists only by running Samba 4.25+ with CTDB (two nodes plus the witness as recovery lock arbiter), Witness, and CA shares that are SMB-exclusive, which OpenTAP should scope as an optional, experimental, SMB-only share class rather than the default.

## Round 3: revised positions

### Opus-Fabrics

#### Concessions
Binding corrections and critiques I accept:

- **FABRICS-4 (reversed).** jonhoo `ibverbs` 0.9.2 is the legacy `ibv_create_qp`/`ibv_post_send` crate and runs on mlx4; only sideway's `build_ex()` is CX-3-incompatible. Choose on ergonomics (sideway: rdmacm plus both paths); probe `ibv_wr_*` at runtime as a fast lane, never a requirement.
- **FABRICS-8.** An FCP write is four frames, eight firmware traversals and 3-4 kernel hops; QD1 4 KiB put-to-Placed on 8GFC/LIO is 80-200 us, RoCE ~10 us median. Criteria revised.
- **FABRICS-6.** trixie ships ql2500_fw.bin 8.07.00 (8.08.207 is sid-only); QLE2672 is ISP83xx, flash-first like 27xx. Extra consequence: `qla24xx_load_risc` never compares versions, so an 8G card with newer flash firmware is silently downgraded to Debian's blob unless `ql2xfwloadbin=1`.
- **FABRICS-7.** Switchless Linux-to-Linux target-to-initiator N2N is public since 2015 with YADRO's 2019 fixes; only symmetric dual mode is unreported. First fallback is asymmetric two-link (each port one direction, second card for clients); a switch only after that.
- **FABRICS-2.** FCLOOP/LOOP were irrelevant; ublk alone disqualifies stock 6.1. Floor: "Debian 6.12.x, trixie or bookworm-backports".
- **Storage 1, Protocols 2.** Whole-block descriptor rewrites withdrawn: out-of-order tags or WRs can erase a Placed header, QD1 commands double, quarantine widens to 64 slots.
- **Storage 2, Protocols.** BUSY does not exist through ublk, iblock or nvmet; back-pressure is held requests under Storage's throttle cap plus shorter CPs, and ring sizing follows that model.
- **Storage 3, Protocols 5.** TCP readv and ack move into `otap-ringrecv` beside the ring-holder unit; a dbladed restart never becomes GrantUnmirrored.
- **Storage 4.** By-reference plex ships ~5% of CP bytes; the second bulk link is withdrawn.
- **Storage 5, Consensus 5.** Spill-on-silence is manual-tier only, written as foreign-tagged rate-limited segments via `Backstore::write_segment`; its trigger commits `NeedsReplayFrom{B, range}` in Raft so a rebooted A cannot export before applying it.
- **Consensus 2.** `panic=5 nowayout=1` stacks to ~13 s and reboots on planned restarts: use `panic=-1`, softdog without `soft_panic`, `nowayout=0`, otap-ctl refusing the magic close while owning an aggregate.
- **Consensus 3.** Lease loss on a healthy owner: stop exporting at TTL, keep petting, alarm; reboot only for stuck workers.
- **Consensus 4.** Prefix replay drops an acked record behind an Unknown one; takeover replays every CRC-valid record above the last CP in seq order, and S4 is judged by the ACK-history checker.
- **Consensus 6, Protocols 4.** Quiesce order, per-port `qlini_mode`, and a flip driven by the heartbeat timer that never waits for SCSI EH.
- **Protocols 1.** My latencies are in-process put-to-Placed; client-visible targets are the N-Blade's. "RoCE buys a core, not latency" stands, so TCP is built before RDMA.
- **Consensus 1, Protocols 3.** A ring mailbox is a required `ControlLink` implementation; "FC never the sole link" becomes install-guide advice.

#### Holds firm
- **fileio-on-tmpfs, not TCMU.** FABRICS-8's aside conflicts with FABRICS-5, confirmed in the same check: HBA DMA into SGL pages, one kernel memcpy into shmem pages, daemon mmap, no userspace copy. The tmpfs file *is* the cache memory. TCMU adds a second kernel copy into the uio data area plus a wakeup per command, and that area cannot be the cache because tcmu recycles it on completion. Fileio stays; the 80-200 us figure is accepted on top of it.
- **One put/ack trait with capability bits.** Placed means bytes in partner ring pages on every leg; who produced the ack is `Caps::placed_by`. Consensus's `Indexed` is a mailbox-reported watermark, not a put ack; the mailbox is a second `ControlLink` implementation, not a second mirror trait.
- **FC ships in Phase 2.** 80-200 us, a 10 s unknown-fate bound, initiator EH quiescing the shared dual-mode host in both directions, and unproven symmetric dual mode. Phase 1 runs S1/S2 on the critical path so Phase 2 is build, not research.
- **Trixie 6.12 baseline.** bookworm-backports is the same source and tolerated; trixie-backports 7.2.x (tracker snapshot in the FABRICS-1 check) is an optional lane for ublk zero-copy and RECV_ZC once verified, never the floor.
- **tmpfs `huge=always` for every inbound ring file**, however Storage partitions them: hugetlbfs cannot back fileio, and a fallback flip must never change the file.
- **Dedicated mirror port**, now stronger: mirror port `dual`, client ports `disabled`.
- **ConnectX-3 stays supported**; the correction made it easier.
- **Active/standby links, manual FC fail-back, 1 s timer-driven flip with quarantine.** The bound is transport data in `Caps`.
- **TCP core cost is bounded, not zero.** io_uring NAPI busy-poll (6.9, present in 6.12) spins only while work is pending: a core while busy, not forever. That, not latency, is why RDMA remains the recommended Ethernet leg on 8C boxes.

#### Revised design changes
**Record format (I own it unless reassigned).** Slot = 4 KiB. A batch is `[descriptor page | payloads]`, contiguous in the partner's file, written exactly once by one link: page header `{magic, ver, page_crc, link_id, batch_seq, owner_epoch, n}` plus up to 63 dense 64 B record headers (round-1 fields). One SCSI WRITE, one writev, one or two WRs per batch; QD1 costs 8 KiB on the wire (TCP may send only used header bytes, `otap-ringrecv` pads). The scanner accepts records individually on header and payload CRC, walks slots in order, rejects `owner_epoch` below current. Slot reuse is gated by max(owner committed `cp_seq`, partner applied `cp_seq` under plex), both carried in the 20 ms heartbeat.

**Files and receiver.** One tmpfs `huge=always,noswap` file per (aggregate, direction), mlocked by the ring-holder, mailbox region at its head, registered once for io_uring and per RDMA device in 1 GiB chunks, exported as the FC LUN with `fd_buffered_io=1 block_size=4096`. `otap-ringrecv` (TCP readv plus ack, scanner, 10 ms mailbox poll) lives with the ring-holder; dbladed only mmaps.

**Topology default.** Direct-attached mirror link (DAC or FC cable) on its own port; ControlLink primary is mTLS over that link's IP and the LAN; the ring mailbox is the fabric-agnostic last resort. A dead LAN switch then costs only the witness, and A+B keep 2-of-3.

**FC.** Module `qlini_mode=dual`; sysfs mirror port `dual`, client ports `disabled`; udev mirror sd `timeout=2`, rport `fast_io_fail_tmo=1 dev_loss_tmo=5`; `fw_version` floor per family (25xx: 8.07.00 blob or flash via `ql2xfwloadbin=1`; 83xx/27xx set by S1); asymmetric two-link is configuration only.

**Timers and fencing.** Flip at 1 s link-dead; in-flight tokens resolve Unknown and quarantine for `unknown_fate_bound`. Cmdline `panic=-1 hardlockup_panic=1 softlockup_panic=1`, softdog `soft_margin=5`, iTCO/sp5100_tco preferred.

**Revised put-to-Placed (QD1 4 KiB / 64 KiB).** 25GbE RoCE 8-12 / ~30 us; FDR CX-3 8-14 / 20-25; busy-polled 10GbE TCP 25-40 / 80-100 (p99 several times p50); 8GFC/LIO 80-200 / 160-280; 16GFC 70-170 / 110-220.

```rust
pub type Slot = u32;                                   // 4 KiB unit in the partner's inbound file
pub struct Batch { pub first: Slot, pub nslots: u32, pub local: RingRef }  // [desc page | payloads], written once
#[derive(Clone, Copy)] pub enum PlacedBy { Hardware, PeerProcess }        // SCSI GOOD / RC ACK vs otap-ringrecv
#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
pub struct Caps {
    pub max_batch_bytes: u32,          // FC 8 MiB (FD_MAX_BYTES); RDMA MR bound; TCP 8 MiB by convention
    pub max_inflight: u32,
    pub placed_by: PlacedBy,
    pub reject_advisory: bool,         // only PeerProcess legs can NAK before placement
    pub unknown_fate_bound: Duration,  // TCP/RDMA ~0 after close or QP->ERR; FC R_A_TOV 10 s unless ABTS confirmed
    pub carries_mailbox: bool,
}
pub enum Ack { Placed, Rejected, Unknown }
pub trait MirrorLink: Send {
    fn caps(&self) -> Caps;
    fn put(&mut self, b: Batch) -> Result<PutToken, PutError>;                // non-blocking; Err(Full) at max_inflight
    fn heartbeat(&mut self, hb: &HeartbeatRec) -> Result<PutToken, PutError>; // 20 ms; carries committed/applied cp_seq
    fn poll(&mut self, out: &mut Vec<(PutToken, Ack)>) -> usize;              // each token resolves exactly once
    fn health(&self) -> LinkHealth;
    fn fence_and_close(&mut self) -> Result<(), LinkError>;                   // after Ok no late write can land
}
pub trait MirrorInbound {                                                     // per (aggregate, direction) file
    fn mapping(&self) -> &RingMapping;
    fn scan(&mut self, cur: &mut ScanCursor, min_epoch: u64, out: &mut Vec<RecordLoc>) -> usize;
    fn quiesce(&mut self) -> Result<(), LinkError>;   // unmap LUN / QP->ERR / close, drain, then one residual scan
}
pub trait ControlLink: Send {                          // impls: TcpMtls, RingMailbox (reserved region of any inbound file)
    fn send(&mut self, m: CtlMsg) -> Result<(), CtlError>;
    fn recv(&mut self, out: &mut Vec<CtlMsg>) -> usize;
    fn peer_answered_since(&self) -> Instant;          // answered RPC is liveness; hardware acks never are
}
```

#### Final Phase 1 recommendation
Build on trixie 6.12, in order: (1) `otap-ring` crate: batch format, CRCs, scanner, all-valid replay above the last CP, property tests for reordering, duplicates, torn batches and epoch flips, plus the ACK-history model for Consensus's checker. (2) TCP leg: io_uring with NAPI registration, SEND_ZC above 16 KiB, `otap-ringrecv`; direct-attach DAC default. (3) RDMA leg on `ibv_post_send` (rxe in CI, CX-3 and CX-4 Lx in the lab), runtime-probed `ibv_wr_*`. (4) `RingMailbox` ControlLink over both legs, fallback/fail-back, udev/modprobe/sysctl/cmdline drop-ins shipped as package files. (5) FC spikes S1/S2 only; the FC leg ships in Phase 2.

Spikes and pass criteria:
- **S1 N2N:** QLE2562 and QLE2692 pairs, symmetric dual mode; 100 cable pulls, re-login <5 s, no oops; QD1 4 KiB put p50 <200 us (8G). If symmetric fails, asymmetric two-link must pass the same bar. Record `fw_version` under both load policies.
- **S2 tcm_loop + fileio(write_back) on tmpfs huge=always:** 1 M batches; every GOOD followed by a CRC-valid record in the mapping within 10 us; LIO thread >=2 GB/s; scanner <1% of a core at 1 ms sleep.
- **S3 RDMA:** 4 KiB WRITE CQE p50 <12 us CX-3, <10 us CX-4 Lx; 64 KiB >=4 GB/s FDR, >=2.8 GB/s 25G; QP->ERR flushes all WRs within 10 ms; an 8 GiB tmpfs MR registers on mlx4 in 1 GiB chunks.
- **S4 flip:** primary at 80% line rate, cable pull; secondary carries puts within 1 s; forced takeover; checker finds every client-acked write; fail-back with no stall >2 s; kill `otap-ringrecv` and dbladed on the partner, puts resume within Storage's 2 s re-attach budget with no GrantUnmirrored.
- **S5 LAN-dead quorum:** unplug both nodes' LAN; A+B keep 2-of-3 over the mailbox, no fence, no takeover, no epoch bump on restore.

Success criteria (measurement point stated): in-process put-to-Placed QD1 4 KiB p50 <=10 us RoCE, <=35 us 10GbE TCP (p99 <=150 us); my share of the client-visible TCP path <=40 us; 10GbE line-rate ingest with back-to-back CPs on a 12-drive RAID6 for 10 minutes with no held request past the throttle cap; Ethernet-only pair with LAN dead: A stops exporting at 3 s, B promotes within 10 s, zero acked writes lost.

#### Top risks
1. **Symmetric dual-mode N2N is unexercised anywhere public.** Failure forces the two-port asymmetric layout (second card per node) or a switch; S1 decides the Phase 2 hardware list and must run in week 1.
2. **FC firmware is outside our control.** 16G/32G cards run flash-resident firmware with no Debian blob, and 8G cards get Debian's 8.07.00 regardless of flash. Used-market cards arrive with anything; the minimum-firmware floor and Marvell flash tooling are a support burden nobody has sized.
3. **Ring file and format ownership.** Three specialists hold three slot maps; the per-batch format, tmpfs-only files and mailbox region need one owner now or the TCP, RDMA and FC legs diverge and a fallback flip changes the file.
4. **Dual-mode port EH coupling.** One ISP abort on the mirror port stalls both mirror directions for 2-10 s and leaves up to 10 s of unknown-fate quarantine; if the secondary is TCP through the LAN switch, it is also the only mailbox path during that window.
5. **CPU budget on 4-core boxes.** NAPI busy-poll bounds idle cost, not loaded cost; a TCP link under sustained ingest still owns a core per side while LIO, nvmet, nfsd and ksmbd compete. The 8C/16T floor must be a stated requirement, not a hope.

### Opus-Storage

#### Concessions
**Concessions**

- **Rings (Fabrics 1; STORAGE-4, -5).** The inbound ring is one tmpfs file per node (`huge=always`, swap off, `ShmemPmdMapped` verified), slots tagged by `aggregate_id`, per-aggregate quotas in the sender's allocator, and the FC fileio LUN *is* that file, so an FC-to-TCP re-put never changes files. The outbound arena stays hugetlbfs for the verdict's reason, not mine: 6.12 multi-hugepage coalescing needs uniform folios, entries are capped at 1 GiB, and cloning must precede any other registration. mlock on hugetlb is a no-op; dropped.
- **Daemon restart (Fabrics 2; Protocols 4, 5; Consensus 1).** The link dies with the daemon and "2 s" was unbudgeted. A ring-holder process owns the files, PD/MR/receiver QP (`dbladed` imports via `ibv_import_mr`), a persisted per-slot state page and the request-age page L4 reads. `/dev/ublkcN` lives in `dbladed`; `nbladed` is control-plane only. The restart budget enters the timer table.
- **Cut and replay (Consensus 2; Fabrics conflict 5).** S advances only over Placed records (or under GrantUnmirrored); replay above the committed cut is every valid Placed record, dedupe by seq. The contiguous-prefix rule is retired.
- **Plex (Fabrics 3; Consensus 3, 5).** Slot reuse needs a receiver credit under every variant; by-replay is the general case, by-reference a matched-geometry optimization; after dual loss authority is the Raft owner epoch, never max-select.
- **Backpressure (Protocols 1).** BUSY is unimplementable through iblock and my hung-disk "degraded service" is an EH storm. Stalled drives Transitioning on the owner TPG with a hard stop.
- **ROLLBACK (Protocols 2).** nvfail semantics: LUNs Inaccessible and NAS down until acknowledged.
- **512e everywhere (Protocols 3; STORAGE-6).** Internal NAS/S3 LUNs are 4Kn; RMW is an exception path.
- **`unmirrored_flush=cp` (Protocols 6).** LocalNvmeRing is the degraded device; `cp` only without a PLP NVMe.
- **Witness (Consensus 4).** One of three votes is one of three; the mirror-IP fallback only prevents a false partner death.
- **Drain (STORAGE-7).** 0.3-0.5 GB/s at 80% fill under uniform overwrite. Reserves move to 70/80/90, usable = 0.8 x physical, ring arithmetic uses the pessimistic drain.
- **Smaller (STORAGE-2, -3, -8; Fabrics 4, 5).** Generic amd64 image only, "6.12.x" as a range; discards whole-stripe only, `modprobe.d` plus initramfs, EOPNOTSUPP raises an alert; pin libublk =0.4.8; split-role FC costs both ports and is Phase 2; 6.15 zero-copy helps no leg I need, so `RingRef` is the only put source and trixie-backports is not a product tier.

#### Holds firm
**Holds firm**

- **WCE=1/VWC with FLUSH = Placed barrier.** Unchallenged; LocalNvmeRing keeps it true while degraded.
- **The ring is a file; a daemon crash is not a takeover.** The goal survives, the budget changes: with the slot-state page, restart is a header walk of non-Free slots (128 MiB per 8 GiB ring) plus an index rebuild, not a payload CRC scan.
- **Epoch-flip aggregate-wide cut.** Mechanism unchanged; only the choice of S is Placed-gated. No I/O pause, no per-volume barrier.
- **Never-overwrite CP on md with the A/B FUA superblock stripe, PBA-keyed read cache, `(vol, lba, seq)` segment summaries, CoW map with a flat Phase 1 image, XFS-on-LUN.** No critic disputed a mechanism; STORAGE-7 changed a number. Planned giveback with a clean CP and unmount is 5-10 s, not 2-4 s; the layer stays.
- **DRAM sets the CP interval.** Strengthened by STORAGE-7: slower drain fills ring/2 sooner. Consensus's 30 s retention (33 GB) is a derived figure, not a requirement: ring = DRAM budget, interval = ring/2 ÷ ingest.
- **Media-dependent HA.** A used shelf is the only 100%-capacity, credit-free block takeover, and the owner's lab can buy one; plex-by-replay is the SATA/consumer tier and moves to Phase 2 (Consensus is right it is the only HA proof there); manual elsewhere. The shelf fence is unverified, so it is the first spike.
- **Sender-chosen slots, one allocator.** Fabrics' link-private descriptor blocks batch header writes per link and Protocols' per-LUN `reserve_slot` is the same call; neither needs a second slot map.
- **Threading.** Thread-per-core ingest/CP reactors feed one mirror core over SPSC queues; that core busy-polls under load and NAPI-timeouts when idle. One model with a power knob, not three.

#### Revised design changes
**Design changes**

1. **Memory per node (64 GB):** 8 GiB hugetlbfs outbound arena plus 8 GiB tmpfs inbound ring, shared by all aggregates (128 GB: 16 + 16). Processes: `otap-ringd` (ring-holder), one `dbladed` per node with per-aggregate reactors holding every `/dev/ublkcN` (USER_COPY), `nbladed` (configfs, ALUA/ANA, reconciler), `otap-ctl`.
2. **Slot state page** in each ring file (`Free | Filled | Placed | Indexed | Committed | Quarantined`) plus the per-put acked bitmap. Restart target 2 s, hard 5 s; a supervised restart (systemd to `otap-ctl`) suspends L4 check-ins and raises Stalled to 5 s for that node while ublk holds I/O.
3. **Slot reuse = own CP committed AND partner `Released{agg, up_to_seq}`** over a dedicated ControlLink stream, in the ring contract for shelf and plex alike (on a shelf the credit is implicit in the committed cut). Ring sizing uses max(own CP, partner CP) plus credit latency; partner lag past ring/2 drives Transitioning.
4. **Stall ladder:** throttle delay capped at 2 s; Stalled 2 s → Transitioning; hard stop 10 s (Consensus) or 20 s (Protocols), the lead's call, handing to stall-to-release. Hung disks: SCT ERC 7 s, SCSI timeout 10 s, `eh_deadline` 5 s; whether md fails a member inside the hold is measured, not assumed.
5. **ROLLBACK = nvfail:** aggregate LUNs Inaccessible and NAS exports down until acknowledged; auto-clear opt-in.
6. **LUN classes:** `Esxi512e` (lbs 512, pbs 4 KiB, sector-mask patch records) and `Native4k` (internal XFS with `-s size=4096`, Linux and NVMe-oF hosts); never 4Kn to ESXi before 9.0, never HDD-backed 4Kn on 9.0.
7. **`LocalNvmeRing`** as a fourth `MirrorRing` (slot-addressed records on a raw PLP namespace, Placed = FUA completion), auto-selected under GrantUnmirrored.
8. **Capacity:** reserves 70/80/90, usable = 0.8 x physical, cost-benefit plus age cleaning with hot/cold streams; the published sustained random-overwrite figure for a 12-drive HDD pool is 0.3-0.5 GB/s at 80%.
9. **Discard:** flash pools only, whole segments (segments are stripe multiples), `options raid456 devices_handle_discard_safely=Y` in `modprobe.d` with an initramfs rebuild, RZAT checked at pool creation.
10. **Kernel:** trixie 6.12.x generic amd64 or bookworm plus backports; stock bookworm refused.
11. **Plex-by-replay:** the partner runs its own allocator over the inbound ring; rejoin resync is a logical diff by `birth_cp` from the survivor's map shipped as ordinary ring records with the seq taken before the read, so live writes win by seq and resync is the mirror path, not a second protocol.

```rust
#[repr(u8)] pub enum SlotState { Free, Filled, Placed, Indexed, Committed, Quarantined } // persisted page beside the ring
pub struct RingRef { pub slot: u32, pub lap: u32 }            // the only put source; ABA-safe
pub struct Cut { pub agg: AggId, pub seq: u64 }               // max seq with every record <= seq Placed, or GrantUnmirrored in force
pub struct Credit { pub agg: AggId, pub released_up_to: u64 } // partner -> owner over ControlLink; mandatory
pub trait SlotLifetime { fn reusable(&self, r: RingRef) -> bool; } // own_committed(seq) && partner_released(seq)
pub enum LunClass { Esxi512e, Native4k }
pub enum Stall { Ok, Transitioning { since: Instant }, Release }   // BUSY does not exist on this stack
pub enum DegradedDurability { LocalNvmeRing(NvmeNs), ForceCp }
pub enum RollbackState { Clean, NvFailed { cp_seq: CpSeq, window: Duration, est_bytes: u64 } } // LUNs offline until acked
```

#### Final Phase 1 recommendation
**Phase 1 (trixie 6.12, two nodes, TCP mirror, shelf for HDD, manual HA on flash)**

1. `otap-ring`: both files, slot-state page, re-attach without a CRC scan, per-aggregate quotas, `Credit`, `LocalNvmeRing`.
2. `otap-index`: Placed-gated cut, replay-all-valid, sector masks only for `Esxi512e`.
3. `otap-cp`: md RAID6 (12 HDD) and md RAID5 (4 NVMe) behind `Backstore` with two `MediaProfile`s; flat map image; A/B FUA superblock; whole-segment discard on flash.
4. In-process ublk in `dbladed` under LIO iSCSI, WCE=1, Transitioning as the only backpressure, nvfail rollback.
5. Checker and harness: `dm-log-writes`, QEMU cuts, PDU pulls, `dbladed` kills, hung-drive injection, aged-pool GC run.
6. Shelf spike: MD1200 plus 2 x 9300-8e, `sg_persist` preempt-abort through the EMMs, assemble time, `eh_deadline` behaviour on a pulled and a hung drive.

**Pass criteria**
- Recovery: 1,000 simulated and 50 real dual cuts; image = committed cut plus every Placed record, zero acked-write divergence against the checker, nvfail engaged in 100%, XFS mounts clean at every log-writes point.
- Restart: 50 `kill -9` at 1 GB/s; serving again with the mirror re-established p99 ≤ 2 s, worst ≤ 5 s, zero watchdog fires, zero lost acks, zero duplicate applies.
- HDD: ≥ 1.2 GB/s empty with zero member reads during a CP; a published drain curve with ≥ 0.4 GB/s at 70% and ≥ 0.3 GB/s at 80% under uniform 4 KiB overwrite.
- Backpressure: 2x drain offered for 10 min on ESXi 8, Windows 2022 and Linux; zero guest errors, no hold over the chosen bound; a hung drive failed by md inside the hold with no EH escalation past ABORT TASK.
- Flash: ≥ 2 GB/s and ≥ 300 K mirrored 4 KiB IOPS within 6 cores; discards reach the drives.
- Latency: D-Blade ack p50 ≤ 60 us, p99 ≤ 200 us on 10GbE TCP; client QD1 4 KiB p50 ≤ 200 us.
- Memory: RSS fixed after 24 h; boot-to-serving ≤ 10 s at 50 TB.
- Decision inputs: PR-through-EMM verdict; credit lag measured with a deliberately slowed partner (the plex sizing number).

#### Top risks
**Top risks**
1. **Shelf fence unverified.** If MD1200 EMMs do not pass PR to the drives, shelf HA rests on lease, watchdog and the two-arena superblock protocol, and the HBA-queue stale-write hole stays open.
2. **HDD GC cliff.** 0.3-0.5 GB/s at 80% is the real sustained-write figure; without the aged benchmark and honest capacity marketing the product over-promises.
3. **Hung disk versus the hold bound.** SCSI EH plus an mpt3sas host reset can outlast 10-20 s and stalls every member; if md cannot fail a drive inside the hold, one bad drive is an APD event on either node.
4. **Restart budget versus L4.** If ring-holder, MR import and the state page do not deliver 2 s/5 s, every daemon bug becomes an 8 s fence of a healthy node.
5. **Plex credit on non-rejecting transports.** A skipped or stalled `Released` silently truncates the plex; by-replay resync is unbuilt and the matched-geometry assumption is gone.

### Opus-Protocols

#### Concessions
**Takeover and fencing (Fabrics 2; Consensus 1, 2, 5).** ADD_DEV at takeover was wrong: a Standby path needs the LUN present on both nodes with identical VPD and capacity, so ublk devices, iblock backstores, LUNs and nvmet namespaces are pre-created on both nodes, the standby's gate is closed (gate-closed completions return `-EIO`; udev masks `ublkb*` from blkid/md/lvm) and takeover is the ALUA/ANA group flip only. DEL_DEV as a fence hands IOERR to LIO and CHECK CONDITION to guests (Broadcom KB 345174): it is teardown, never the fence. A healthy node that loses its lease stops exporting (gate, Standby/Inaccessible, LIFs down); a wedged node is fenced by silence (lease lapse, watchdog). The gate is per aggregate: a slot in the epoch page checked at submit and completion.

**Threads, memory, crash (Storage 1, 2, 5; Fabrics 4).** One thread per LUN was wrong: 6.12 binds a queue to one task, but one task may serve many queues, so ublk queues live on Storage's per-core reactors and `submit` is a same-thread call; the TCP mirror wait uses `IORING_REGISTER_NAPI` (`busy_poll_to`, `prefer_busy_poll`), not a userspace spin. Rings leave any slice with MemoryMax; per-SVM MemoryMax is derived from DRAM minus pinned memory. A dbladed crash is an all-LUN START/re-FETCH/END recovery that must fit the 2 s budget, and `req_id` = (device, queue, tag, generation).

**NAS semantics (Consensus 3, 4; PROTOCOLS-3, -6).** Lease 10 s punishes every 10 s stall: default 30/45; NFSv3 for ESXi datastores (no NLM, pause = block ladder); Proxmox/QEMU holds fcntl locks, so v4.1 there unless statd/sm-notify run per SVM. Unplanned takeover loses up to `dirty_expire` of acknowledged SMB writes: `dirty_expire_centisecs=200`, `dirty_writeback_centisecs=100`, `strict sync = yes`, printed as "SMB <= 2 s" in the share-class table. `posix locking = yes` stays on smb-only shares; only byte-range-locked handles lose durability. `--scope` is unshare+sethostname and `nfsd_svc()` rewrites scope and major_id from the caller's nodename, so nbladed sets the SVM utsns hostname itself, mounts nfsd and rpc_pipefs per netns and issues every nfsd control call from inside the SVM.

**Smaller corrections.** iSCSI digest costs ~0.1 core (Fabrics 6). Sub-second SG_IO timeouts were the wrong fix: per-device `timeout` 1-2 s, `fast_io_fail_tmo=1`, `dev_loss_tmo=5` and RSCN suffice, writes stay on the block layer, ring sizing is transport-blind except the quarantine term (Fabrics 1). qla2xxx must load `qlini_mode=dual` for any per-port role; nbladed is the sole configfs/sysfs/modprobe.d writer and `BladeKind::FcMirror` is a spec it applies (Fabrics 3). Online `-o discard` is cheap, XFS metadata needs its own cache class, thick reservation includes GC reserve and snapshot deadlists (Storage 3). Under plex-by-reference Standby paths are real; single-node targets are manual mode only, and "shelf first" is withdrawn as my recommendation because the front end is tier-agnostic (Storage 4). PR shipping can regress one operation on unplanned takeover: WSFC is documented, not promised (Consensus 6). versitygw POSIX versioning is experimental; object lock needs xattrs and an explicit `--object-lock-mode` (PROTOCOLS-7). The lab adds a CX-4 pair and blade_nvmet an RDMA port (Fabrics 5).

#### Holds firm
**Kernel floor 6.12** (PROTOCOLS-1, -2, -5 confirmed): trixie or bookworm-backports; the installer probes `/sys/module/ublk_drv` and refuses 6.1. trixie-backports is already on 7.2, so 7.x joins the test matrix.

**XFS-on-LUN through Phase 2.** Consensus wants the native inode layer for NAS HA, but NAS takeover time is protocol state: grace, SMB session loss and daemon restart cost 5-45 s, while a clean umount/mount is milliseconds and unplanned XFS log replay is seconds. No layer makes planned giveback 2-4 s while grace exists; the honest planned number is 5-8 s on plex, 10-15 s on a shelf (md assemble), plus grace unless early lift works.

**External takeover promise 10 s p99 / 15 s hard**, bounded by Linux `no_path_retry` and Windows LinkDownTime; Consensus' 6 s internal target is welcome. Silent death still costs ESXi ~35 s unless the survivor borrows the dead portal IP solely to RST stray segments (the RST proxy); that keeps "no floating IPs for block" because the borrowed IP never serves I/O.

**BUSY is not dead for SCSI.** Storage is right for nvmet (BLK_STS_RESOURCE becomes INTERNAL|DNR) and bare ublk, but ublk turns `-ENOMEM` into BLK_STS_RESOURCE via `errno_to_blk_status()`, and `iblock_complete_cmd()` maps that to SAM_STAT_BUSY on 6.x (verify on 6.12). If confirmed, Stalled -> BUSY is a real, initiator-retried path for iSCSI/FC; NVMe holds requests.

**Hung-but-alive is worse than dead**: the diagnosis stands; the remedy is silence, not DEL_DEV.

**Unchanged and confirmed**: share classes, versitygw, iSCSI first for Windows (PROTOCOLS-8), VAAI limits and no ODX (PROTOCOLS-4), no per-I/O RPC wire format in a two-node pair, FC-mirror-as-LUN needs tmpfs (hugetlbfs has no write(2)) and is not Phase 1.

#### Revised design changes
1. **Flip-only takeover.** Both nodes carry byte-identical target trees differing only in portal addresses, RTPI and cntlid ranges; `set_access` flips the aggregate's ALUA/ANA groups. LUN grow on 6.12 (no UPDATE_SIZE) is a planned relocation: recreate the standby device at the new size, take over, repeat.
2. **Fence ladder**: `stop_exporting(agg)` under 50 ms, then lease lapse, then watchdog; `fence()` and DEL_DEV leave the trait. Lease loss on a healthy owner means stop exporting and stay up (Consensus).
3. **Epoch page** replaces the node-wide CompletionGate; `req_id` is structural.
4. **NAS**: 30/45; share classes smb-only (durable handles, leases, `posix locking = yes`) and multiprotocol (kernel oplocks, no leases or durables, share modes never enforced against NFS); per-share durability `Expire2s` (default) or `SyncAlways` (fsync = XFS log force + FLUSH, mirror-RTT class; measure); statd per SVM when v3 locking is enabled.
5. **SVM**: utsns hostname set by nbladed, nfsd and rpc_pipefs mounted per netns, nfsdcld `-p/-s` on the SVM root, all nfsd control from inside the namespace.
6. **S3**: versitygw with `--object-lock-mode`, `--versioning-dir` on the same filesystem outside the bucket root, never `--nometa`; Veeam object-lock conformance is a release gate.
7. **Degraded durability**: adopt Storage's `unmirrored_flush = placed|cp`; blades always advertise a volatile cache; alarm mandatory.
8. **Withdrawn**: the second-NIC ControlLink; I only require the install guide to state the both-LAN-down-with-FC-alive outcome.

```rust
pub struct AggSlot { pub owner_epoch: AtomicU64, pub lease_deadline_ns: AtomicU64 } // epoch page, one slot per aggregate
pub struct EpochPage { pub slots: [AggSlot; MAX_AGG] }
impl EpochPage { pub fn check(&self, agg: AggregateId, epoch: u64, now_ns: u64) -> Result<(), BlockErr> { /* at submit and completion */ } }
pub struct ReqId { pub dev: u32, pub queue: u16, pub tag: u16, pub generation: u32 } // stable across daemon restarts: REISSUE dedupe key
pub struct IoCtx { pub lun: LunId, pub agg: AggregateId, pub owner_epoch: u64, pub req: ReqId }
pub enum BlockErr { StaleEpoch, NotOwner, Fenced, Stalled /* SAM BUSY on LIO if verified; else hold */, Media }
pub trait BlockService {            // called on the reactor thread that owns the ublk queue; no hop, no Send bound
    fn reserve_slot(&self, lun: LunId, len: u32) -> Result<RingSlot, BlockErr>;
    fn submit(&self, ctx: IoCtx, op: BlockOp<'_>) -> impl Future<Output = Result<(), BlockErr>>; // BlockOp unchanged
    fn epochs(&self) -> &EpochPage;
}
pub enum BladeKind { Iscsi, FcScsi, FcMirror, NvmeTcp, NvmeRdma, Nfs, Smb, S3 }
pub enum AccessState { ActiveOptimized, Transitioning, Standby, Unavailable }
pub enum ShareClass { SmbOnly, Multiprotocol }
pub enum WriteDurability { Expire2s, SyncAlways }
pub trait ProtocolBlade {           // nbladed: sole writer of configfs, sysfs (qlini_mode) and modprobe.d
    fn kind(&self) -> BladeKind;
    fn apply(&mut self, desired: &BladeSpec) -> Result<(), ApplyErr>;                      // idempotent; devices pre-created on both nodes
    fn set_access(&mut self, agg: AggregateId, st: AccessState) -> Result<(), ApplyErr>; // THE takeover primitive: group flip or LIF up/down
    fn stop_exporting(&mut self, agg: AggregateId) -> Result<(), ApplyErr>;                // < 50 ms; never deletes devices; hard fence is the watchdog
    fn exported_state(&self) -> Vec<StateBlob>;                                            // APTPL (one-op window), nfsdcld db, secrets.tdb
}
```

#### Final Phase 1 recommendation
**Platform.** Trixie 6.12 (bookworm-backports accepted, 7.x in the matrix); two nodes, 10/25GbE plus a CX-4 pair; QLE2562 pair for FC-SCSI clients only; HA tier per the lead's shelf/plex decision (the front end is identical, only takeover timing differs). Clients: ESXi 8, Proxmox, Windows 11/Server 2025, macOS, Linux, Veeam.

**Build order.**
1. ublk front door on the reactors: pre-created devices on both nodes, USER_RECOVERY|REISSUE, USER_COPY into ring slots, epoch page, structural `req_id`; null engine first.
2. blade_lio: single configfs/sysfs writer, pre-staged identical trees, group flip, `stop_exporting`, APTPL shipping with the documented window, RST-proxy test hook.
3. blade_nvmet: TCP and RDMA ports (Linux/Proxmox nvme-rdma clients), ANA group per aggregate, DH-HMAC-CHAP.
4. SVM namespaces: knfsd (30/45, v3 and v4.1), Samba share classes, `vfs_shadow_copy2`, `idmap_rid`.
5. versitygw per SVM.
6. Client recipes: multipath.conf, ESXi SATP_ALUA claim rules, Windows MPIO.

**Success criteria.**
- ublk null round trip p50 <= 10 us on-reactor; daemon kill and recovery of 16 LUN x 128 tags <= 2 s with zero initiator errors.
- iSCSI 4 KiB QD1 write with mirror p50 <= 150 us, p99 <= 400 us; NVMe/TCP p50 <= 120 us; NVMe/RDMA p50 <= 80 us; >= 1.0 GB/s sequential on <= 2 cores for target plus ublk plus engine front end.
- 100 forced takeovers each of planned, daemon kill, lease lapse and power cut: ALUA/ANA flip <= 50 ms; I/O resumes <= 10 s p99 on ESXi (RST proxy), dm-multipath and Windows MPIO; zero guest errors; PRs intact except the documented window.
- Configfs dumps of both nodes differ only in addresses and id ranges.
- NFSv3 datastore pause equals the block ladder; NFSv4.1 resume <= 45 s worst with early-lift behaviour on 6.12 documented; SMB reconnect <= 20 s with measured unplanned loss <= 2 s; Veeam immutable repository passes backup, restore and tamper tests, and a takeover mid-job completes.
- If iblock BUSY is confirmed: a 3 s induced stall yields BUSY retries, no errors, on all three initiator families.
- CPU and memory table validated on 8C/64 GB with all four protocols live.

#### Top risks
1. **NAS RTO is protocol state.** NFSv4 early grace lift on 6.12 and SMB session loss set the NAS pause at 20-45 s; no engine or inode layer fixes it, and the owner may expect block-class numbers.
2. **Silent death on ESXi.** The 10 s promise for power-cut and watchdog fences rests on the unproven RST proxy; without it ESXi pauses ~35 s on every unplanned takeover.
3. **6.12 ublk limits.** No FAIL_IO, QUIESCE or UPDATE_SIZE: all-LUN re-FETCH inside 2 s after a daemon crash is unmeasured, and LUN grow is a relocation.
4. **Shelf versus plex is undecided.** It determines whether Standby paths are real, the planned-giveback number and the lab purchase; the front end is the same either way.
5. **Durability promises users will not read.** SMB 2 s unplanned window, PR one-operation regression, versitygw's experimental versioning and iblock BUSY (if unconfirmed, stalls hold commands into 30 s initiator timeouts).

### Opus-Consensus

#### Concessions
- **Ring mailbox (Fabrics 1; Storage).** Withdrawn: it ties control liveness to `dbladed`, inherits FC timeouts and collides with sender-owned slots. `otap-ctl` opens its own fabric channel (own QP on an imported PD; own fd on reserved blocks of the partner's mirror LUN) as a second `ControlLink` at 100 ms; the trust boundary is the WWPN ACL or the direct cable.
- **The ladder and the RST proxy assumed a silent node (Fabrics 2; Protocols 2; CONSENSUS-7).** A wedged node keeps its IPs and FC logins, so for wedged nodes the watchdog bound is on the client-visible path. The proxy now runs only on positive evidence; block portal IPs are raised by `nbladed` under Raft state, never by ifupdown; the shipped ESXi recipe and multipath.conf become the baseline. Its real payoff is Windows, which sends no NOP-Out.
- **Stall-to-release at 10 s (Fabrics 4; Storage 4; Protocols 4).** Too late, and it ping-pongs on a shelf. Member timeouts are drive failures (SCT ERC 7 s, SCSI timeout 10 s, md marks faulty); only node-local stalls (ublk queues stuck while the backstore progresses, HBA or daemon wedge) report put-stuck at 2 s and propose self-takeover at 5 s.
- **Two-arena does not fence an md shelf (Storage 1).** md's superblock and bitmap sit at fixed addresses. PR preempt-and-abort on SAS members or a confirmed power fence stays mandatory; two-arena is defence in depth with per-node superblock slots.
- **Geometry-independent plex (Storage 2; Fabrics 5).** Dropped: it is a second allocator, and the 30 s of ring does not exist. I adopt identical-geometry by-reference plex for Phase 2; the shadow index re-scales to the 8 GiB ring (about 7 s, 0.1 GB).
- **PR survival versus pre-staged paths (Protocols 1; CONSENSUS-4).** The gate is `export_count`, `res_aptpl_metadata` is write-only, nothing in LIO replicates the file, and `initiator_sid` must be stripped. Per-LUN policy: `standby_paths` (default) or `pr_survival`.
- **NFS 30/45 s versus NAS 8 s (Protocols 3; CONSENSUS-3).** Grace >= lease is unenforced, early end needs every prior-epoch client to RECLAIM_COMPLETE, lockd's grace overrides it, and an empty nfsdcld DB skips grace. Now: lease 10 / grace 20, NFSv4.1-only exports by default, nfsdcld DB and pinned fsids on the SVM root, the controller writes `v4_end_grace` from its own tally, and the criterion splits.
- **The shelf ladder omitted storage steps (Storage 3)**, and L3 must gate CP submits and the superblock write. **BUSY does not exist** on LIO iblock, nvmet or ublk: hold, then Transitioning/ANA-change.
- **Smaller:** softdog's hrtimer migrates and `soft_panic=1` hangs forever without `kernel.panic=N` (CONSENSUS-5); old-epoch inbound teardown before slot reuse and (epoch, lap, cp_seq) filtering (Fabrics 3); `AckLevel` collapses to `placed_is_hw_ack`; LocalNvmeRing and spill-on-silence merge into one PLP-only Phase 3 `LocalLink`; XFS-on-LUN stays through Phase 2, exclusive mount being the same fence; versitygw replaces s3s; bookworm lacks `rpc.nfsd --scope`, so trixie is pinned (CONSENSUS-2).

#### Holds firm
- **Leases carry the safety proof; fences accelerate.** CONSENSUS-1 confirms the mechanics; the lease layer is OpenTAP's own, validated against `ensure_linearizable()` (one quorum heartbeat RTT, no log write, no witness fsync). No critique touches L1-L4. The watchdog stays off the *survivor's* critical path; it is on the *client-visible* path only for wedged nodes, hence two published bounds.
- **Non-electable witness** on pinned openraft 0.9.25, `Trigger::elect()` never exposed to it; a Pi on microSD or a 100 ms cloud VM stays legitimate because steady-state commits need A+B only.
- **Replay all CRC-valid records above the last CP.** Storage accepts it for takeover (every hole is unacked because flush acks are Placed-gated); Fabrics' per-put acks make the prefix rule lossy. The owner's hole-free ring may keep the prefix cut for normal CPs: same invariant.
- **Lease TTL 3 s.** A 2 s TTL turns any 2 s `otap-ctl` stall into a false takeover for 1 s of gain.
- **A control path over the mirror fabric is mandatory** for direct DAC plus dead switch; Fabrics' critique supplies the mechanism, so only mine was wrong.
- **GrantUnmirrored: grant, alarm, 2 s CPs, forced takeover prints the loss.** BUSY-forever makes one cable a pair-wide outage; the knob is Storage's `unmirrored_flush=cp`.
- **Plex as the homelab product default**: softdog plus private media is the only fence tier consumer boards without BMC or SAS can honestly meet. Sequencing changes, the default does not.
- **SVM = netns; block flips before NAS IPs move; SMB labelled disruptive** (CONSENSUS-2, -8). Samba 4.25 persistent handles with CTDB become an optional experimental SMB-only share class.

#### Revised design changes
**Media sequencing.** Phase 1 proves HA on a PR-fenced SAS shelf (Protocols is right: the only medium where takeover of flushed data exists before plex ships); plex, as Storage's by-reference manifest, is Phase 2 and becomes the default once it passes the same suite. A shelf without PR-capable members and without a confirmed power fence refuses HA.

**Orchestration.** Desired state is declarative in Raft (owner, epoch, per-LUN ALUA/ANA target, SVM placement); blades reconcile toward it (Protocols' reconciler). `otap-ctl` sequences only four committed barriers: `Fenced`, `DataReady`, `BlockServing`, `NasServing`. `EpochPage` and `CompletionGate` are one seqlock page written by `otap-ctl`, read at submission, CP submit, superblock write and completion; its flip is the single trigger for the old owner's gate → Standby → ublk-delete sequence.

**Ladder (silent A, LAN witness).** 1.0-1.2 s election; 2.0 s partner silent; 4.3 s `Takeover` committed, surviving TPGs Transitioning; 4.4 s PR preempt-and-abort and old-epoch inbound teardown (QP to ERR; FC ACL drop plus ABTS); 4.5-7 s assemble, superblock max-select, segment table, residual scan, epoch flip, takeover CP, AO/ANA Optimized; 7-9 s SVM netns, ublk plus XFS mount, nfsd grace with the replicated nfsdcld DB, smbd, S3. Block p50 5 s plex, 6-8 s shelf. The proxy claims an address only on power evidence or after three unanswered ARP probes (a live kernel answers); FC logins free only at A's reset. Wedged node: pet withheld at lease expiry or 2 s after a worker stall (Storage's restart pause), watchdog 5 s, reset about 3 s, re-login 1 s: 11-13 s, hard 15 s.

**Timers.** BUSY removed: Stalled ≤ 2 s hold; node-local stall → self-takeover proposal at 5 s; member stall → SCT ERC 7 s, SCSI 10 s, md faulty. The pet consumes link health and data-path check-ins. **Kernel profile:** `hardlockup_panic=1`, `panic=5`, `panic_on_oops=1`, softdog without `soft_reboot_cmd`, install-time self-fire of each driver. **PR:** OpenTAP replicates `$dbroot/pr/aptpl_<serial>` on every change (inotify → `SetPr`), identical `vpd_unit_serial`, `force_pr_aptpl=1` before mapping, `initiator_sid` stripped, dbroot on local flash.

```rust
trait ControlLink { async fn send(&self, to: NodeId, m: CtrlMsg) -> io::Result<()>;
  fn health(&self) -> PathHealth; }            // impls: TcpMtls, FabricSideband{Rdma|FcLun}
enum FenceEvidence { Lease, PrPreempted, PowerOffConfirmed, WatchdogDeadlinePassed(MonoNs), ArpSilent }
enum Barrier { Fenced(FenceEvidence), DataReady, BlockServing, NasServing }
enum ClusterOp { Takeover{agg, new_owner, new_epoch}, Barrier{agg, epoch, b: Barrier},
  Giveback{agg}, GrantUnmirrored{agg, epoch}, RevokeUnmirrored{agg}, PlaceSvm{svm, node},
  SetPr{lun, aptpl: String}, SetDesired{lun, alua: AluaState, ana: AnaState}, Config(ConfigDelta) }
#[repr(C, align(64))] struct AggSlot { agg: u64, epoch: u64, state: u32, serving: u32,
  lease_expiry_mono_ns: u64, gate_seq: u64, pad: [u8; 24] }   // = Protocols' CompletionGate
struct NfsCfg { v41_only: bool, lease_s: u8 /*10*/, grace_s: u8 /*20*/, scope: String }
```

#### Final Phase 1 recommendation
**Build (trixie 6.12, nfs-utils 2.8.3, Samba 4.22).** `otap-ctl` complete: pinned openraft 0.9.25 on redb, leader-granted leases, the gate page, barrier commits, fence executor with evidence (softdog, iTCO/ipmi_watchdog, `fence_ipmilan`/`fence_redfish`, one Shelly/Tasmota outlet, PR via `sg_persist`, ARP probing), watchdog petter consuming link health, `TcpMtls` and `FabricSideband` links. Testbed: two data nodes on a dual-attached MD1200-class SAS shelf with PR-capable drives (about $400 used), a witness run as both Proxmox VM and Pi, a stub `dbladed` as its own process (`otap-ctl` never holds `/dev/ublkcN`; hugetlbfs ring, TCP MirrorRing, replay-all), LIO iSCSI with ALUA and both PR policies, one SVM netns (nfsd 10/20, NFSv4.1-only, replicated nfsdcld, Samba), a versitygw canary bucket with state on the SVM volume.

**Prove (automated, measured).**
1. TLA+/Apalache: leases, epochs, GrantUnmirrored, replay-all, barriers; "no overlapping ACKs per aggregate in real time" and "every ACKed write is replayed"; 3 voters, 2 aggregates, 2 clients.
2. madsim or turmoil: 100,000 schedules over the partition matrix (switch dead, DAC dead, both, one-sided witness, SIGSTOP wedge, stale reboot, dual power loss), zero violations; loom on the gate page.
3. 100 unplanned takeovers on real gear (power pull, DAC pull, switch kill, SIGSTOP, sysrq-c): silent-node block resume p99 ≤ 9 s; wedged-node p99 ≤ 13 s; hard bound 15 s on every run; Windows+MPIO reconnects inside 15 s via the gated proxy; ESXi with the shipped recipe ≤ 10 s; Porcupine-port check shows zero ACKed-write loss and no stale reads.
4. NAS: reconnect plus I/O on reclaimed state ≤ 8 s; new-OPEN latency ≤ 20 s, early-end tally logged per run.
5. Planned takeover and giveback ≤ 3 s client-visible over 30 nightly rehearsals measured by the witness as synthetic client.
6. GrantUnmirrored within 2 s of mirror loss; forced takeover prints the correct loss figure.
7. Dual power loss converges with no operator input.
8. Witness at netem 100 ms ± 50 ms passes 3-6; the Pi logs < 100 fsyncs/day.
9. Fence quality: HA refused on a shelf without PR or a confirmed power fence; each watchdog driver self-fires at install; `soft_panic` without `kernel.panic` flagged.

#### Top risks
1. **Wedged-node recovery rests on the watchdog plus a platform reset.** SMI hangs, paused VMs and failed resets are unbounded, FC clients have no RST equivalent, and 11-13 s leaves 2 s under the hard bound. BMC or PDU evidence is the accelerator; two bounds are published.
2. **NFS grace** is defeated by one absent or v4.0 client and by lockd; nfsdcld replication is the load-bearing piece. If early end is unreliable, new opens wait 20 s after every failover.
3. **md shelves without PR-capable SAS members cannot be made safe** (fixed-address superblock and bitmap); the product must refuse HA on SATA-interposer shelves, which many homelabs own.
4. **Ownership fragmentation:** who holds `/dev/ublkcN`, one ring file type, one orchestration owner, one gate-page format. Unresolved, the N-Blade becomes three products.
5. **openraft 0.9 carries no API stability promise** and 0.10 restructures it; the lease layer is ours and must be model-checked, never assumed.

## Critics

### Completeness critic

#### Missing topics
**Nobody owns these; the phase is when the decision must exist.**

1. **Management plane, API, CLI, cluster bootstrap (Phase 1).** Consensus made desired state declarative in Raft and Protocols' reconciler consumes it, so the API *is* "write `ClusterState`", yet nobody specified cluster formation (how A, B and the witness join, `ca_roots` enrollment, cert rotation, witness replacement), a CLI vocabulary, or the installer, whose probes are scattered across Storage (RZAT, SCT ERC, SMR lists), Consensus (watchdog self-fire, fence quality) and Fabrics (firmware floors). Every "the UI must say so" in the record is an unassigned requirement; the web UI itself is Phase 2.
