2. **Observability (Phase 1).** The criteria are per-path percentiles, the ROLLBACK/GrantUnmirrored/fence-quality alarms are load-bearing, and Consensus' witness-as-synthetic-client writes results into Raft, but there is no metrics schema, no per-I/O trace through ublk, ring, mirror and CP, and no alert transport beyond "syslog CRIT and webhook".
3. **CI, lab plan, bill of materials (Phase 1).** Four harnesses (Fabrics' rxe/tcm_loop, Storage's dm-log-writes/QEMU/PDU, Consensus' TLA+/madsim/Porcupine port, Protocols' real ESXi/Windows/macOS/Veeam clients), no integrated matrix, no nested-KVM runner for kernel-module tests, no hardware-in-the-loop runner, and no purchase list keyed to the week each spike needs it (shelf, 9300-8e pair, CX-3/CX-4, QLE2562/2692, Pi, Shelly, PDU).
4. **Upgrades, rollback, version skew (rules Phase 1; procedure Phase 2).** Rolling upgrade of a 2+1 cluster is unmentioned, as are compatibility rules for the batch format (`ver` exists, semantics do not), superblock and map, the Raft schema (openraft 0.10 restructures, Consensus risk 5), kernel point releases altering ublk/LIO, Debian 14 (rtslib 3.0, 7.x kernels per FABRICS-3 and PROTOCOLS-2) and ksmbd CVE tracking (CONSENSUS-6). Rule now: every persistent and on-wire structure is versioned with an N/N+1 promise.
5. **Privilege model and secrets (Phase 1).** Every daemon is implicitly root (configfs, ublk-control, netns, mounts, SG_IO, watchdog, uverbs). Needed: per-daemon capabilities, systemd hardening, memlock/hugetlb reservations, and a decision on CHAP, DH-HMAC-CHAP keys and keytabs "from Raft secrets" (Protocols), which today means plaintext redb on the witness's microSD.
6. **Core and memory budget with all protocols live (Phase 1).** Five inputs (Protocols' table, Storage's MemoryMax objection, Fabrics' 8C/16T floor, Consensus' otap-ctl core, the owner's core-0 rule), no owner, no isolcpus/nohz_full/IRQ-affinity profile, no answer for 4-core boxes.
7. **Performance methodology (Phase 1).** fio profiles, QD matrix, aged-pool preconditioning (STORAGE-7), measurement points (Protocols), run-to-run variance, concurrent four-protocol load.
8. **md operations on a PR-fenced shelf (Phase 1).** Hot spares, rebuild rate against CP drain, SCT ERC enforcement at pool creation (Storage's open question), `checkarray` overrides, and drive replacement: a new member carries no PR registration, so keys must be registered before `mdadm --add` or the fence silently weakens.
9. **UPS integration (Phase 2, cheap).** A NUT-triggered final CP turns most homelab outages into zero-loss shutdowns; nobody mentioned it despite the volatile-cache model.
10. **Data reduction and encryption (format decision Phase 1; build Phase 3).** Never mentioned. Storage's 16 B extent and PBA-keyed cache assume physical length equals logical length; compression needs a physical-length field reserved now. Encryption at rest (dm-crypt under md versus per-volume keys) and plaintext rings in tmpfs need a stated posture.
11. **Thin provisioning across protocols (Phase 1 attributes).** Map holes and discard exist; VPD B2h/LBPRZ, threshold UNIT ATTENTION, nvmet thin reporting, S3 quotas and block-LUN behaviour at write-stop (hold or CHECK CONDITION) do not.
12. **Identity and ACLs (Phase 2; SVM-root layout Phase 1).** NFSv4-to-NT ACL mapping on XFS, nfsidmap, local-user replication, versitygw's single-uid ownership on shared volumes; Protocols fixed only `idmap_rid` and asked whether AD is assumed.
13. **S3 on rollback (Phase 2).** After nvfail, objects Veeam was told are immutable may vanish; in-flight multipart cleanup, a per-bucket durability class and a reconciliation report are undesigned, on top of PROTOCOLS-7's experimental versioning.
14. **Backups, SnapMirror-like replication, snapshot scheduling (Phase 3; primitives Phase 1).** Storage's `birth_cp` map walk for plex rejoin is a snapshot-diff shipper; keep `birth_cp` and stable snapshot ids in the v1 format. Schedules, retention, autodelete and snapshot space accounting are absent (Phase 2).
15. **Licensing (Phase 1).** Samba, nfs-utils and fence-agents are GPL (exec'd, fine); versitygw, openraft and s3s Apache; libublk, sideway and redb to confirm; MinIO and Garage AGPL (excluded); Marvell firmware non-free. Pick the project licence and a cargo-deny allowlist before the first crate lands.
16. **Documentation and runbooks (install guide and the three alarms Phase 1).** Forced-takeover loss, nvfail clear, degraded mirror, drive and witness replacement, cert rotation, an HCL (firmware floors, SMR, PLP).
17. **Smaller gaps.** otap-ringd is now the process that must not die and has no restart semantics; CRC verification on normal reads (Storage verifies only in scrub); NTP for `cp_wallclock`, Kerberos and SigV4; LUN resize is a relocation on 6.12 and volume move is absent; NAS LIF behaviour on link failure without takeover (Protocols' LACP question) was never answered; Storage's 8 MiB flash segment is not a full-stripe multiple of a 4-drive RAID5 at 64 KiB chunk (192 KiB stripe).

#### Unresolved conflicts
**Positions that crossed or still split after round 3.**

1. **Plex variant (crossed).** Storage now says by-replay is the general case and by-reference an optimization; Consensus now says identical-geometry by-reference only. For by-replay: plex is the SATA/consumer tier where mismatched arrays are the norm. For by-reference: one CP program, 5% manifest, physical resync, no second allocator, map and GC.
2. **Inbound ring files (crossed).** Storage conceded one tmpfs file per node with aggregate tags; Fabrics' revised trait is per (aggregate, direction). Per node: fewer MRs, LUNs and registrations, no static DRAM split. Per aggregate: quiesce and old-epoch teardown at takeover without touching other aggregates' slots.
3. **Control sideband (crossed).** Consensus withdrew the ring mailbox for an otap-ctl-owned QP or LUN-fd channel (Fabrics' own critique); Fabrics' revision keeps a mailbox region at the head of the ring file polled by otap-ringrecv. Owned channel: control liveness independent of dbladed and ringrecv. Mailbox: no extra QP or FC exchange budget.
4. **NFS defaults (crossed).** Protocols now 30/45 with NFSv3 for ESXi and Proxmox datastores; Consensus now 10/20 with NFSv4.1-only. For 30/45: a 10 s lease expires state in any 10 s stall and v3 datastores have no grace at all. For 10/20: grace is the NAS RTO and CONSENSUS-3 makes early end unreliable, so only a short grace bounds it.
5. **Hold hard bound.** Consensus: self-takeover proposal at 5 s for node-local stalls; Protocols: hard stop near 20 s, inside 30 s initiator timeouts; Storage defers. 5-10 s keeps the 10 s p99 and 15 s hard promise (dm-multipath fails at 15 s); 20 s avoids a takeover that inherits the same backlog during a legitimate throttle wait.
6. **BUSY.** Protocols holds that ublk `-ENOMEM` becomes BLK_STS_RESOURCE and SAM_STAT_BUSY through iblock (unverified on 6.12); Storage and Consensus deleted BUSY. True: iSCSI and FC get an initiator-retried signal. False: hold-then-Transitioning is the only path.
7. **Degraded durability.** Storage: LocalNvmeRing in Phase 1, `cp` only without PLP; Consensus: PLP-only LocalLink in Phase 3, `cp` with 2 s CPs; Protocols: `cp` collapses NAS and S3 to about 8 MB/s; Fabrics: sync-to-disk default without a UPS.
8. **Kernel cmdline and timer-table owner.** Fabrics accepted `panic=-1`; Consensus argued `panic=-1` in cross-examination yet shipped `panic=5` in its profile, which with `hardlockup_panic=1` is about 13 s against the published 8 s bound. Consensus asked who owns the single timer table; nobody answered.
9. **Kernel matrix.** Protocols puts 7.x in the test matrix; Storage says trixie-backports is not a product tier; Fabrics calls it an optional lane.
10. **Long-term default tier.** Consensus: plex is the homelab default because softdog plus private media is the only fence consumer boards can meet; Storage: the shelf is the only credit-free, 100%-capacity takeover. This is the owner's call, not a technical one.

#### Decisions for the lead architect
**Ordered by how much each constrains the rest; each with my recommendation.**

1. **Phase 1 HA tier and the lab purchase.** PR-fenced dual-EMM SAS shelf in Phase 1 (Storage and Consensus now agree; Protocols is tier-neutral), gated by a week-1 PR-through-EMM spike, with a confirmed Redfish power fence on the owner's iDRAC as fallback; plex in Phase 2, becoming the consumer default once it passes the same suite. It is the only medium where takeover of flushed data exists before plex ships, and the front end is identical either way.
2. **Plex variant.** By-reference, with "identical geometry" relaxed to a common segment size that is a full-stripe multiple on both arrays (lcm of the two full stripes) and aggregate size = the smaller array. This keeps one allocator, the 5% manifest and physical resync while admitting Storage's mismatched drive counts; `Credit` enters the ring contract now under either variant.
3. **Ring files and format ownership.** One tmpfs inbound file per node plus a hugetlbfs outbound arena; Fabrics owns the batch format and scanner, Storage owns slot lifetime (slot-state page, Placed-gated cut, `Credit`); freeze v1 with versioning semantics before the TCP leg. Fewer registrations and LUNs, a fallback flip never changes files, and one owner ends the three slot maps that Fabrics (risk 3) and Consensus (risk 4) both flag.
4. **Process topology and control sideband.** Ratify otap-ringd (files, PD/MR/receiver QP, otap-ringrecv, slot-state and request-age pages), dbladed (`/dev/ublkcN` on per-core reactors, index, CP), nbladed (sole configfs/sysfs/modprobe.d/netns writer, reconciler) and otap-ctl (Raft, leases, gate page, Consensus' four barriers, fences). The sideband is otap-ctl-owned (Consensus revised), not a mailbox region: in Phase 1 with a TCP mirror on a direct DAC it is simply a second TcpMtls path over the DAC IP; FabricSideband{Rdma} ships with the RDMA leg, FabricSideband{FcLun} with FC in Phase 2. Specify otap-ringd restart semantics now.
5. **Back-pressure contract and one timer table.** Owner: Consensus, with Fabrics' link timers, Protocols' initiator and NFS timers and Storage's restart budget as inputs. Throttle delay at most 2 s per request; Stalled 2 s then Transitioning; node-local stall proposes self-takeover at 5 s; hard bound 10 s, not 20, since legitimate throttle waits never exceed 2 s and 20 s breaks the 15 s hard promise; member stall is a drive failure (SCT ERC 7 s, SCSI 10 s, eh_deadline 5 s); daemon restart 2 s target, 5 s hard; lease 3 s; `panic=-1`, softdog `soft_margin=5` without soft_panic, nowayout=0. BUSY stays out until a one-day 6.12 spike proves SAM_STAT_BUSY; my reading of `iblock_complete_cmd` is that only BLK_STS_RESV_CONFLICT is special-cased and every other bio error is CHECK CONDITION.
6. **NAS defaults by SVM class.** Datastore SVMs NFSv3-only (ESXi and Proxmox; pause equals the block ladder, no grace); file SVMs NFSv4.1-only with lease 10, grace 20, replicated nfsdcld and a controller-written `v4_end_grace`; SMB `Expire2s` with the disruptive label. This reconciles Protocols' protocol split with Consensus' grace bound: lockd starts only where v3 is enabled, so separate SVM classes keep its 50 s grace away from v4 opens. Verify whether 6.12's courteous-server behaviour removes the 10 s lease objection.
7. **Degraded durability.** One knob, `unmirrored_flush = placed | local_nvme | cp`; `local_nvme` automatic when a PLP namespace exists, otherwise `placed` with the mandatory alarm and the forced-takeover loss printout; `cp` opt-in only, since Protocols showed it collapses NAS and S3; LocalNvmeRing in Phase 2 with the trait slot reserved in Phase 1. The owner must ratify the `placed` default: it is the "one more fault loses the window" stance.
8. **Platform floor.** Trixie 6.12.x generic amd64, bookworm-backports tolerated, stock bookworm refused by the installer, 7.x a CI lane with no product claims; 64 GB DRAM floor for HA tiers, 32 GB only for manual or single-node (Storage's open question); 8C/16T stated as a requirement.
9. **FC.** Ships in Phase 2; S1 (symmetric dual mode, asymmetric two-link fallback) and S2 in Phase 1 week 1; module `qlini_mode=dual`, mirror port dual, client ports disabled; the 25xx `ql2xfwloadbin` policy after S1.
10. **Owner-only items before the first crate lands.** Project licence and dependency allowlist; secrets-at-rest posture for Raft on the witness; the long-term default tier (shelf versus plex); the 0.8x usable-capacity marketing (Storage).

#### Phase 1 risks
1. **Shelf fence unverified** (Storage risk 1, Consensus risk 3). Week 1: `sg_persist` register and preempt-and-abort through both EMMs, then SIGSTOP the owner with a dirty 1.5 GiB stripe cache, preempt, resume, and prove nothing lands. Fallback: confirmed Redfish fence on iDRAC. If neither works, the Phase 1 HA proof moves to plex and the roadmap flips.
2. **Restart budget versus L4** (Storage risk 4, Protocols risk 3). Build the slot-state page and `ibv_import_mr` first; measure 16 LUN x 128 tag re-FETCH; gate on p99 2 s, worst 5 s, zero watchdog fires over 50 kills. If missed, persist the index or raise Stalled before anything is built on top.
3. **Three slot maps and format drift** (Fabrics risk 3, Consensus risk 4). Assign the owner and freeze v1 with property tests before the TCP leg exists.
4. **NAS RTO** (Protocols risk 1, Consensus risk 2). Week 2: measure early grace end on 6.12 with replicated nfsdcld, one absent client and one v4.0 client; the result decides whether 20 s is the promise or the floor.
5. **HDD drain cliff** (Storage risk 2, STORAGE-7). Run the aged-pool benchmark at 70% and 80% before ring sizing and the throttle constants are frozen.
6. **Silent death on ESXi** (Protocols risk 2). Ship the ESXi recipe as the baseline, build the RST-proxy test hook, and measure both published bounds over 100 power pulls.
7. **Hung disk versus the hold bound** (Storage risk 3). Hung-drive injection on the shelf; verify md fails the member inside the bound with `eh_deadline=5` and the mpt3sas host reset timed; otherwise one bad drive is an APD event on either node.
8. **iblock BUSY** (Protocols). One-day spike before the back-pressure contract is coded.
9. **CPU budget** (Fabrics risk 5). Run Protocols' four-protocol load test on an 8C/64 GB box early, not as the final criterion.
10. **openraft 0.9** (Consensus risk 5). Pin 0.9.25, keep the storage traits thin, and model-check the lease layer, which is OpenTAP's own code.

### ONTAP veteran

#### Where the plan diverges from ONTAP
*Public ONTAP documentation only; "(inference)" marks my reading.*

**Layering.** ONTAP: everything is a WAFL file. A LUN is a space-reserved file in a FlexVol; a FlexVol is a container file in the aggregate with its own virtual block numbers (VVBN to PVBN); a snapshot is a root-inode copy; a qtree is a tagged subtree carrying quota, security style and oplock policy. Plan: the LUN is native and NAS is XFS inside a LUN. Verdict: legitimate, with costs to state: two block layers; NAS snapshots need fsfreeze; qtree becomes directory plus XFS project quota; no vol move without a second address level, so moving a volume between aggregates is replication plus cutover, not Phase 1.

**Multiprotocol.** ONTAP: security styles per volume and qtree (unix, ntfs, mixed, where mixed flips a file's effective style on each chmod or ACL set), a name-mapping engine (uid to SID; "user mapping failed" was a top ticket class for a decade), NT ACLs, DOS attributes and streams in the inode, native case-insensitive lookups, and one lock manager that enforces SMB share modes and byte-range locks against NFS and breaks oplocks and leases on NFS access. Plan: two share classes. Verdict: legitimate; it is "security style plus oplocks off per qtree" at a fraction of the cost. The mistake is the missing identity decision: sec=sys NFS plus idmap_rid SMB does not give one person one uid on both sides. Pick one source (AD through SSSD with the same RID algorithm, or SMB-only shares) before any multiprotocol share exists.

**NAS state across takeover.** ONTAP: the N-blade is not stateless; NFSv4 client and session state and SMB sessions live there. Public NDO matrix: NFSv3 non-disruptive; NFSv4.x re-establishes under grace (lease 30 s, grace 45 s, delegations off by default); SMB non-disruptive only on Continuously Available shares (persistent handles plus Witness), supported only for Hyper-V and SQL Server. Plan (revised): the same matrix. Verdict: correct; the residual divergence is 10/20 versus 30/45, and ONTAP chose 30/45 because short leases expire healthy clients during ordinary stalls (inference: laptops and vMotion will hit a 10 s lease before a failover does).

**NAS LIFs versus SAN paths.** ONTAP: NAS LIFs move (VifMgr, failover groups, auto-revert, gratuitous ARP) to any node in the group and the N-blade reaches the volume over the cluster network (SpinNP), so LIF placement and volume placement are independent; SAN LIFs never move and ALUA state derives from volume location. Plan: the SVM follows its aggregate. Verdict: correct for two nodes; NAS failover is then a takeover (daemons restart, XFS mounts, grace), never a LIF move, and a NIC failure needs per-node bonding, not a LIF move.

**HA interconnect.** ONTAP: NVLOG mirror, HA heartbeat, takeover and giveback coordination; no client data, no configuration. The second arbitration channel is the mailbox disks on the shared shelves; hardware-assisted takeover is the SP/BMC telling the partner the node reset; the data path never waits on a quorum service. Plan: Raft-granted leases on the data path and a witness vote. Verdict: legitimate for the plex tier (the witness is Select's mediator); stricter than ONTAP on the shelf tier, where a free third channel is ignored.

**ONTAP Select.** Public: Select requires a RAID controller with battery- or flash-backed write-back cache and emulates NVRAM as a disk-backed log; HA is RAID SyncMirror with the partner's local disks as the second plex, so NVLOG and CP data both cross the internal network, 50 percent usable; the 2-node mediator is, to my recollection of TR-4517, an iSCSI mailbox target on the Deploy VM, and losing it disables automatic takeover, not data service. Plan: mirrored volatile DRAM, plex-by-reference. Verdict: the volatile window is weaker than Select by the owner's explicit choice, honest as long as nvfail exists; plex-by-reference is not Select (Select ships segments) and couples the owner's slot lifetime to the partner's CP: build the credit properly or copy the dumb 2x-link version.

**RDB.** ONTAP: VLDB (volume location), VifMgr (LIF placement), mgwd (cluster and SVM configuration), bcomd (SMB configuration); configuration only, one ring each, majority with epsilon; out of quorum freezes configuration changes and LIF failover while data keeps being served. Plan: one Raft state machine. Verdict: the right collapse; Raft loss stopping data through leases is the divergence, where ONTAP keeps serving.

**Takeover unit and promises.** ONTAP: unplanned takeover takes the whole node; aggregate relocation moves one aggregate, planned. ONTAP never promised clients 10 s; it shipped Host Utilities and guest-timeout guidance (190 s for ESXi guests) instead. Plan: per-aggregate epoch, 10 s p99. Verdict: per-aggregate is ARL and right; the client timeout contract is the part NetApp learned to own.

#### Hard lessons
- **Identity before shares.** Most multiprotocol outages I worked were mapping or security-style surprises, not lock bugs. Decide uid/SID mapping, root squash and the default user once; refuse to create a multiprotocol share until the SVM has an identity source.
- **Windows semantics on POSIX are the long tail.** Case-insensitive lookups (Samba scans directories unless `case sensitive = yes`), DOS attributes and streams in xattrs, ACL inheritance, Mac forks and Time Machine (vfs_fruit). WAFL absorbed these in the inode; Samba emulates them per VFS module. Budget permanent support.
- **Snapshots eat the pool.** The first homelab outage will be snapshots pinning space until writes stop and XFS shuts down. ONTAP needed snapshot reserve, autodelete, autogrow and LUN-offline-on-no-space. Ship autodelete with snapshots, or ship snapshots off.
- **Snapshot directories leak.** `.snapshot` visible to rsync, du and a POSIX S3 gateway multiplies backups and listings; keep snapshot mounts outside share and bucket roots, hidden by default.
- **Delegations off.** ONTAP ships NFSv4 delegations disabled: recall storms and reclaim on failover cost more than they save.
- **Giveback is the dangerous half.** More incidents came from giveback than takeover: vetoes, terminated SMB sessions, partial givebacks. Auto-giveback waits 600 s; the returning node must never assemble shelf arrays at boot (`AUTO -all` in mdadm.conf, no udev incremental assembly in the initramfs).
- **Randomly written, sequentially read.** Log placement scatters VM disks; a later backup reads one contiguous LBA megabyte from fifty HDD locations. WAFL added read reallocation and reallocate scans; GC should re-sequence survivors by (vol, lba). A read cache does not fix backups.
- **Hung disks and dirty SAS paths.** One drive past its timeout stalls every full-stripe write, and a used shelf's cables and EMMs produce CRC errors that look like hung drives: SCT ERC, short SCSI timeouts, PHY error counters (`/sys/class/sas_phy/*/invalid_dword_count`), early path failure.
- **Lost NVLOG is a data event.** nvfail (adopted) plus ONTAP's nvfail-rename idea: tell the operator which volumes had writes in the lost window; a per-volume dirty-since-last-CP bit in each superblock gets most of it.
- **Resync needs a common base.** SnapMirror resync works only from a snapshot both sides hold; without a pinned common CP, divergence after dual power loss means a full copy. Pin it on both plexes until resync completes.
- **The NVLOG device is the product.** Select's commodity trick is a disk-backed log behind protected cache; a used Optane P1600X is that device at homelab prices, and the volatile window then exists only on nodes without one.
- **Round-1 engine lessons still stand:** back-to-back CPs are the steady state; log size follows CP duration; the CP never blocks on a read (pre-read patch targets at ingest); the 80 to 90 percent cliff; pet on control liveness and I/O progress.
- **Clients detect slower than you fail over.** Ship host timeouts (Windows TimeOutValue, 190 s guest disks on ESXi, multipath.conf) as Host Utilities did, or the 10 s ladder is invisible.

#### Copy this
- **nvfail** (adopted), with the affected-volume list.
- **Mailbox blocks on the shared shelf.** ONTAP arbitrates takeover through mailbox disks as well as the interconnect. Reserve 1 MiB on two members; each node writes a 4 KiB heartbeat/epoch block with O_DIRECT every 500 ms and reads the partner's. It is a third ControlLink needing no Ethernet and no witness, and carries the stopped-exporting flag on the medium that matters.
- **Giveback vetoes and the 600 s auto-giveback delay:** a giveback preflight (CP in flight, GC urgent, S3 job running, open SMB handles above N) with an explicit override.
- **Host Utilities:** shipped multipath.conf, ESXi claim rules and recipe, Windows MPIO and TimeOutValue, guest disk timeouts, and one `otap-host-check` script that audits a client.
- **Qtrees:** XFS project quotas (`prjquota`, `xfs_quota -x -c 'project -s'`) per directory, Samba `dfree command` reporting the project quota, per-directory share and snapshot policy. It is what homelab users mean by a share.
- **Snapshot reserve, autodelete and autogrow** as one per-volume policy (age, count, space pressure), on by default.
- **SnapMirror async from birth_cp.** Extents with `birth > base` are the snapshot diff; stream them over TLS to a second OpenTAP box or as objects to S3 (SnapMirror Cloud's shape). For a homelab this beats HA for data survival, and the map already provides it.
- **Select's disk-backed NVLOG:** `LocalNvmeRing` on a PLP device, Optane preferred, as the default when present rather than a Phase 3 option.
- **The NDO matrix.** Publish per-protocol tables, planned and unplanned, per client OS: what continues, what reconnects, what errors.
- **Already in the plan, keep:** hardware-assisted takeover as fence evidence, SVM as the identity unit (netns, computer account, keytab, scope), aggregate relocation per aggregate, nightly rehearsal measured by a synthetic client.

#### Do not copy
- **A WAFL-class inode layer, unified lock manager and name-mapping engine.** Decades of work; XFS, Samba and knfsd give a homelab 90 percent.
- **N-blade to D-blade RPC and indirect access over a cluster network.** Needs a third node to pay off; the trait call is right.
- **Multiple RDB rings** with independent quorum: one state machine.
- **CA shares, persistent handles, Witness.** NetApp scoped them to two workloads after years; experimental at most.
- **MetroCluster, SyncMirror across sites, SnapMirror Synchronous.** Not for two boxes in one rack.
- **FlexVol virtual addressing and vol move.** A second map for movable volumes; do replication plus cutover later.
- **Dedupe, compression, compaction.** Not before block ships.
- **pNFS, referrals, FlexGroups.** Multi-node features.
- **Fractional reserve and the option sprawl.** Thin block LUNs with alerts, thick XFS volumes, fewer than twenty tunables.
- **The 10 s CP timer as a design center.** The ring/2 rule already supersedes it.

#### Phase 1 advice
- **Order.** (1) Engine plus checker, no protocol: Placed-gated cut, replay-all, nvfail, dual-cut harness, HDD drain curve. (2) iSCSI with ALUA, pre-staged paths and host recipes: the ESXi, Windows and Proxmox floor and the only protocol whose takeover you fully control. (3) NFSv3 datastores: stateless, pause equals the block ladder, nothing to reclaim. (4) S3 through versitygw as a black box: Veeam and restic targets make a homelab safer than any HA tier, at zero engine cost. (5) SMB-only shares, labelled disruptive, with Previous Versions and Time Machine. (6) NFSv4.1 home directories at 30/45 with nfsdcld replication, after early grace end is measured. (7) NVMe/TCP for Linux, Proxmox and ESXi. (8) FC-SCSI client targets on tcm_qla2xxx only if the owner's lab needs them.
- **Phase 2:** async snapshot replication (I would pull it ahead of plex), plex, FC mirror, multiprotocol shares.
- **Do not attempt in Phase 1:** multiprotocol shares, CA shares, a native inode layer, vol move, dedupe, 4Kn LUNs to ESXi, a 10 s NFS lease, LIF-only migration, NFSv4 delegations.
- **Decide now:** the identity source; shelf for Phase 1 with the PR-through-EMM spike in week one (Consensus's revised sequencing is right: it is the only tier where takeover of flushed data exists); Optane `LocalNvmeRing` as the default durability device; mailbox blocks on the shelf as the third control channel; no md auto-assembly in the initramfs; snapshot autodelete.
- **Contracts before code:** the per-protocol NDO matrix, one timer table with one owner, and the client timeout recipe, written before the first takeover test so the test has something to fail against.
- **Rehearse:** nightly planned takeover and giveback with the witness as client from the first week block works; giveback is the riskier half and never runs automatically in business hours.

### Homelab operator

#### Hardware reality
**The quiet re-scoping.** Four specialists turned "generic homelab x86" into "used dual-socket Dell with 64 GB, 8C/16T, a BMC, a ConnectX and a SAS shelf". That is my R730xd, but say it on page one: the N100/32 GB/2.5GbE box most TrueNAS and Unraid users own cannot host 16-32 GiB of pinned rings, isolated cores and hugepages. Positioning, not a flaw.

**FC.** The $15 QLE2562 is HP/Dell/IBM-branded with vendor firmware bundles; 16G QLE2672/2692 run flash firmware with no Debian blob, and Marvell's flash path is not a given for OEM cards; per FABRICS-6 an 8G card is silently downgraded to Debian's 8.07.00 blob. Symmetric dual-mode N2N is unproven, split-role eats both ports, and nobody at home owns a switch. FC is a client fabric for old ESXi hosts, never the mirror; Fabrics' "FC ships in Phase 2" should read never. The mirror is a $15 DAC.

**RDMA.** The $20 ConnectX-3 everyone owns is the non-Pro card: RoCEv1 only. Fabrics' "one code path for RoCEv2 and IB" needs CX-3 Pro or CX-4; a plain CX-3 is RoCEv1 on a direct DAC (L2 only) or IB mode with opensm on both nodes (if opensm failover is packaged; an IS5022-class used switch has no SM).

**Flash.** Storage's "4 to 8 NVMe md RAID5" is a new-server assumption. Used R730xd/R740xd ship SAS backplanes with zero or four NVMe bays; consumer boards have 1-3 M.2. Real flash pools are a 2x NVMe mirror, SATA SSDs on the HBA, or used SAS SSDs (PM1633/1643) in the shelf. The NVMe is a 970 EVO or SN850 without PLP (FUA 0.4-0.8 ms), so the LocalNvmeRing degraded-mode device does not exist in most labs; degraded mode falls to `cp`, which Protocols showed makes NAS and S3 crawl. The one cheap PLP device is used Optane (P1600X $40-60, 900P about $100), the ZFS SLOG everyone already has.

**Shelf.** MD1200 plus two 9207-8e (6G matches the shelf, $30 each) is real and I would buy it. But used SAS drives from EMC/NetApp arrive 520/528-byte formatted (`sg_format`, hours per drive); PR-through-EMM is unverified; a replaced drive needs the owner's PR key before it rejoins or the fence has a hole; 150-200 W and audible.

**Boot device.** Nobody mentioned it. Unraid culture boots from USB; this design puts the redb Raft log, the APTPL file written synchronously inside PR OUT, nfsdcld's sqlite and journald on the boot disk. Refuse USB; require a SATA or BOSS SSD.

**2.5GbE** boards lack a spare port and core for the mirror: not a target. **Power:** one consumer UPS and one breaker for everything, so "simultaneous site loss" is the common case, not the edge case.

#### Ops burden
- **Install: acceptable only as an ISO** that lays down the modprobe.d, udev, sysctl, hugepage, isolcpus and cmdline drop-ins, rebuilds the initramfs and disables Debian's monthly mdadm checkarray. The revised positions ship two cmdlines: Fabrics `panic=-1`, Consensus `panic=5`, after Consensus itself showed `panic=5` stacks to 13 s. Consensus asked "who owns the single timer table" and nobody answered. Until someone does: deal-breaker.
- **Upgrades: annoying, with a dangerous tail.** Each trixie-security kernel (one or two a month) is a rolling takeover and giveback: 5-15 s of block pause plus 20-45 s of NFS grace, twice. Needs one command with pre-flight (modules, ublk flags, a qla2xxx target smoke test), apt pin and hold, test before unpin. tcm_qla2xxx broke on me across a kernel update once; ublk flags move per kernel; libublk yanks releases.
- **Disk replacement: annoying.** md rebuilds all 16 TB (a day or more under CP load) where ZFS resilvers live data; the UI must wrap mdadm; checkarray will flag torn never-committed stripes (Storage's own hole); a new member needs PR registration.
- **Monitoring: acceptable** if Prometheus, smartd, `mdadm --monitor` and webhooks (ntfy, Pushover, email) ship in Phase 1. nvfail by default is fine if the alert carries the one-line clear command; offer auto-clear at first boot for one-UPS labs.
- **UI and CLI: deal-breaker.** Four specialists on rings and Raft, none on a UI, installer or docs. TrueNAS and Unraid *are* their UIs. An ONTAP-shaped CLI would pull refugees but is 1-2 engineer-years nobody budgeted.
- **Backups:** no config export (ONTAP's `system configuration backup`); data replication is below.
- **Witness: acceptable** (under 100 fsyncs a day, 100 ms RTT, a $5 VPS). Two rules nobody wrote: its disk must never live on OpenTAP, and it will live on the Proxmox host I reboot for updates, so witness-down plus a DAC flap is 1 of 3 and a pair-wide hold. openraft 0.9 with no API promise means three version-locked binaries.
- **3 a.m. flapping DAC: acceptable only with hysteresis.** As designed, every flap is a 2 s Stall, Transitioning on the owner TPG, GrantUnmirrored, then revoke after 30 s stable: ESXi path flaps, VM pauses every 30 s, an alert storm. ONTAP stays degraded and waits for a human. I want: after N grants in 10 minutes stay unmirrored; no auto-revoke under 10 minutes stable; never stall I/O to re-mirror; rate-limited alerts that say what to do. The lease plus witness cures the split brain DRBD gave me; an eager re-mirror undoes it.

#### Over-engineering
- **Fabric-agnostic mirror.** TCP in Phase 1, RDMA in Phase 2 (rxe in CI), FC mirror never: it carries the worst numbers (80-200 us, 10 s unknown fate, EH coupling), the only unproven spike and a second control channel. Consensus holds "a control path over the mirror fabric is mandatory"; the DAC has an IP (IPoIB on IB) and mTLS over it suffices. Cut `FabricSideband`.
- **Protocol count.** iSCSI, NFS, SMB first; NVMe/TCP next; S3 is cheap via versitygw; FC client later; the NVMe/RDMA port Protocols added, cut. Their own lesson: every protocol brings state that must outlive the front end.
- **Per-SVM netns with AD-joined machine accounts, krb5p and per-netns nftables.** One default SVM; the netns is only the IP-migration mechanism. Multi-tenancy is Phase 3.
- **The HA tier matrix.** Four tiers plus spill-on-silence plus LocalLink, still open after three rounds. One tier in Phase 1: the shelf, the thing I would buy. Plex-by-reference for flash in Phase 2; drop plex-by-replay and spill-on-silence. Consensus holds "plex as the homelab product default": at 50% of 12x16 TB, nobody at home accepts that for HDD.
- **PR survival and WSFC:** Phase 3; document "reservations do not survive takeover" like every entry array.
- **Perf bars.** 300 K mirrored IOPS, 1 M records/s and 25GbE ring arithmetic are datacenter targets. Homelab bar: 10GbE line rate, 50 K IOPS, p99 under 1 ms. Ship against that.
- **Keep:** TLA+ and madsim on the lease core (cheap next to a split brain), the RST proxy, the 512e/4Kn classes, XFS-on-LUN, versitygw.
- **Concrete shape:** v0.1 single node (md, D-Blade, ublk, iSCSI/NFS/SMB, snapshots, Prometheus, a UI); v0.2 the pair over TCP on a shelf; v0.3 NVMe/TCP, S3, replication.

#### Missing for homelab
- **Migration.** No import from ZFS, mdadm or Unraid, and nobody has 12 spare drives. Needs a pull-from-NFS/SMB/iSCSI/`zfs send` wizard.
- **Pool growth and mismatched drives.** Nobody mentions adding a drive. `mdadm --grow` reshapes for days and changes the stripe width under a segment-equals-32-stripes layout. OpenZFS 2.3 has RAIDZ expansion; Unraid mixes sizes. Homelabbers grow one drive at a time. Top blocker after HA.
- **Compression and encryption at rest.** Both absent. ZFS lz4 is a free 1.3-2x on VM images and CP-time zstd on full stripes is a natural fit; stolen-box risk is real at home.
- **Bit rot.** Phases 1-2 detect but cannot repair on a single-plex md pool until the Phase 3 P/Q repair; ZFS heals today.
- **Scheduled snapshots and replication.** Retention schedules and SnapMirror-style replication to a second box or S3 are why people run ZFS. Storage's plex-rejoin "logical diff by `birth_cp`" *is* the incremental send; build it as replication first, plex second.
- **NUT/UPS.** On battery: force a CP, stop exporting, clean shutdown; on power return: auto-boot, rejoin, giveback. Standard on TrueNAS, absent here.
- **Single-node mode.** Must be first-class and quiet (no Raft, no watchdog starvation, no GrantUnmirrored). With volatile DRAM it is RPO 10-15 s, worse than ZFS; Consensus parks LocalNvmeRing in "Phase 3, PLP only"; pull it into Phase 1 for single node on used Optane.
- **Hyperconverged.** Pinned cores, hugepages and mlock make the node an appliance; say so, then bless "OpenTAP as a Proxmox VM with HBA and NIC passthrough" (how TrueNAS runs at home) with i6300esb and the paused-VM lease rule.
- **Time Machine** (vfs_fruit, avahi, `fruit:time machine max size`) as a share type; **Kubernetes CSI** needs a management API for LUN, snapshot and clone; **SMB ACLs** on XFS (POSIX only) are a step down from ZFS NFSv4 ACLs.
- **Noise and power.** Pair plus shelf plus witness is 500-700 W, $50-170 a month by tariff: the strongest argument for single node plus replication at home.
- **Docs.** An HCL (firmware floors, the NO_REBOOT self-test, the PR-through-EMM verdict), the failover contract per protocol, a "what you lose and when" page, 3 a.m. runbooks. No docs persona exists.

#### Would I run it
**Against the field.** TrueNAS SCALE community has no HA; Proxmox plus ZFS replication is minutes of RPO and a VM restart; Ceph wants three nodes, 10GbE and RAM per OSD (I tore mine down); DRBD split-brained on me; Unraid has no HA and no ALUA. A witness-arbitrated two-node active/standby pair serving iSCSI, NFS and SMB from a $300 used shelf with a 10 s block failover and zero split brain: nothing in the homelab world does that. It is the only reason to run OpenTAP, and a good one.

**What stops me today.** Single-node OpenTAP is worse than ZFS (volatile cache, no compression, detect-only bit rot, full-disk rebuilds, no growth, no migration), so I must run the pair just to break even on durability. No UI, docs or alerting exist. Stability rides on tcm_qla2xxx, ublk and io_uring across kernel updates. After three rounds the specialists swapped NFS timers (Protocols 10/20 to 30/45, Consensus 30/45 to 10/20), ship two kernel cmdlines, and the record format has an owner only "unless reassigned"; a product with four opinions ships four bugs. And I want 12-24 months of other people's 3 a.m. stories first. Ceph taught me that.

**What I would do.** Run it as the iSCSI/NFS backend for my Proxmox and ESXi cluster only, media and photos staying on ZFS, once it has the pair on a shelf, snapshot replication to my ZFS box or S3, a UI with alerts, and a year of soak.

**The single compelling feature.** Verified sub-15 s iSCSI and NFS failover for ESXi and Proxmox on $300 of used SAS shelf with a witness that can be a $5 VPS, proven by the Porcupine checker and shown on a dashboard as "last failover: 2.8 s, 03:00 today". Prove that in Phase 1 and I buy the shelf tomorrow.
